) implies that all those edges corresponding to
\{\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{j,(s)},1\leq s\leq k_{\tau}\}
will be rejected according to Algorithm
1
. Since the number of rejected edges is at least
k_{\tau}
, we have
\psi_{j,\alpha}=1
. Second, we show
\{\psi_{j,\alpha}=1\}\subset\{\alpha_{j}\leq\alpha\}
. If
\psi_{j,\alpha}=1\}
, we know the edges corresponding to
\{\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{j,(s)},1\leq s\leq k_{\tau}\}
will be rejected, which immediately imply
\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{j,(1)}|\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,E^{(1)}_{j})
. Regarding the edge corresponding to
\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{j,(2)}
, it must get rejected in the first two iterations of Algorithm
1
. In either cases, we always have
\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{j,(2)}|\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,E^{(2)}_{j})
due to
E^{(2)}_{j}\subset E^{(1)}_{j}
and the fact that
\widehat{c}(\alpha,E)\leq\widehat{c}(\alpha,E^{\prime})
when
E\subset E^{\prime}
. Finally, we establish (
A.1
).

The validity of the single node test in Algorithm
1
is mainly based on the following result which characterizes the accuracy of the approximate quantiles.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\mathcal{U}(M,s,r_{0})
denote the parameter space of precision matrices i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}(M,s,r_{0})

\displaystyle=\Big\{\bm{\Theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\,\big|\,\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Theta})\geq 1/r_{0},\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Theta})\leq r_{0},\max_{j\in[d]}\|\bm{\Theta}_{j}\|_{0}\leq s,\|\bm{\Theta}\|_{1}\leq M\Big\}.

Suppose
\bm{\Theta}\in\mathcal{U}(M,s,r_{0})
and
(\log(dn))^{7}/n+s^{2}(\log dn)^{4}/{n}=o(1)
, for any edge set
E\subseteq\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{V}
, we have

\lim_{(n,d)\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{\bm{\Theta}\in\mathcal{U}(M,s,r_{0})}\sup_{\alpha\in(0,1)}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{e\in E}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{e}|>\widehat{c}(\alpha,E)\right)-\alpha\right|=0.

(A.3)

where
\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}
defined in (
5.1
) is the standardized version of the one-step estimator and
\bm{\Theta}^{\star}
denotes the
standardized true precision matrix
(\bm{\Theta}_{jk}/\sqrt{\bm{\Theta}_{jj}\bm{\Theta}_{kk}})_{j,k\in[d]}
.

Lemma A.2
.

Under the same conditions as Lemma
A.1
, given some
1\leq j\leq d
, we have the following results.

(i)

Additionally, suppose for any
|\bm{\Theta}_{jk}|>0
, we also have
|\bm{\Theta}_{jk}|\geq c\sqrt{\log d/n}
for some constant
c>0
. Under the alternative hypothesis
H_{1j}:\|\bm{\Theta}_{j,-j}\|_{0}\geq k_{\tau}
, we then have for any
\alpha\in(0,1)
,

\lim_{(n,d)\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(\psi_{j,\alpha}=1)=1.

(ii)

Under the null hypothesis
H_{0j}:\|\bm{\Theta}_{j,-j}\|_{0}<k_{\tau}
, we have for any
u\in(0,1)
,

\lim_{(n,d)\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\left({\psi_{j,\alpha}=1}\right)\leq\alpha.

The proof of the above two lemmas are deferred to Appendix
D.1
.
The maximum statistic used in our testing procedure takes the form of

T_{E}=\max_{(j,k)\in E}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jk}|
.
In our key proof procedure, we deal with the case where
E=\{(j,k):\bm{\Theta}_{jk}=0\}
. Since some of the results hold for general
E
, we will work with the general notations. Specifically, through out Appendices
A.1
and
A.2
, we introduce the following notations: in order to approximate

T_{E}:=\max_{(j,k)\in E}\sqrt{n}\left|(\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jk}/\sqrt{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jj}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{kk}}-{\bm{\Theta}_{jk}}/{\sqrt{\bm{\Theta}_{jj}\bm{\Theta}_{jk}}})\right|

(A.4)

by the multiplier bootstrap process

T_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}:=\max_{(j,k)\in E}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n~\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k})\xi_{i}\bigg|,

(A.5)

we define two intermediate processes:

\displaystyle\breve{T}_{E}

\displaystyle:=\max_{(j,k)\in E}\bigg|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n~{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k})\bigg|,

(A.6)

\displaystyle\breve{T}_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle:=\max_{(j,k)\in E}\bigg|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n~\bm{\Theta}_{jj}\bm{\Theta}_{kk}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k})\xi_{i}\bigg|.

(A.7)

A.1
Proof of Theorem
5.2

Given some
j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
, denote
N_{0j}=\{(j,k):\bm{\Theta}_{jk}=0\}
.
By the first part of Lemma
A.2
,
we have
\forall j\in\mathcal{B}
,

{\mathbb{P}\left({\psi_{j,\alpha}=1}\right)>1-3/d^{2},}

(A.8)

when
\alpha=\Omega(1/d)
, where
\mathcal{B}:=\{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}^{c}:\forall k\in\text{supp}(\bm{\Theta}_{j}),|\bm{\Theta}_{jk}|>c\sqrt{{\log d}/{n}}\}
.
Note that we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{q|\mathcal{B}|}{d}\leq\widehat{\alpha}\leq 1\right)\geq\mathbb{P}\left({\frac{{q|\mathcal{B}|}/{d}\cdot d}{\sum_{j\in[d]}\psi_{j,{q|\mathcal{B}|}/{d}}}\leq q}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left({\frac{|\mathcal{B}|}{\sum_{j\in[d]}\psi_{j,{q|\mathcal{B}|}/{d}}}\leq 1}\right){\geq 1-3/d},

(A.9)

where the first inequality is by (
2.3
) and the last inequality is due to
q\frac{|\mathcal{B}|}{d}=\Omega(1/d)
, (
A.8
) and the the union bound. Rewrite the FDP (with
\widehat{\alpha}
) as

\displaystyle\mathrm{FDP}(\widehat{\alpha}):=~\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}{\psi_{j,\widehat{\alpha}}}}{\max\left\{1,\sum_{j\in[d]}\psi_{j,\widehat{\alpha}}\right\}}=~\frac{\widehat{\alpha}d}{\max\left\{1,\sum_{j\in[d]}\psi_{j,\widehat{\alpha}}\right\}}\cdot{\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\psi_{j,\widehat{\alpha}}}{d_{0}\widehat{\alpha}}}\cdot\frac{d_{0}}{d},

and notice that

\frac{\widehat{\alpha}d}{\max\left\{1,\sum_{j\in[d]}\psi_{j,\widehat{\alpha}}\right\}}\cdot\frac{d_{0}}{d}\leq\frac{qd_{0}}{d}\leq q.

Then it suffices to control the
\mathrm{FDP}(\widehat{\alpha})
by dealing with
{\left(\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\psi_{j,\widehat{\alpha}}\right)}/{d_{0}\widehat{\alpha}}
. By (
A.9
), the FDP control problem is now reduced to showing

\sup_{\alpha\in[\alpha_{L},1]}\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\psi_{j,\alpha}}{d_{0}\alpha}\leq 1+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

where
\alpha_{L}=q|\mathcal{B}|/d
,
By (
D.3
) in the proof of the second part of Lemma
A.2
,
\psi_{j,\alpha}=1
implies that
\max_{e\in N_{0j}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{e}|\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j})
, where
N_{0j}=\{(j,k):\bm{\Theta}_{jk}=0\}=\{(j,k):\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{jk}=0\}
. Therefore, we have

\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\psi_{j,\alpha}}{d_{0}\alpha}\leq\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathds{1}{(\max_{e\in N_{0j}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{e}|\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j}))}}{d_{0}\alpha}.

Hence it suffices to prove that

\sup_{\alpha\in[\alpha_{L},1]}\left|\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathds{1}{(\max_{e\in N_{0j}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{e}|\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j}))}}{d_{0}\alpha}-1\right|\rightarrow 0~~~\text{in probability}.

(A.10)

In order to prove (
A.10
), we construct a discrete grid of the interval
[\alpha_{L},1]
. The number of grid points is denoted by
\lambda_{d}
and will be decided later. First, we let
t_{1}:=\widehat{c}(1,N_{0j})=0
,
t_{\lambda_{d}}:=\widehat{c}(\alpha_{L},N_{0j})
. Here
\widehat{c}(\alpha_{L},N_{0j})=\inf\left\{t\in\mathbb{R}:\mathbb{P}_{\xi}\left(T^{\mathcal{B}}_{N_{0j}}\leq t\right)\geq 1-\alpha\right\}
is the quantile based on the Gaussian multiplier bootstrap process and depends on the data
\bm{X}
. Note that the involving random vectors in the Gaussian multiplier bootstrap process are Gaussian conditioning on the data
\bm{X}
and have bounded variances with probability growing to
1
. Since
\alpha_{L}=\Omega(1/d)
, then by the maximal inequalities for sub-Gaussian random variables (Lemma 5.2 in
[
82
]
), we have
t_{\lambda_{d}}=O(\sqrt{\log d})
with probability growing to
1
. Second, note there exists
h_{d}
such that
h_{d}t_{\lambda_{d}}=o(1)
and
t_{\lambda_{d}}/h_{d}=O(\log d)
. Based on such
h_{d}
, we construct equally spaced sequences
\{t_{m}\}_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}
over the range
[t_{1},t_{\lambda_{d}}]=[0,t_{\lambda_{d}}]
with
t_{m}-t_{m-1}=h_{d}
. Then by setting
\alpha_{m}
such that
t_{m}=\widehat{c}(\alpha_{m},N_{0j})
, we obtain a discrete grid
\{\alpha_{m}\}_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}
of the interval
[\alpha_{L},1]
. For such
\alpha_{m},1\leq m\leq\lambda_{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq m\leq\lambda_{d}}\left|\frac{\alpha_{m-1}}{\alpha_{m}}-1\right|

\displaystyle=\max_{1\leq m\leq\lambda_{d}}\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}\left({T^{\mathcal{B}}_{N_{0j}}>t_{m-1}}\right)}{\mathbb{P}\left({T^{\mathcal{B}}_{N_{0j}}>t_{m}}\right)}-1\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq m\leq\lambda_{d}}C^{\prime\prime}(t_{m}-t_{m-1})(t_{m}+1)\exp(C^{\prime}(t_{m}-t_{m-1})(t_{m}+1))=o(1)

(A.11)

with probability growing to 1, where the first equality holds by the definition of
\alpha_{m}
, the first inequality holds due to part
2
and
3
of Theorem 2.1 in
[
43
]
(by first choosing
r-\epsilon,r+\epsilon
in part
3
to be
t_{m-1},t_{m}
respectively then letting
r-\epsilon,r
in part
2
to be
t_{m-1},t_{m}
respectively). And the right hand side of the inequality is
o(1)
since
(t_{m}-t_{m-1})t_{m}\leq h_{d}t_{\lambda_{d}}=o(1)
with probability growing to 1.

Denote

I_{j}(\alpha)=\mathds{1}{(\max_{e\in N_{0j}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{e}|\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j}))}
.
Then given
\alpha_{m}\leq\alpha\leq\alpha_{m-1}
, for
m=1,\cdots,\lambda_{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}I_{j}(\alpha_{m})}{d_{0}\alpha_{m}}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{m}}{\alpha_{m-1}}\leq\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}I_{j}(\alpha)}{d_{0}\alpha}\leq\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}I_{j}(\alpha_{m-1})}{d_{0}\alpha_{m-1}}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{m-1}}{\alpha_{m}}.

(A.12)

Hence by (
A.11
) and (
A.12
), showing (
A.10
) is reduced to proving

\max_{1\leq m\leq\lambda_{d}}\left|\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}I_{j}(\alpha_{m})}{d_{0}\alpha_{m}}-1\right|\rightarrow 0,~~~\text{in probability}.

(A.13)

Then it suffices to show that, for any
\epsilon>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left({\max_{1\leq m\leq\lambda_{d}}\left|\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}I_{j}(\alpha_{m})}{d_{0}\alpha_{m}}-1\right|\geq\epsilon}\right)\rightarrow 0.

By the union bound argument and Chebyshev’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle~~~~~\mathbb{P}\left({\max_{1\leq m\leq\lambda_{d}}\left|\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}I_{j}(\alpha_{m})}{d_{0}\alpha_{m}}-1\right|\geq\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\leq~\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\mathbb{P}\left({\left|\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}I_{j}(\alpha_{m})}{d_{0}\alpha_{m}}-1\right|\geq\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\leq~\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}I_{j}(\alpha_{m})-d_{0}\alpha_{m}}\right]^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}

(A.14)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&=~\underbrace{\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathrm{Var}\left({I_{j}(\alpha_{m})-d_{0}\alpha_{m}}\right)}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}}_{\mathrm{III}_{1}}+\underbrace{\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\left(\mathbb{E}\left[{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}I_{j}(\alpha_{m})-d_{0}\alpha_{m}}\right]\right)^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}}_{\mathrm{III}_{2}}\\
&~~~~~+\underbrace{\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}}\mathrm{Cov}\left({I_{j_{1}}(\alpha_{m})},{I_{j_{2}}(\alpha_{m})}\right)}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}}_{\mathrm{III}_{3}}.\end{split}

(A.15)

By Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
A.4
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{1}+\mathrm{III}_{2}+\mathrm{III}_{3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C^{\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\epsilon^{2}h_{d}}\left(\frac{d}{d_{0}|\mathcal{B}|}+\eta^{2}(d,n)\right)+\frac{C^{\prime\prime\prime}d}{\epsilon^{2}|\mathcal{B}|d_{0}}\cdot\frac{t_{\lambda_{d}}}{h_{d}}\cdot\left(1+\eta(d,n)d_{0}+\frac{|S|\log d}{d_{0}p}\right)

(A.16)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}t_{\lambda_{d}}\eta^{2}(d,n)}{\epsilon^{2}h_{d}}+\frac{C_{2}}{\epsilon^{2}\rho d_{0}}\cdot\frac{t_{\lambda_{d}}}{h_{d}}\cdot\left(1+\eta(d,n)d_{0}+\frac{|S|\log d}{d_{0}p}\right),

where we substitute
\zeta_{1}={s(\log d)^{2}}/\sqrt{n}
,
\zeta_{2}=1/d^{2}
and
\alpha_{L}=q|\mathcal{B}|/d=\Omega(\rho)
in
\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})
of Lemma
A.3
and note
|\mathcal{B}|>0
then obtain the concise form
\eta(d,n)
below,

\displaystyle\eta(d,n)=\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}+\frac{(\log d)^{11/6}}{\rho^{1/3}n^{1/6}}+\frac{{s(\log d)^{3}}}{n^{1/2}}+\frac{1}{d}.

Recall that
t_{\lambda_{d}}=q(\alpha_{L};T^{B}_{N_{0j}})=O\left(\sqrt{\log d}\right)
with probability growing to 1 and
t_{\lambda_{d}}/h_{d}=O(\log d)
.
Under Assumption
5.1
,
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\log d}{\rho}\left(\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}+\frac{(\log d)^{11/6}}{\rho^{1/3}n^{1/6}}+\frac{{s(\log d)^{3}}}{{n}^{1/2}}\right)=o(1),\quad\frac{\log d}{\rho d_{0}}+\frac{({\log d})^{2}|S|}{\rho d_{0}^{2}p}=o(1),

and thus
\mathrm{III}_{1}+\mathrm{III}_{2}+\mathrm{III}_{3}=o(1)
with probability growing to 1.
Therefore, we have proved
(
A.10
)
, and finally establish the FDP control result below,

\mathrm{FDP}(\widehat{\alpha})\leq q\frac{d_{0}}{d}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

In order to establish FDR control, it remains to check the uniformly integrability of the random variable sequence in (
A.13
). Note for a sequence of random variable
R_{1},R_{2},\cdots
, we have
\sup_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[{|R_{n}|\mathds{1}{(|R_{n}|>x)}}\right]\leq x^{-1}\sup_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[{R_{n}^{2}}\right]
by Markov’s inequality. Then to show the uniform integrability of the random variable sequence
\{R_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
, where
R_{n}=\max_{1\leq m\leq\lambda_{d}}\left|\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}I_{j}(\alpha_{m})}{d_{0}\alpha_{m}}-1\right|
, it suffices to show
\sup_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[{R^{2}_{n}}\right]<\infty
. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[{\left(\max_{1\leq m\leq\lambda_{d}}\left|\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}I_{j}(\alpha_{m})}{d_{0}\alpha_{m}}-1\right|\right)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}I_{j}(\alpha_{m})-d_{0}\alpha_{m}}\right]^{2}}{d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\epsilon^{2}(\mathrm{III}_{1}+\mathrm{III}_{2}+\mathrm{III}_{3}).

Since
\mathrm{III}_{1}+\mathrm{III}_{2}+\mathrm{III}_{3}=o(1)
with probability growing to 1, we immediately have
\sup_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[{R^{2}_{n}}\right]<\infty
, thus finally establish the FDR control result:

\lim_{(n,d)\rightarrow\infty}\mathrm{FDR}\leq q\frac{d_{0}}{d}.

∎

A.2
Ancillary lemmas for Theorem
5.2

Lemma A.3
.

Recalling the definitions of
\mathrm{III}_{1},\mathrm{III}_{2}
in (
A.15
), we have

\mathrm{III}_{1}+\mathrm{III}_{2}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\epsilon^{2}h_{d}}\left(\frac{1}{\rho d_{0}}+\eta^{2}(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})\right),

where
\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})=O\big(\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}+\frac{(\log d)^{11/6}}{n^{1/6}\alpha_{L}^{1/3}}+\zeta_{1}\log d+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{\alpha_{L}}\big)
with
\zeta_{1}={s(\log d)^{2}}/\sqrt{n}
,
\zeta_{2}=1/d^{2}
.

First note the definitions of
T_{E},\breve{T}_{E},T_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}
and
\breve{T}_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}
in (
A.4
), (
A.6
), (
A.5
) and (
A.7
) respectively, then we apply Proposition
C.2
to
T=T_{E},T_{\bm{Y}}=\breve{T}_{E},T^{\mathcal{B}}=T_{E}^{\mathcal{B}},T_{\mathcal{W}}=\breve{T}_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}
with
E=N_{0j}
. And we can find the terms
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}
in (
C.4
), (
C.5
) to be
{s(\log d)^{2}}/\sqrt{n},1/d^{2}
respectively, due to (
D.28
) and (
D.33
) (i.e., the bound on the differences
T_{E}-T_{0}
,
T_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}-T_{0}^{\mathcal{B}}
) in the proof of Lemma
A.1
. Thus we have

\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(\max_{e\in N_{0j}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{e}|\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j}))}{\alpha}-1\right|=\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L}),

(A.17)

where
\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{e}=0,e\in N_{0j}
and
\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})=O\left(\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}+\zeta_{1}\log d+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{\alpha_{L}}\right)
with
\zeta_{1}={s(\log d)^{2}}/\sqrt{n}
,
\zeta_{2}=1/d^{2}
. Recalling the definition of
\mathrm{III}_{2}
in (
A.15
), we have

\mathrm{III}_{2}=\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\left(\mathbb{E}\left[{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}I_{j}(\alpha_{m})-d_{0}\alpha_{m}}\right]\right)^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}},

where
I_{j}(\alpha)=\mathds{1}{(\max_{e\in N_{0j}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{e}|\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j}))}
. Note that
\alpha_{m}\in[\alpha_{L},1],

\forall~1\leq m\leq\lambda_{d}
, then we arrive at the following bound

\mathrm{III}_{2}\leq\frac{\lambda_{d}}{\epsilon^{2}}\cdot\eta^{2}(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})\leq\frac{t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\epsilon^{2}h_{d}}\cdot\eta^{2}(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})

(A.18)

up to some constant, where the first inequality holds by (
A.17
). As for the second inequality, we recall the construction of
\{t_{m}\}_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}
(over the course of derivations from (
A.10
) to (
A.11
)) in the proof of Theorem
5.2
thus note
\alpha_{1}=1,t_{1}=0
and
t_{\lambda_{d}}-t_{1}=\sum_{m=2}^{\lambda_{d}}(t_{m}-t_{m-1})=(\lambda_{d}-1)h_{d}
.
Regarding the term
\mathrm{III}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathrm{Var}\left({I_{j}(\alpha_{m})-d_{0}\alpha_{m}}\right)}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}

(A.19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathbb{E}(I_{j}(\alpha_{m}))(1-\mathbb{E}(I_{j}(\alpha_{m}))}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}}\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{C}{\alpha_{m}}\leq\frac{C}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}\alpha_{L}}\cdot\frac{t_{\lambda_{d}}}{h_{d}},

where the first inequality holds due to
(
A.17
)
and the second inequality holds since
\alpha_{m}\geq\alpha_{L}

\forall~1\leq m\leq\lambda_{d}
and
t_{\lambda_{d}}=(\lambda_{d}-1)h_{d}
.
Therefore, combining (
A.18
) with (
A.19
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{1}+\mathrm{III}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\cdot\frac{t_{\lambda_{d}}}{h_{d}}\left(\frac{C}{d_{0}\alpha_{L}}+\eta^{2}(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})\right)\leq\frac{C^{\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\epsilon^{2}h_{d}}\left(\frac{1}{\rho d_{0}}+\eta^{2}(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})\right)

for some constant
C^{\prime}
, where the second inequality holds by the definition
\alpha_{L}=q|\mathcal{B}|/d
in the proof of Theorem
5.2
and the definition
\rho=|\mathcal{B}|/d
in Section
5
.
∎

Lemma A.4
.

Recalling the definition of
\mathrm{III}_{3}
in (
A.15
), we have

\mathrm{III}_{3}\leq\frac{C^{\prime\prime\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\rho\epsilon^{2}d_{0}h_{d}}\left(1+\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})d_{0}+\frac{|S|\log d}{d_{0}p}\right),

where
\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})=O\big(\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}+\frac{(\log d)^{11/6}}{n^{1/6}\alpha_{L}^{1/3}}+\zeta_{1}\log d+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{\alpha_{L}}\big)
with
\zeta_{1}={s(\log d)^{2}}/\sqrt{n}
,
\zeta_{2}=1/d^{2}
.

Note that
\mathrm{III}_{3}
in (
A.15
) equals

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{III}_{3}&=\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}}\mathrm{Cov}\left({I_{j_{1}}(\alpha_{m})},{I_{j_{2}}(\alpha_{m})}\right)}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}},\\
&\quad\text{ where $I_{j}(\alpha)=\mathds{1}{(\max_{e\in N_{0j}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{e}|\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j}))}$ }\end{split}

(A.20)

for
j\in\{j_{1},j_{2}\}
. To quantify the covariance between
I_{j_{1}}(\alpha_{m})
and
I_{j_{2}}(\alpha_{m})
for
j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}
, we define

W_{j}(\alpha)=\mathds{1}{(\max_{e\in N_{0j}}|Z_{e}|\geq c(\alpha,N_{0j}))},

(A.21)

where
(Z_{e})_{e\in E}
(with
E=N_{0j}
) is a Gaussian random vector and shares the same mean vector and covariance matrix as the term
(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n~{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}))_{(j,k)\in E}
in
\breve{T}_{E}
. Here
\breve{T}_{E}
(with
E=N_{0j}
) has the explicit form below

\breve{T}_{E}=\max_{(j,k)\in E}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n~{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k})\bigg|.

Remark here
\breve{T}_{E}
corresponds to the term
T_{\bm{Y}}
in Proposition
C.2
and
\max_{e\in E}|Z_{e}|
corresponds to the term
T_{\bm{Z}}
in Proposition
C.1
. And
c(\alpha,N_{0j})
is the corresponding Gaussian maxima quantile
q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})
(which does not need to be computed). Since
\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))=\alpha
, we immediately have have
\mathbb{E}[W_{j}(\alpha)]=\mathbb{P}\left({\max_{e\in N_{0j}}\sqrt{n}|Z_{e}|\geq c(\alpha,N_{0j})}\right)=\alpha
.

Now we replace
I_{j_{1}}(\alpha),I_{j_{2}}(\alpha)
in
\mathrm{III}_{3}
by
W_{j_{1}}(\alpha),W_{j_{2}}(\alpha)
and define
\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}
as

\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}:=\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}}\mathrm{Cov}\left({W_{j_{1}}(\alpha_{m})},{W_{j_{2}}(\alpha_{m})}\right)}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}.

(A.22)

To bound
\mathrm{III}_{3}-\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}
, we first note
\cov(I_{j_{1}}(\alpha),I_{j_{2}}(\alpha))=\mathbb{E}[I_{j_{1}}(\alpha)I_{j_{2}}(\alpha)]-\mathbb{E}[I_{j_{1}}(\alpha)]\mathbb{E}[I_{j_{2}}(\alpha)]
then separately deal with the term
\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{1}}(\alpha)I_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{1}}(\alpha)W_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]\right|
and the term
\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{1}}(\alpha)}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{1}}(\alpha)}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]\right|
.

By Lemma
A.6
, we have up to some constant factor,

\frac{\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{1}}(\alpha)I_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{1}}(\alpha)W_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]\right|}{\alpha^{2}}\leq\frac{\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})}{\alpha}.

Applying the same strategy to the term
\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{1}}(\alpha)}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]
, we obtain

\frac{\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{1}}(\alpha)}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{1}}(\alpha)}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]\right|}{\alpha^{2}}\leq\frac{\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})}{\alpha}.

Combining the above two inequalities, and noting the definition of
\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}
in (
A.22
), we derive the following bound on the difference between
\mathrm{III}_{3}
and
\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}
,

\left|\mathrm{III}_{3}-\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})}{\alpha_{m}}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\rho\epsilon^{2}h_{d}}\cdot\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L}).

where the second inequality holds due to the fact
\alpha_{m}\geq\alpha_{L}

\forall~1\leq m\leq\lambda_{d}
and
t_{\lambda_{d}}=(\lambda_{d}-1)h_{d}
, the definition
\alpha_{L}=q|\mathcal{B}|/d
in the proof of Theorem
5.2
, and the definition
\rho=|\mathcal{B}|/d
in Section
5
.

The above bound on
\left|\mathrm{III}_{3}-\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}\right|
, when combined with Lemma
A.5
, immediately establishes

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C^{\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\rho\epsilon^{2}h_{d}}\cdot\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})+\frac{C^{\prime\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\rho\epsilon^{2}d_{0}h_{d}}\left(1+C_{\bm{\Theta}}\frac{|S|\log d}{d_{0}p}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C^{\prime\prime\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\rho\epsilon^{2}d_{0}h_{d}}\left(1+\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})d_{0}+\frac{|S|\log d}{d_{0}p}\right),

for some constant
C^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
∎

Lemma A.5
.

Recalling the term
\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}
from (
A.22
) in the proof of Lemma
A.4
, we have

\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}=\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}}\cov(W_{j_{1}}(\alpha_{m}),W_{j_{2}}(\alpha_{m}))}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}\leq\frac{C^{\prime\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\rho\epsilon^{2}d_{0}h_{d}}\left(1+C_{\bm{\Theta}}\frac{|S|\log d}{d_{0}p}\right).

Similarly as in the proof of Lemma
A.4
, we define
(Z_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
to be jointly Gaussian such that this
(|N_{0j_{1}}|+|N_{0j_{2}}|)
-dimensional Gaussian random vector shares the same mean vector and covariance matrix as
(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n~{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}))_{(j,k)\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
.
Note that the two sub-vectors
(Z_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}
and
(Z_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}
are generally dependent. Then we define
(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}},(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{2}}}
to be two Gaussian random vectors such that

(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}(Z_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}},~~(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{2}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}(Z_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{2}}}~~\text{ and }(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{2}}}.

(A.23)

Recalling the definition of
W_{j}(\alpha)
in (
A.21
):
W_{j}(\alpha)=\mathds{1}{(\max_{e\in N_{0j}}|Z_{e}|\geq c(\alpha,N_{0j}))}
, we thus have the following,

\displaystyle\mathrm{IV}_{j_{1}j_{2}}(\alpha):=~

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\cov(W_{j_{1}}(\alpha_{m}),W_{j_{2}}(\alpha_{m}))\right|}{\alpha^{2}}

(A.24)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{1}}(\alpha)W_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{1}}(\alpha)}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]\right|}{\alpha^{2}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\Big|\mathbb{P}(\max_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}|Z_{e}|\geq c(\alpha,N_{0j_{1}}),\max_{e\in N_{0j_{2}}}|Z_{e}|\geq c(\alpha,N_{0j_{2}}))-

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}|Z^{\prime}_{e}|\geq c(\alpha,N_{0j_{1}}),\max_{e\in N_{0j_{2}}}|Z^{\prime}_{e}|\geq c(\alpha,N_{0j_{2}}))\Big|

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\Big|\mathbb{P}(\max_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}|Z_{e}|\geq t,\max_{e\in N_{0j_{2}}}|Z_{e}|\geq t)-\mathbb{P}(\max_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}|Z^{\prime}_{e}|\geq t,\max_{e\in N_{0j_{2}}}|Z^{\prime}_{e}|\geq t)\Big|

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\Big|\mathbb{P}(\max_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}|Z_{e}|\geq t)-\mathbb{P}(\max_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}|Z^{\prime}_{e}|\geq t)\Big|,

(A.25)

where the third equality follows due to the construction of
(Z_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}},(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
. Note that in the fourth equality, we assume
c(\alpha,N_{0j_{1}})=c(\alpha,N_{0j_{2}}):=t
without loss of generality, since we can rescale one of the maximum statistic by rescaling the Gaussian random vectors. Remark that the scaling will not break down the application of Theorem
3.3
, which will be explained in detail
later in this proof.
The last inequality holds by (
A.23
) and the fact that
\mathbb{P}\left({A\cap B}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left({A}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({B}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({A\cup B}\right)
.

Notice that we can apply the Cramér-type Gaussian comparison bound with
\ell_{0}
norm to control (
A.25
). Specifically, we first figure out the difference between the covariance matrices of
(Z_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
and
(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
. Denote the covariance matrices by
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}
and
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z^{\prime}}
respectively. As these two Gaussian random vectors have two sub-vectors, we write their covariance matrices in a block form

\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}_{11}&\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}_{12}\\
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}_{21}&\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}_{22}\end{array}\right),~~\bm{\Sigma}^{Z^{\prime}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\bm{\Sigma}^{Z^{\prime}}_{11}&\bm{O}\\
\bm{O}&\bm{\Sigma}^{Z^{\prime}}_{22}\end{array}\right).

where
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z^{\prime}}
is block diagonal due to (
A.23
). Note that we also have
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}_{11}=\bm{\Sigma}^{Z^{\prime}}_{11}
and
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}_{22}=\bm{\Sigma}^{Z^{\prime}}_{22}
. Then we have

\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}-\bm{\Sigma}^{Z^{\prime}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\bm{O}&\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}_{12}\\
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}_{21}&\bm{O}\end{array}\right).

(A.26)

Throughout the following proof, we assume
\bm{\Theta}_{jj}=1,j\in[d]
without loss of generality, since the standardized version is considered in
\breve{T}_{E}
(
A.6
). Recall that
(Z_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
shares the same covariance structure as
(Y_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
where
Y_{e}
(with
e=(j,k)
) is defined as

Y_{e}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}).

Then we are ready to calculate the covariance matrix
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}
. Specifically, we compute the entries in each block.
Regarding the block
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}_{11}
, for any
k,k^{\prime}\in N_{0j_{1}}
where
N_{0j_{1}}=\{k:\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k}=0\}
, we have the corresponding
(k,k^{\prime})
entry in
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}_{11}
equals

\cov({\bm{\Theta}}_{j_{1}}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}),{\bm{\Theta}}_{j_{1}}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k^{\prime}}-\mathbf{e}_{k^{\prime}}))=\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{1}}\bm{\Theta}_{kk^{\prime}}+\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k}\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k^{\prime}}=\bm{\Theta}_{kk^{\prime}},

(A.27)

by applying Isserlis’ theorem
[
33
]
and noting
\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k}=\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k^{\prime}}=0
. Similar results hold for the block
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}_{22}
. Regarding the block
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}_{12}
, consider
k_{1}\in N_{0j_{1}},k_{2}\in N_{0j_{2}}
, then we have the corresponding
(k_{1},k_{2})
entry in the block equals

\cov({\bm{\Theta}}_{j_{1}}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k_{1}}-\mathbf{e}_{k_{1}}),{\bm{\Theta}}_{j_{2}}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k_{2}}-\mathbf{e}_{k_{2}}))=\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{2}}\bm{\Theta}_{k_{1}k_{2}}+\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k_{2}}\bm{\Theta}_{j_{2}k_{1}}.

(A.28)

Now we have fully characterized the covariance matrix
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}
and the covariance matrix difference in (
A.26
)
for any
j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}
. Specifically, we have

||\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}-\bm{\Sigma}^{Z^{\prime}}||_{0}=||\bm{\Sigma}_{12}^{Z}||_{0}=\sum_{k_{1}\in N_{0j_{1}},k_{2}\in N_{0j_{2}}}\mathds{1}{(\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{2}}\bm{\Theta}_{k_{1}k_{2}}+\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k_{2}}\bm{\Theta}_{j_{2}k_{1}}\neq 0)}
.
Based on whether
\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{2}}
is zero or not, we consider the following two cases then handle them separately:

•

Case 1:
\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{2}}=0
. If
k_{1}=k_{2}
, then we have the covariance matrix entry (
A.28
) equal zero; If
k_{1}\neq k_{2}
, then (
A.28
) is nonzero only if
\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k_{2}}\neq 0,\bm{\Theta}_{j_{2}k_{1}}\neq 0
(i.e.,
k_{2}\notin N_{0j_{1}}
,
k_{1}\notin N_{0j_{2}}
). By the fact
j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}
and the definition of
\mathcal{H}_{0}=\{j:\|\bm{\Theta}_{j,-j}\|_{0}<k_{\tau}\}
, we have
\#\{(k_{1},k_{2}):k_{1}\neq k_{2},\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k_{2}}\neq 0,\bm{\Theta}_{j_{2}k_{1}}\neq 0\}\leq k_{\tau}^{2}
. Hence
||\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}-\bm{\Sigma}^{Z^{\prime}}||_{0}\leq k_{\tau}^{2}
.

•

Case 2:
\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{2}}\neq 0
. The covariance matrix entry (
A.28
) is nonzero only if
\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k_{2}}\neq 0,\bm{\Theta}_{j_{2}k_{1}}\neq 0
(i.e.,
k_{2}\notin N_{0j_{1}}
,
k_{1}\notin N_{0j_{2}}
) or
\bm{\Theta}_{k_{1}k_{2}}\neq 0
.

We start from the simpler case, i.e., Case 2 where
\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{2}}\neq 0
. Simply, we obtain

\mathrm{IV}_{j_{1}j_{2}}(\alpha)=\frac{|\cov(W_{j_{1}}(\alpha),W_{j_{2}}(\alpha))|}{\alpha^{2}}\leq\frac{\var(W_{j_{1}}(\alpha))}{\alpha^{2}}+\frac{\var(W_{j_{2}}(\alpha))}{\alpha^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{\alpha},

for some constant
C
since
\mathrm{Var}\left({W_{j}(\alpha)}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j}(\alpha)}\right](1-\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j}(\alpha)}\right])=\alpha(1-\alpha)
for
j=j_{1},j_{2}
.
For a fixed
j_{1}
, we also know that
|\{j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}:j_{2}\neq j_{1},\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{2}}\neq 0\}|<k_{\tau}
. Then we have

\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\sum_{\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{2}}\neq 0}\frac{\mathrm{IV}_{j_{1}j_{2}}(\alpha_{m})}{\epsilon^{2}d^{2}_{0}}\leq\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{d_{0}k_{\tau}}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}}\cdot\frac{C}{\alpha_{m}}\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}}\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{C^{\prime}}{\alpha_{m}},

(A.29)

where the last inequality holds due to the same derivations for
\mathrm{III}_{1}
in the proof of Lemma
A.3
.

Regarding Case
1
where
\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{2}}=0
, we will give a more careful treatment to
\mathrm{IV}_{j_{1}j_{2}}(\alpha)
in (
A.24
).
Due to the discussion about Case 1, we have
||\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}-\bm{\Sigma}^{Z^{\prime}}||_{0}\leq k_{\tau}^{2}
.
This fact will be utilized to derive a nice bound on
\mathrm{III^{\prime}_{3}}
. Indeed, we can apply Theorem
3.3
to (
A.25
) (with
U
and
V
chosen to be
Z_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
and
(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
respectively)
and obtain

\mathrm{IV}_{j_{1}j_{2}}(\alpha)\leq\frac{\log d}{\alpha p}\left(\sum_{k_{1}\in N_{0j_{1}},k_{2}\in N_{0j_{2}},k_{1}\neq k_{2}}\mathds{1}{(\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k_{2}}\bm{\Theta}_{j_{2}k_{1}}\neq 0)}\right).

(A.30)

when
\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{2}}=0
(i.e., under Case 1). Recall Theorem
3.3
assumes for Gaussian random vectors
U
and
V
, there exists a disjoint
\mathfrak{p}
-partition of nodes
\cup_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathcal{C}_{\ell}=[d]
such that
\sigma^{U}_{jk}=\sigma^{V}_{jk}=0
when
j\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell}
and
k\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell^{\prime}}
for some
\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}
.
This is the connectivity assumption. Theorem
3.3
also assumes that
U
and
V
have unit variances i.e.,
\sigma^{U}_{jj}=\sigma^{V}_{jj}=1,j\in[d]
and there exists some
\sigma_{0}<1
such that
|\sigma^{V}_{jk}|\leq\sigma_{0}
for any
j\neq k
and
|\{(j,k):j\neq k,|\sigma^{U}_{jk}|>\sigma_{0}\}|\leq b_{0}
for some constant
b_{0}
. Under its general version (which is actually proved in Appendix
B.2
), we only need to assume
a_{0}\leq\sigma^{U}_{jj}=\sigma^{V}_{jj}\leq a_{1},~\forall j\in[d]
, and given any
j\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell}
with some
\ell
, there exists at least one
m\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell^{\prime}}
such that
\sigma^{U}_{jj}=\sigma^{V}_{jj}=\sigma^{U}_{mm}=\sigma^{V}_{mm}
for any
\ell^{\prime}\neq\ell
.
From now, we will call it the general variance condition. Accordingly, we assume there exists some
\sigma_{0}<1
such that
|\sigma^{V}_{jk}/\sqrt{\sigma^{V}_{jj}\sigma^{V}_{kk}}|\leq\sigma_{0}
for any
j\neq k
and
|\{(j,k):j\neq k,|\sigma^{U}_{jk}|\sqrt{\sigma^{U}_{jj}\sigma^{U}_{kk}}>\sigma_{0}\}|\leq b_{0}
for some constant
b_{0}
. Such condition is referred as the general covariance assumption.
Below we give the details of applying Theorem
3.3
(with a general version of the variance assumption) by checking those three conditions.

We start from the connectivity assumption and the general variance condition. Notice that in Section
5
,
p
denotes the number of connected components in the associated graph
\mathcal{G}
of
\bm{X}
. Then we know there exist disjoint partitions of nodes
\cup_{\ell=1}^{p}\mathcal{C}^{X}_{\ell}=[d]
such that
\bm{\Theta}_{jk}=0
when
j\in\mathcal{C}^{X}_{\ell},k\in\mathcal{C}^{X}_{\ell^{\prime}}
for some
\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}
. We will utilize this fact to examine the covariance matrices of
U:=(Z_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
and
V:=(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
and show the connectivity assumption holds.
Note that for given
j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}
, there exist at least
p-2
components
\cup_{\ell=1}^{p-2}\mathcal{C}^{X}_{\ell}
such that
j_{1}
and
j_{2}
do not belong to them. Without loss of generality, we write
j_{1},j_{2}\notin\cup_{\ell=1}^{p-2}\mathcal{C}^{X}_{\ell}
. Thus we have
\cup_{l=1}^{p-2}\mathcal{C}^{X}_{\ell}\subset N_{0j_{1}}\cap N_{0j_{2}}
by definition.

In the following, we will show the number of connected components on the associated graph of the Gaussian random vector
U:=(Z_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
is at least
2(p-2)
by examining its covariance matrix
\bm{\Sigma}_{Z}
. First we focus on the covariance entries in the block
\bm{\Sigma}_{11}^{Z}
. When
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\in[p-2]
and
\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}
, we have for any
k\in\mathcal{C}^{X}_{\ell_{1}},k^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}^{X}_{\ell_{2}}
(thus
k,k^{\prime}\in N_{0j_{1}}\cap N_{0j_{2}}
), the
(k,k^{\prime})
covariance entry (
A.27
) in the block
\bm{\Sigma}_{11}^{Z}
equals

\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{1}}\bm{\Theta}_{kk^{\prime}}+\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k}\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k^{\prime}}=\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{1}}\bm{\Theta}_{kk^{\prime}}=0,

(A.31)

where the first equality holds since
k,k^{\prime}\in N_{0j_{1}}
, and the second equality holds since
\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}
.
Similarly, we have the
(k,k^{\prime})
covariance entry in the block
\bm{\Sigma}_{22}^{Z}
also equals to zero. Next we compute the covariance entries in the block
\bm{\Sigma}_{12}^{Z}
. For the same
(k,k^{\prime})
, we know that
k\in N_{0j_{1}},k^{\prime}\in N_{0j_{2}}
. Thus the corresponding covariance entry (
A.28
) equals

\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{2}}\bm{\Theta}_{kk^{\prime}}+\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k^{\prime}}\bm{\Theta}_{j_{2}k}=0,

(A.32)

since we also have
k\in N_{0j_{2}},k^{\prime}\in N_{0j_{1}}
and
k\in\mathcal{C}^{X}_{\ell_{1}},k^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}^{X}_{\ell_{2}}
for some
\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}
. Denote the nodes in the associated graph of
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}
by
\mathcal{V}_{Z}:=\{(j,k):k\in N_{0j},j=j_{1},j_{2}\}
. Remark here we use a pair
(j,k)
to represent a node since there exists some
k\in N_{0j_{1}}\cap N_{0j_{2}}
and we have to distinguish the covariance entries
(j_{1},k)
and
(j_{2},k)
. Based on previous calculations, we immediately find
\cup_{\ell=1}^{2(p-2)}\mathcal{C}^{Z}_{\ell}\subset\mathcal{V}_{Z}
, where
\mathcal{C}^{Z}_{\ell}
is chosen to be

\mathcal{C}^{Z}_{\ell}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\{(j_{1},k):k\in\mathcal{C}^{X}_{\ell}\}&~\text{when}~1\leq\ell\leq p-2,\\
\{(j_{2},k):k\in\mathcal{C}^{X}_{\ell}\}&~\text{when}~p-1\leq\ell\leq 2(p-2).\end{array}\right.

(A.33)

Further, we know they form different components on the associated graph of
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}
. This is due to (
A.31
) and (
A.32
).
The above results also apply to the Gaussian random vector
V:=(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
by construction of
Z^{\prime}_{e}
, i.e., we have the same subset of nodes
\cup_{\ell=1}^{2(p-2)}\mathcal{C}^{Z}_{\ell}\subset\mathcal{V}_{Z}
from different components on the associated graph of
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z^{\prime}}
.

When
k\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell}
for some
\ell\in[p-2]
, the corresponding diagonal entries of the covariance matrices
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z},\bm{\Sigma}^{Z^{\prime}}
equal

\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{1}}\bm{\Theta}_{kk}+\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k}\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k}=\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{1}}\bm{\Theta}_{kk}=1=\bm{\Theta}_{j_{2}j_{2}}\bm{\Theta}_{kk},

where the first equality holds since
\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k}=0
when
k\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell}\subset N_{0j_{1}}
. As for the second equality, we use the fact that
\bm{\Theta}_{jj}=1,j\in[d]
. This is because
\breve{T}_{E}
in (
A.6
) considers the standardized version
{{\bm{\Theta}}_{jk}}/{\sqrt{{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}
. Remark that the rescaling in Lemma
A.5
is performed on one of the two random vectors
(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}},(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{2}}}
. Then we have the variances across the
p-2
components
\cup_{\ell=1}^{p-2}\mathcal{C}^{Z}_{\ell}
are the same. The variances across the other
p-2
components
\cup_{\ell=p-1}^{2(p-2)}\mathcal{C}^{Z}_{\ell}
are also the same. Finally, we show there exist at least
p-2
components
\cup_{\ell=1}^{p-2}\mathcal{C}^{Z}_{\ell}
(or
\cup_{\ell=p-1}^{2(p-2)}\mathcal{C}^{Z}_{\ell}
) satisfying the requirement in the connectivity assumption and the general variance condition.

Regarding the general covariance condition, we first note
that
\bm{\Theta}\in\mathcal{U}(M,s,r_{0})
which says that
\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Theta})\geq 1/r_{0},\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Theta})\leq r_{0}
. Thus we have
\max_{j,k\in[d],j\neq k}|\bm{\Theta}_{jk}|\leq\sigma_{0}
for some
\sigma_{0}<1
. Below we will examine all the off-diagonal entries of
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}
and
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z^{\prime}}
. Regarding the block
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}_{11}
, for any
k,k^{\prime}\in N_{0j_{1}},k\neq k^{\prime}
where
N_{0j_{1}}=\{k:\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k}=0\}
, (
A.27
) says that the corresponding
(k,k^{\prime})
entry in
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}_{11}
equals
\bm{\Theta}_{kk^{\prime}}
(here we have
|\bm{\Theta}_{kk^{\prime}}|\leq\sigma_{0}
). Similar results hold for the block
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}_{22}
.
Regarding the block
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}_{12}
, consider
k_{1}\in N_{0j_{1}},k_{2}\in N_{0j_{2}}
, then we have the corresponding
(k_{1},k_{2})
entry in the block equals
\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k_{2}}\bm{\Theta}_{j_{2}k_{1}}
. This is due to (
A.28
) and the fact that
\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{2}}=0
under Case 1. Only when
k_{2}=j_{1},k_{1}=j_{2}
, we have
\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k_{2}}\bm{\Theta}_{j_{2}k_{1}}=1
. Otherwise,
|\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k_{2}}\bm{\Theta}_{j_{2}k_{1}}|\leq\sigma_{0}^{2}<\sigma_{0}
always holds. As for the
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z^{\prime}}
, since its block
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z^{\prime}}_{12}=\bm{O}
, we immediately have the absolute values of all its off-diagonal entries is bounded by
\sigma_{0}
. In summary, we verify the covariance condition of Theorem
3.3
(here
U
and
V
are chosen to be
Z_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
and
(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
respectively).

Having checked all the three conditions, we now obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\sum_{\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{2}}=0}\frac{\mathrm{IV}_{j_{1}j_{2}}(\alpha_{m})}{\epsilon^{2}d^{2}_{0}}

(A.34)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\log d}{\alpha_{m}p}\bigg(\sum_{k_{1}\in N_{0j_{1}},k_{2}\in N_{0j_{2}},k_{1}\neq k_{2}}\mathds{1}{(\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k_{2}}\bm{\Theta}_{j_{2}k_{1}}\neq 0)}\bigg)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\bm{\Theta}}|S|\log d}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}p}\left(\frac{1}{d_{0}}\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{C^{\prime}}{\alpha_{m}}\right),

where
S
represents the set

S=\{(j_{1},j_{2},k_{1},k_{2}):j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2},k_{1}\neq k_{2},\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}j_{2}}=\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k_{1}}=\bm{\Theta}_{j_{2}k_{2}}=0,\bm{\Theta}_{j_{1}k_{2}}\neq 0,\bm{\Theta}_{j_{2}k_{1}}\neq 0\}

as defined in Section
5
, and
C_{\bm{\Theta}}
is some universal constant over
\bm{\Theta}\in\mathcal{U}(M,s,r_{0})
. Finally, combining (
A.34
) with (
A.29
), we obtain the following bound on
\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\bm{\Theta}}|S|\log d}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}p}\left(\frac{1}{d_{0}}\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{C^{\prime}}{\alpha_{m}}\right)+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}}\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{C^{\prime}}{\alpha_{m}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{C_{\bm{\Theta}}|S|\log d}{d_{0}p}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}}\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{C^{\prime}}{\alpha_{m}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C^{\prime\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\rho\epsilon^{2}d_{0}h_{d}}\left(1+\frac{C_{\bm{\Theta}}|S|\log d}{d_{0}p}\right),

where the last inequality holds due to the same derivations for
\mathrm{III}_{1}
in the proof of Lemma
A.3
.
∎

Lemma A.6
.

Recall the definitions of
I_{j}(\alpha)
and
W_{j}(\alpha)
in (
A.20
) and (
A.21
), for
j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}
, when
\alpha\in[\alpha_{L},1]
, we have

{\big|\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{1}}(\alpha)I_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{1}}(\alpha)W_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]\big|}\leq{\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})}{\alpha}.

(A.35)

First express
\big|\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{1}}(\alpha)I_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{1}}(\alpha)W_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]\big|
as

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{1}}(\alpha)I_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{1}}(\alpha)W_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]\big|

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{e}|\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j_{1}}),\max_{e\in N_{0j_{2}}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{e}|\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j_{2}})\Big)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}|Z_{e}|\geq c(\alpha,N_{0j_{1}}),\max_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}|Z_{e}|\geq c(\alpha,N_{0j_{2}})\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\begin{split}=~&\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{N_{0j_{1}}}\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j_{1}}),T_{N_{0j_{2}}}\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j_{2}})\Big)\\
~~&-\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}|Z_{e}|\geq c(\alpha,N_{0j_{1}}),\max_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}|Z_{e}|\geq c(\alpha,N_{0j_{2}})\Big)\Big|,\end{split}

(A.36)

where the second equality holds by the definition of
T_{E}
in (
A.4
) and the definitions of
N_{0j_{1}},N_{0j_{2}}
. Now proving the bound in (
A.35
) is reduced to showing

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{N_{0j_{1}}}\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j_{1}}),T_{N_{0j_{2}}}\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j_{2}})\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}|Z_{e}|\geq c(\alpha,N_{0j_{1}}),\max_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}|Z_{e}|\geq c(\alpha,N_{0j_{2}})\Big)\Big|\\
~~&\leq{\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})}{\alpha}.\end{split}

(A.37)

We first relate the notations in the above expression to the notations in Appendix
C
:
T_{N_{0j_{1}}},T_{N_{0j_{2}}}
correspond to
T
;
\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j_{1}}),\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j_{2}})
correspond to
q_{\xi}(\alpha,T^{\mathcal{B}})
;
\max_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}|Z_{e}|
,
\max_{e\in N_{0j_{2}}}|Z_{e}|
correspond to
T_{\bm{Z}}
;
c(\alpha,N_{0j_{1}}),c(\alpha,N_{0j_{2}})
correspond to
q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})
. In Appendix
C
, we prove Propositions
C.1
and
C.2
. And the strategy can be used to derive the bound on (
A.36
). First, we note that
T_{N_{0j_{1}}},T_{N_{0j_{2}}}
satisfy the conditions of Proposition
C.2
, i.e., (
C.4
) and (
C.5
). This is due to the same derivations as the first parapraph of the proof of Lemma
A.3
. Since the proving strategy is quite similar, we omit the proof of (
A.37
) for simplicity. Instead, we prove (
A.38
), i.e.,
when
\alpha\in[\alpha_{L},1]
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{D}:=~&\Big|\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}_{1}}\geq q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}_{1}}),T_{\bm{Y}_{2}}\geq q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}_{2}})}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}_{1}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{1}}),T_{\bm{Z}_{2}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})}\right)\Big|\\
\leq~&C\alpha\left(\frac{(\log d)^{11/6}}{n^{1/6}\alpha_{L}^{1/3}}+\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}\right),\end{split}

(A.38)

where
T_{\bm{Y}_{1}},T_{\bm{Y}_{2}}
correspond to
\breve{T}_{E}
with
E=N_{0j_{1}},N_{0j_{2}}
respectively,
T_{\bm{W}_{1}},T_{\bm{W}_{2}}
correspond to
\breve{T}_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}
with
E=N_{0j_{1}},N_{0j_{2}}
respectively, and
T_{\bm{Z}_{1}}=\max_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}|Z_{e}|,T_{\bm{Z}_{2}}=\max_{e\in N_{0j_{2}}}|Z_{e}|
. As for the quantiles,
q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}_{1}}),q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}_{2}})
are the Gaussian multiplier bootstrap quantiles based on
T_{\bm{W}_{1}},T_{\bm{W}_{2}}
.
q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{1}}),q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})
are the quantiles of the Gaussian maxima
T_{\bm{Z}_{1}},T_{\bm{Z}_{2}}
.
Denote
A_{1}=\{T_{\bm{Y}_{1}}\geq q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}_{1}})\},A_{2}=\{T_{\bm{Y}_{2}}\geq q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}_{2}})\}
,
B_{1}=\{T_{\bm{Y}_{1}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{1}})\}
,
B_{2}=\{T_{\bm{Y}_{2}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})\}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}_{12}~:=~

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}_{1}}\geq q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}_{1}}),T_{\bm{Y}_{2}}\geq q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}_{2}})}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}_{1}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{1}}),T_{\bm{Y}_{2}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})}\right)\big|

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({(A_{1}\cap A_{2})\ominus(B_{1}\cap B_{2})}\right)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({(A_{1}\cap A_{2})\cap(B_{1}^{c}\cup B_{2}^{c})}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({(B_{1}\cap B_{2})\cap(A_{1}^{c}\cup A_{2}^{c})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({A_{1}\cap B_{1}^{c}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({A_{2}\cap B_{2}^{c}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({B_{1}\cap A_{1}^{c}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({B_{2}\cap A_{2}^{c}}\right)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({(A_{1}\cap B_{1}^{c})\cup(B_{1}\cap A_{1}^{c})}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({(A_{2}\cap B_{2}^{c})\cup(B_{2}\cap A_{2}^{c})}\right)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({A_{1}\ominus B_{1}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({A_{2}\ominus B_{2}}\right).

(A.39)

By (
C.14
) and (
C.15
), we can bound (
A.39
) as

\mathrm{D}_{12}\leq\mathbb{P}\left({A_{1}\ominus B_{1}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({A_{2}\ominus B_{2}}\right)\leq~2C^{\prime}\alpha\left(\frac{(\log d)^{11/6}}{n^{1/6}\alpha_{L}^{1/3}}+\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}\right).

(A.40)

By the triangle inequality, we have the following bound on
\mathrm{D}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}=~

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}_{1}}\geq q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}_{1}}),T_{\bm{Y}_{2}}\geq q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}_{2}})}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}_{1}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{1}}),T_{\bm{Z}_{2}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})}\right)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}_{12}+{\Big|\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}_{1}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{1}}),T_{\bm{Y}_{2}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}_{1}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{1}}),T_{\bm{Z}_{2}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})}\right)\Big|}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}_{12}+\Big|\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}_{1}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{1}})}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}_{1}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{1}})}\right)\big|+\big|\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}_{2}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}_{2}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})}\right)\Big|

\displaystyle~+\underbrace{\Big|\mathbb{P}\left({\{T_{\bm{Y}_{1}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{1}})\}\cup\{T_{\bm{Y}_{2}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})\}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({\{T_{\bm{Z}_{1}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{1}})\}\cup\{T_{\bm{Z}_{2}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})\}}\right)\Big|}_{\mathrm{D}^{\prime}_{12}},

(A.41)

where the last inequality holds since
\mathbb{P}\left({A\cap B}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left({A}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({B}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({A\cup B}\right)
. For the second term and the third term in (
A.41
), we can directly apply the results (
C.10
) in Proposition
C.1
and bound them as

\displaystyle\begin{split}~~&\Big|\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}_{1}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{1}})}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}_{1}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{1}})}\right)\big|+\big|\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}_{2}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}_{2}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})}\right)\Big|\\
~~&\leq C\alpha\cdot\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}\end{split}

(A.42)

for some constant
C
.
Regarding the term
\mathrm{D}^{\prime}_{12}
, we assume
q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})=q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}}):=t
without loss of generality. This is because
q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{1}}),q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})
are all deterministic values and we can rescale the random vector inside one of the maximum statistics
T_{\bm{Z}_{1}},T_{\bm{Z}_{2}}
. Now we rewrite
\mathrm{D}^{\prime}_{12}
based on
q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})=q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})=t
and derive the following bound:

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}^{\prime}_{12}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}\left({\max\{T_{\bm{Y}_{1}},T_{\bm{Y}_{2}}\}\geq t}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({\max\{T_{\bm{Z}_{1}},T_{\bm{Z}_{2}}\}\geq t}\right)\Big|

(A.43)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C^{\prime\prime}(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}\cdot\mathbb{P}\left({\max\{T_{\bm{Z}_{1}},T_{\bm{Z}_{2}}\}\geq t}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C^{\prime\prime}(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}\cdot\big(\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}_{1}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{1}})}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}_{2}}\geq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}_{2}})}\right)\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2C^{\prime\prime}\alpha\cdot\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}},

(A.44)

where the first inequality holds by applying Corollary 5.1 of
[
43
]
similarly as in the derivation of (
C.10
). Here we briefly explain why Corollary 5.1 of
[
43
]
is applicable to (
A.43
).
Note that
\max\{T_{\bm{Y}_{1}},T_{\bm{Y}_{2}}\}={T_{\bm{Y}_{12}}}
is the maximum statistic with respect to the random vectors which concatenate the random vectors involved in
T_{\bm{Y}_{1}},T_{\bm{Y}_{2}}
. Write
T_{\bm{Y}_{1}},T_{\bm{Y}_{2}}
explicitly as

T_{\bm{Y}_{1}}:=\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}^{(1)}_{i}\right\|_{\infty},\quad T_{\bm{Y}_{2}}:=\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}^{(2)}_{i}\right\|_{\infty},

and denote
\bm{Y}^{(12)}_{i}=(\bm{Y}^{(1)}_{i},\bm{Y}^{(2)}_{i})
, then
T_{\bm{Y}_{12}}
is defined as

T_{\bm{Y}_{12}}:=\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}^{(12)}_{i}\right\|_{\infty}.

By the definition of
\bm{Z}_{1},\bm{Z}_{2}
, we have
{\rm Cov}((\bm{Z}_{1}^{\top},\bm{Z}_{2}^{\top})^{\top})={\rm Cov}((\bm{Y}_{1}^{\top},\bm{Y}_{2}^{\top})^{\top})
.
Hence we can apply Corollary 5.1 of
[
43
]
to (
A.43
).
Now we combine (
A.40
), (
A.41
), (
A.42
) with (
A.44
) and obtain the following bound

\mathrm{D}\leq C\alpha\left(\frac{(\log d)^{11/6}}{n^{1/6}\alpha_{L}^{1/3}}+\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}\right),

for some constant
C
, thus (
A.38
) is established. The above strategy of obtaining (
A.38
) can be similarly applied to the term in (
A.36
), then establishes the bound in (
A.35
).

∎

A.3
Proof of Theorem
4.2

Throughout the proof, we condition on the design matrix
\bm{X}
, but without explicitly writing it out in order to simplify the notation. In the context of selecting hub response variables, we recall
\mathcal{H}_{0}=\{j\in[d_{1}]:||\bm{\Theta}_{j}||_{0}\geq k_{\tau}\}
and
d_{0}=|\mathcal{H}_{0}|
. For a non-hub response variable
j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
, let
N_{0j}
be the set of its null covariates, i.e.,
N_{0j}=\{(j,k):\bm{\Theta}_{jk}=0\}
.

To establish FDR control, we follow the same derivations as in the proof of Theorem
5.2
. Specifically, it suffices to bound

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\mathbb{\var}[\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}I_{j}(\alpha_{m})-d_{0}\alpha_{m}]}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}+\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{(\mathbb{E}[\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}I_{j}(\alpha_{m})-d_{0}\alpha_{m}])^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}}\cov(I_{j_{1}}(\alpha_{m}),I_{j_{2}}(\alpha_{m}))}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}:=\mathrm{III}_{1}+\mathrm{III}_{2}+0

(A.45)

for any
\epsilon>0
. In the above terms, the sequence
\{\alpha_{m}\}_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}
is chosen similarly as in the proof of Theorem
5.2
and
I_{j}(\alpha)
is defined as

I_{j}(\alpha)=\mathds{1}{(\max_{e\in N_{0j}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{e}|\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j}))},

where
\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{j}
is the debiased Lasso estimator defined in (
4.2
). Note that the cross term in (
A.45
) equals zero as
\cov(I_{j_{1}}(\alpha_{m}),I_{j_{2}}(\alpha_{m}))=0
. This is because
\bm{Y}^{(j)},j\in[d_{1}]
are conditionally independent given
\mathbf{X}
. Therefore it suffices to bound
\mathrm{III}_{1}
and
\mathrm{III}_{2}
. By applying Lemma
A.3
with the term
\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})
replaced by
\eta_{0}(d_{1},d_{2},n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})
in Lemma
A.9
, (
A.45
) can be controlled by

\mathrm{III}_{2}+\mathrm{III}_{2}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}t_{\lambda_{d_{2}}}}{\epsilon^{2}h_{d_{2}}}\left(\frac{d_{1}}{d_{0}|\mathcal{B}|}+\eta_{0}(d_{1},d_{2},n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})\right),

where
\alpha_{L}=q|\mathcal{B}|/d_{1}
and
t_{\lambda_{d_{2}}},h_{d_{2}}
are similarly defined as in the proof of Theorem
5.2
. According to Lemma
A.9
, we have the explicit form of
\eta_{0}(d_{1},d_{2},n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\delta,\alpha_{L})
:

\eta_{0}(d_{1},d_{2},n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\delta,\alpha_{L})=\zeta_{1}\log d_{2}+(\log d_{2})^{5/2}\delta^{1/2}+\frac{\eta+\zeta_{2}}{\alpha_{L}},

where

\zeta_{1}=O({s\log d_{2}}/{\sqrt{n}})
,
\zeta_{2}=O(e^{-c_{1}n}+d_{2}^{-\widetilde{c_{0}}\wedge c_{2}})
,
\delta
satisfies
\frac{1}{\delta}\sqrt{\frac{s\log d_{2}}{n}}=O(1)
and
\eta=e^{-c_{1}n}+\frac{1}{d_{2}}+\frac{1}{n\delta^{2}}
. By rearranging, we obtain the following bound on
\mathrm{III}_{2}+\mathrm{III}_{2}
:

\frac{\log d_{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{d_{0}\rho}+\frac{s(\log d_{2})^{2}}{n^{1/2}}+(\log d_{2})^{5/2}\delta^{1/2}+\frac{1}{n\delta^{2}\rho}+\frac{1}{\rho}\big(\frac{1}{d_{2}}+{e^{-c_{1}n}+d_{2}^{-\widetilde{c_{0}}\wedge c_{2}}}\big)\right).

where
\rho=\mathcal{B}/d_{1}
. We choose
\delta
to be
\frac{1}{(n\rho)^{2/5}\log d_{2}}
and have
\delta>\frac{1}{n^{2/5}\log d_{2}}
(since
\rho<1
). Thus this choice of
\delta
satisfies the requirement in Lemma
A.9
. Finally we have
(
A.45
)
is bounded as

\frac{\log d_{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{d_{0}\rho}+\frac{s(\log d_{2})^{2}}{n^{1/2}}+\frac{(\log d_{2})^{2}}{(n\rho)^{1/5}}+\frac{1}{\rho d_{2}}\right).

Under the stated assumption in Theorem
4.2
, the above term is
o(1)
. Thus the FDP control result is established. Due to similar derivations as in Theorem
5.2
, the FDR control result follows.
∎

A.4
Ancillary lemmas for Theorem
4.2

To prove FDR control, we will establish a key result, i.e., Lemma
A.9
in this section. Recall that in Section
4
, we utilize the following result

\sqrt{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{j}-\bm{\Theta}_{j})=Z_{j}+\Xi,\quad Z_{j}|\bm{X}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{j}^{2}M\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}M^{\top}).

and approximate the quantile of the maximum statistics
T_{E}=\max_{(j,k)\in E}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jk}|
by
T_{E}^{\mathcal{N}}=\max_{(j,k)\in E}|Z_{jk}|
. Lemma
A.9
basically establishes the Cramér deviation bounds for such quantile approximation. Note that this lemma can be seen as a special case of Proposition
C.1
since the involving random vector
\sqrt{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{j}-\bm{\Theta}_{j})
can be decomposed into a Gaussian random vector plus some error term. Hence we do not need to use the results in
[
43
]
to handle the case of a general random vector (and quantify Gaussian approximation errors).

In this section, we will define some notations similar to the theoretical results in Appendix
C
. First, we will drop the
j
-th subscript for simplicity. Without loss of generality, we prove relevant results for
E=\{(j,k):k\in[d_{2}]\}
and drop the subscript
E
. Note the results hold for any
j\in[d_{1}]
and any subset of
\{(j,k):k\in[d_{2}]\}
. Now we rewrite (
4.4
) using new notations, i.e.,

\sqrt{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{j}-\bm{\Theta}_{j})=\bm{Z}+\Xi,\quad\bm{Z}|\bm{X}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{j}^{2}M\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}M^{\top}),

(A.46)

and denote its maximum by
T_{\bm{Z}}=||\bm{Z}||_{\infty}
. Intuitively, we can use the quantile of
T_{\bm{Z}}
to approximate the quantile of
T:=\sqrt{n}||\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{j}-\bm{\Theta}_{j}||_{\infty}
. Since the covariance matrix
\sigma_{j}^{2}M\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}M^{\top}
of the Gaussian random vector
\bm{Z}
is not completely known, we can not directly compute its quantile (denoted by
q(\alpha;\bm{Z})
). Instead, we first estimate the unknown parameter
\sigma_{j}
by
\widehat{\sigma}_{j}
, which is constructed according to (
4.5
). Then we define
\bm{W}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\widehat{\sigma}_{j}^{2}M\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}M^{\top})
(given the data
\bm{X},\bm{Y}^{(j)}
), and denote its maximum by
T_{\bm{W}}=||W||_{\infty}
. We will approximate the unknown quantile of
T
by the conditional quantile
q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}})
. Here we use the
\xi
subscript to emphasize that we are conditioning on the data when defining such quantiles.

Due to the existence of the term
\Xi
in (
A.46
), there also exist additional estimation errors when we approximate the quantiles of
T
by the conditional quantiles
q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}})
. Lemma
A.8
characterizes such approximation errors. As for the difference between the distributions of the two Gaussian random vectors
\bm{W}
and
\bm{Z}
, Lemma
A.8
provides a bound on the maximal difference of their covariance matrices, which is denoted by
\Delta_{\infty}
. Finally, Lemma
A.9
builds on these results and establishes the Cramér-type deviation bounds for the quantile approximation of
T
.

Lemma A.7
.

In the context of multiple linear models, we have

\mathbb{P}(|T-T_{\bm{Z}}|>\zeta_{1})<\zeta_{2},

where
\zeta_{1}=O({s\log d_{2}}/{\sqrt{n}})
and
\zeta_{2}=O(e^{-c_{1}n}+d_{2}^{-\widetilde{c_{0}}\wedge c_{2}})
.

By Theorem 2.5 in
[
39
]
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{j}-\bm{\Theta}_{j})=\bm{Z}+\Xi,\quad\bm{Z}|\bm{X}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{j}^{2}M\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}M^{\top}),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\|\Xi\|_{\infty}\geq\Big(\frac{16ac\,\sigma}{C_{\rm min}}\Big)\frac{s\log d_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\leq 4\,e^{-c_{1}n}+4\,d_{2}^{-\widetilde{c_{0}}\wedge c_{2}}\,.

Thus we immediately obtain the following bound on the difference between
T
and
T_{\bm{Z}}
:

\mathbb{P}(|T-T_{\bm{Z}}|>\zeta_{1})<\zeta_{2}

where
\zeta_{1}=O({s\log d_{2}}/{\sqrt{n}})
and
\zeta_{2}=O(e^{-c_{1}n}+d_{2}^{-\widetilde{c_{0}}\wedge c_{2}})
.
∎

Lemma A.8
.

For the the maximal difference term
\Delta_{\infty}=||\widehat{\sigma}^{2}M\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}M^{\top}-\sigma^{2}M\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}M^{\top}||_{\max}
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left({\Delta_{\infty}\geq\delta}\right)\leq\eta,

(A.47)

where
\delta
satisfies
\frac{1}{\delta}\sqrt{\frac{s\log d_{2}}{n}}=O(1)
and
\eta=O\left(e^{-c_{1}n}+\frac{1}{d_{2}}+\frac{1}{n\delta^{2}}\right)
.

To bound
\Delta_{\infty}
, we start with the term
|\widehat{\sigma}/{\sigma}-1|
. First we denote

\mathcal{E}_{n}=\mathcal{E}_{n}(\phi_{0},s_{0},K):=\Big\{\bm{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d_{1}}:\min_{S:|S|\leq s_{0}}\phi(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}},S)\geq\phi_{0},\max_{j\in[d_{1}]}\bm{\Sigma}_{jj}\leq K,\bm{\Sigma}=(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X})/n\Big\}

similarly as in Theorem 7.(a) of
[
39
]
, where
\phi(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}},S)
is the compatibility constant as defined in Definition 1 of
[
39
]
. Following the proof of Lemma
14
in
[
39
]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\Big|\frac{\widehat{\sigma}}{\sigma}-1\Big|\geq\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle~\leq\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{X}\notin\mathcal{E}_{n}}\right)+\sup_{\bm{X}\in\mathcal{E}_{n}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\frac{\widehat{\sigma}}{\sigma}-1\Big|\geq\epsilon\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big)

\displaystyle~\leq 4e^{-c_{1}n}+\sup_{\bm{X}\in\mathcal{E}_{n}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{||\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{E}||_{\infty}}{n\sigma^{\star}}\geq{\widetilde{\lambda}}/{4}\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big)+\sup_{\bm{X}\in\mathcal{E}_{n}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\frac{{\sigma}^{\star}}{\sigma}-1\Big|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{10}\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big)

(A.48)

where
\widetilde{\lambda}=10\sqrt{(2\log d_{2})/n}
,
\sigma^{\star}
is the oracle estimator of
\sigma
introduced in
[
77
]
and
\epsilon
satisfies
\frac{2\sqrt{s}\widetilde{\lambda}}{\sigma^{\star}\phi_{0}}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}<a_{0}
.

Now we separately bound the last two terms in (
A.48
). The second term in (
A.48
) can be bounded by the derivation in the proof of Theorem 2 (ii)
[
77
]
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{X}\in\mathcal{E}_{n}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{||\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{E}||_{\infty}}{n\sigma^{\star}}\geq{\widetilde{\lambda}}/{4}\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d_{2}\mathbb{P}\Big(|L_{k}|\geq\sqrt{{2\log(d_{2}^{25/4})}/{n}}\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big)

(A.49)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d_{2}\cdot\frac{C}{d_{2}^{25/4}\sqrt{\log d_{2}}}\leq\frac{C}{d_{2}},

where
L_{k}
is the
k
-th element of
\frac{\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{E}}{n\sigma^{\star}}
and
\frac{\sqrt{n-1}L_{k}}{\sqrt{1-L_{k}^{2}}}
follows the Student’s t-distribution with
n-1
degrees of freedom. Then (
A.49
) holds due to equation (A7) in
[
77
]
together with the union bound. As for the last term in
(
A.48
)
, we note
n(\sigma^{\star}/\sigma)^{2}
follows the
\chi^{2}_{n}
distribution according to
[
77
]
. Thus by Markov’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{X}\in\mathcal{E}_{n}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\frac{{\sigma}^{\star}}{\sigma}-1\Big|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{10}\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\left[{(n(\sigma^{\star}/\sigma)^{2}-n)^{2}}\right]}{n^{2}\epsilon^{2}}\leq\frac{2C^{\prime}}{n\epsilon^{2}}.

(A.50)

Now we arrive at the following bound on
\Delta_{\infty}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\Delta_{\infty}\geq(\epsilon^{2}+2\epsilon)\cdot\sigma^{2}||M\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}M^{\top}||_{\max}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(||\widehat{\sigma}^{2}M\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}M^{\top}-\sigma^{2}M\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}M^{\top}||_{\max}\geq{(\epsilon^{2}+2\epsilon)\cdot\sigma^{2}||M\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}M^{\top}||_{\max}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\frac{\widehat{\sigma}}{\sigma}-1\Big|\geq\epsilon\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4e^{-c_{1}n}+\frac{C}{d_{2}}+\frac{2C^{\prime}}{n\epsilon^{2}},

where the last inequality comes from combining (
A.48
), (
A.49
) with (
A.50
). Note that the proof of Theorem 16 in
[
39
]
shows that
||M\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}M^{\top}||_{\max}=O(1)
. Hence, we finally establish (
A.47
) with

\delta={\sigma^{2}(\epsilon^{2}+2\epsilon)}{||M\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}M^{\top}||_{\max}}=C_{\sigma}\epsilon,\quad\eta=O\left(e^{-c_{1}n}+\frac{1}{d_{2}}+\frac{1}{n\delta^{2}}\right).

where
C_{\sigma}
is some constant and
\delta=C_{\sigma}\epsilon
satisfies
\frac{1}{\delta}\sqrt{\frac{s\log d_{2}}{n}}=O(1)
due to the choice of
\epsilon
.
∎

Lemma A.9
.

Based on the result about the approximation error between
T
and
T_{\bm{Z}}
(Lemma
A.7
) and the bound on
||\Delta||_{\infty}
in Lemma
A.8
, we have

\sup_{\alpha\in[\alpha_{L},1]}\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(T>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}}))}{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}-1\right|=O\left(\eta_{0}(d_{1},d_{2},n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\delta,\alpha_{L})\right),

(A.51)

where
\eta_{0}(d_{1},d_{2},n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\delta,\alpha_{L}):=\zeta_{1}\log d_{2}+(\log d_{2})^{5/2}\delta^{1/2}+\frac{\eta+\zeta_{2}}{\alpha_{L}}
with
\zeta_{1}=O({s\log d_{2}}/{\sqrt{n}})
,
\zeta_{2}=O(e^{-c_{1}n}+d_{2}^{-\widetilde{c_{0}}\wedge c_{2}})
. Here
\delta
is a term to be determined and we requite
\frac{1}{\delta}\sqrt{\frac{s\log d_{2}}{n}}=O(1)
.
\eta
depends on
\delta
, i.e.,

\eta=e^{-c_{1}n}+\frac{1}{d_{2}}+\frac{1}{n\delta^{2}}
.

First we have
\mathbb{P}(|T-T_{\bm{Z}}|>\zeta_{1})<\zeta_{2}
by Lemma
A.7
, thus we obtain

\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(T>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}}))}{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}-1\right|\leq\max\{\mathrm{II}_{1},\mathrm{II}_{2}\}+\frac{2\zeta_{2}}{\alpha}

for
\alpha\in[\alpha_{L},1]
, where
\mathrm{II}_{1}
and
\mathrm{II}_{2}
are defined as:

\mathrm{II}_{1}:=\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}})+\zeta_{1})}{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}-1\right|,\quad\mathrm{II}_{2}:=\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}})-\zeta_{1})}{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}-1\right|.

The above two terms can be bounded similarly. Take
\mathrm{II}_{1}
as an example, we use similar strategy as in Proposition
C.1
. Consider the event
S:=\{\Delta_{\infty}\leq\delta\}
where
\delta
satisfies
\frac{1}{\delta}\sqrt{\frac{s\log d_{2}}{n}}=O(1)
, we apply Lemma
C.3
and bound
\mathrm{II}_{1}
by

\frac{1}{1-\pi(\delta)}\cdot\mathrm{II}_{11}+\mathrm{II}_{12}+\frac{\mathbb{P}\left({\Delta_{\infty}>\delta}\right)}{\alpha},

where
\mathrm{II}_{11}
and
\mathrm{II}_{11}
are defined as

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}_{11}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1-\pi(\delta)}{\alpha}\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}})+\zeta_{1}\Big)-\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}))\Big|,

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}_{12}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}})\Big)-\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))\Big|,

(A.52)

where
\pi(\Delta_{\infty})=[A(\Delta_{\infty})+1]e^{M_{1}(\log d)^{3/2}A(\Delta_{\infty})}-1
.
By applying the part 3 of Theorem 2.1 in
[
43
]
(with
r+\epsilon=q(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}})+\zeta_{1},r-\epsilon=q(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}})
) to the Gaussian random vector
\bm{Z}
, we have

\mathrm{II}_{11}\leq K_{4}\zeta_{1}\big(q(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}})+{\zeta_{1}}/{2}\big)\leq C\zeta_{1}\log d_{2}.

(A.53)

where the second inequality holds due to the similar reason stated in the proof of Proposition
C.2
. And the term
\mathrm{II}_{11}
can be simply derived as

\mathrm{II}_{12}=\frac{1}{\alpha}\Big|\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)}-\alpha\Big|=\frac{\pi(\delta)}{1-\pi(\delta)}.

(A.54)

Combing the results above, we have

\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(T>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}}))}{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}-1\right|\leq C^{\prime}\zeta_{1}\log d_{2}+\frac{\pi(\delta)}{1-\pi(\delta)}+\frac{\pi(\delta)}{1+\pi(\delta)}+\frac{2\mathbb{P}\left({\Delta_{\infty}>\delta}\right)}{\alpha}+\frac{2\zeta_{2}}{\alpha}.

Applying the bound in Lemma
A.8
, we finally establish (
A.51
) i.e.,
\eta_{0}(d_{1},d_{2},n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L}):=\zeta_{1}\log d_{2}+(\log d_{2})^{5/2}\delta^{1/2}+\frac{\eta+\zeta_{2}}{\alpha_{L}}
up to some constant factor, where
\eta=e^{-c_{1}n}+\frac{1}{d_{2}}+\frac{1}{n\delta^{2}}
.
∎

A.5
Proof of Theorem
6.3

In this section, we present the proof of our FDR control result for general graphical models, i.e., Theorem
6.3
.
Recall that the generic estimator
\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}
is approximated by general mean zero random vectors
\bm{Y}_{i}(e),i\in[n]
, i.e.,

\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}_{e}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}(e)=o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).

We estimate the quantile of
T_{E}
by some Gaussian multiplier bootstrap statistic, i.e.,

\widehat{c}(\alpha,E)=\inf\left\{t\in\mathbb{R}:\mathbb{P}_{\xi}\left(T^{\mathcal{B}}_{E}\leq t\right)\geq 1-\alpha\right\},

where
T^{\mathcal{B}}_{E}
can be approximated by
T_{0E}^{\mathcal{B}}=\max_{e\in E}|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}(e)\xi_{i}|
.

First note we have a general version of Lemma
A.2
i.e., Lemma
A.10
. Then we follow the same derivations as in the proof of Theorem
5.2
(specifically from the beginning to (
A.15
)) and reduce the FDR control problem to proving: for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&~\underbrace{\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathrm{Var}\left({I_{j}(\alpha_{m})-d_{0}\alpha_{m}}\right)}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}}_{\mathrm{III}_{1}}+\underbrace{\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\left(\mathbb{E}\left[{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}I_{j}(\alpha_{m})-d_{0}\alpha_{m}}\right]\right)^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}}_{\mathrm{III}_{2}}\\
&~~~~~+\underbrace{\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}}\mathrm{Cov}\left({I_{j_{1}}(\alpha_{m})},{I_{j_{2}}(\alpha_{m})}\right)}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}}_{\mathrm{III}_{3}}\rightarrow 0.\end{split}

(A.55)

By Lemma
A.13
and Lemma
A.14
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{1}+\mathrm{III}_{2}+\mathrm{III}_{3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C^{\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\epsilon^{2}h_{d}}\left(\frac{d}{d_{0}|\mathcal{B}|}+\eta^{2}\right)+\frac{C^{\prime\prime\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\epsilon^{2}\rho h_{d}}\cdot\left(\eta+\frac{|S|\log d}{d_{0}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}t_{\lambda_{d}}\eta^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}h_{d}}+\frac{C_{2}}{\epsilon^{2}\rho d_{0}}\cdot\frac{t_{\lambda_{d}}}{h_{d}}\cdot\left(1+\eta d_{0}+\frac{|S|\log d}{d_{0}}\right),

where
\eta:=\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})=O\big(\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}+\frac{(\log d)^{11/6}}{n^{1/6}\alpha_{L}^{1/3}}+\zeta_{1}\log d+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{\alpha_{L}}\big)
as defined in Lemma
A.13
, with
\zeta_{1}
and
\zeta_{2}
being the general terms defined in Assumption
6.1
and
\alpha_{L}=q|\mathcal{B}|/d=\Omega(\rho)
.

Recall that
t_{\lambda_{d}}=q(\alpha_{L};T^{B}_{N_{0j}})=O\left(\sqrt{\log d}\right)
with probability growing to 1 and
t_{\lambda_{d}}/h_{d}=O(\log d)
.
Under Assumption
6.2
,
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\log d}{\rho}\left(\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}+\frac{(\log d)^{11/6}}{\rho^{1/3}n^{1/6}}+\zeta_{1}\log d+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{\rho}\right)=o(1),\quad\frac{\log d}{\rho d_{0}}+\frac{({\log d})^{2}|S|}{\rho d_{0}^{2}}=o(1),

and thus
\mathrm{III}_{1}+\mathrm{III}_{2}+\mathrm{III}_{3}=o(1)
with probability growing to 1.
Therefore, we have proved
(
A.55
)
, and finally establish the FDP control result below,

\text{FDP}(\widehat{\alpha})\leq q\frac{d_{0}}{d}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

FDR control can be similarly established as in the proof of Theorem
5.2
.
∎

A.6
Ancillary lemmas for Theorem
6.3

Lemma A.10
.

Suppose Assumptions
6.1
and
6.2
hold. Given some
1\leq j\leq d
, we have the following results.

(a)

Additionally, suppose for any
|\bm{\Theta}_{jk}|>0
, we also have
|\bm{\Theta}_{jk}|\geq c\sqrt{\log d/n}
for some constant
c>0
. Under the alternative hypothesis
H_{1j}:\|\bm{\Theta}_{j,-j}\|_{0}\geq k_{\tau}
, we then have for any
\alpha\in(0,1)
,

\lim_{(n,d)\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(\psi_{j,\alpha}=1)=1.

(b)

Under the null hypothesis
H_{0j}:\|\bm{\Theta}_{j,-j}\|_{0}<k_{\tau}
, we have for any
u\in(0,1)
,

\lim_{(n,d)\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\left({\psi_{j,\alpha}=1}\right)\leq\alpha.

Following the same derivations as in the proof of Lemma
A.2
(specifically from the beginning to (
D.2
)), we can reduce the proof of (a) to

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\min_{e\in N_{0j}^{c}}|\bm{\Theta}_{e}|>\frac{\widehat{c}(\alpha,E_{0})}{\sqrt{n}}+C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}\text{ and }||\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}-\bm{\Theta}||_{\max}\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}}\right)>1-3/d^{2}.

(A.56)

for some constant
C_{0}>0
, where
N_{0j}^{c}=\{(j,k):|\bm{\Theta}_{jk}|>0\}
and
E_{0}=\{(j,k):k\neq j,k\in[d]\}
. For any fixed
\alpha\in(0,1)
, we consider sufficiently large
d
such that
1/d\leq\alpha
. Then due to (
A.58
) in Lemma
A.11
and the definition
\widehat{c}(\alpha,E)=\inf\left\{t\in\mathbb{R}:\mathbb{P}_{\xi}\left(T^{\mathcal{B}}_{E}\leq t\right)\geq 1-\alpha\right\}
, we have
\widehat{c}(\alpha,E_{0})\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
for some constant
C_{0}>0
, with probability greater than
1-1/d^{2}
. Choosing the constant in the signal strength condition of Lemma
A.10
to be
2C_{0}
, we have with probability greater than
1-1/d^{2}

\displaystyle\min_{e\in N_{0j}^{c}}|\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{e}|\geq 2C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}\geq\frac{\widehat{c}(\alpha,E_{0})}{\sqrt{n}}+C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}

Then applying (
A.57
) in Lemma
A.11
, we have (
A.56
) holds, thus proving (a). Regarding (b), it can be proved by following the same derivations of the second part of Lemma
A.2
and noting the accuracy results of quantile approximation i.e., Lemma
A.12
, thus we omit the details.
∎

Lemma A.11
.

Under Assumptions
6.1
and
6.2
, we have

\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{e\in E_{\mathcal{V}}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}_{e}|>C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\Big)<\frac{2}{d^{2}},

(A.57)

\max_{j\in[d]}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(T^{\mathcal{B}}_{E_{j}}\geq 2C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\geq 2/d\right)\leq 2/d^{2},

(A.58)

for some constant
C_{0}>0
, where
E_{\mathcal{V}}=\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{V}
and
E_{j}=\{(j,k):k\neq j,k\in[d]\}
.

To prove (
A.57
), we first notice that

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{e\in E_{\mathcal{V}}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}_{e}|>2C\sqrt{\log d}\Big)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{e\in E_{\mathcal{V}}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}_{e}-n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}(e)+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}(e)|>2C\sqrt{\log d}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{e\in E_{\mathcal{V}}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}_{e}-n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}(e)|>C\sqrt{\log d}\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{e\in E_{\mathcal{V}}}|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}(e)|>C\sqrt{\log d}\Big)

\displaystyle:=~

\displaystyle~\mathrm{II}_{1}+\mathrm{II}_{2}

(A.59)

Regarding the term
\mathrm{II}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}_{1}\leq~

\displaystyle~\sum_{e\in E_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}_{e}-n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}(e)|>C\sqrt{\log d}\Big)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle~\sum_{e\in E_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathbb{P}\Big(|T_{e}-T_{0e}|>C\sqrt{\log d}\Big)\leq\sum_{e\in E_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathbb{P}\Big(|T_{e}-T_{0e}|>\zeta_{1}\Big)\leq\zeta_{2}d^{2}\leq 1/d^{2},

(A.60)

where the first inequality is by the union bound, the second to last inequalities hold due to Assumptions
6.1
and
6.2
and
|E_{\mathcal{V}}|=d^{2}
.
Regarding the term
\mathrm{II}_{2}
, we apply the maximal inequality (Lemma 2.2.2 in
[
81
]
)) and obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}_{2}=\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{e\in E_{\mathcal{V}}}\big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}(e)\big|>C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}\Big)\leq 1/d^{2}

(A.61)

under the tail condition (
\max_{e\in\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{V}}||\bm{Y}(e)||_{\psi_{1}}\leq C
) in Assumption
6.1
. Therefore, combining (
A.59
), (
A.60
) with (
A.61
) establishes (
A.57
).

Regarding (
A.58
), we notice that

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(T^{\mathcal{B}}_{E_{j}}\geq 2C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\geq 2/d\right)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(T^{\mathcal{B}}_{0E_{j}}+T^{\mathcal{B}}_{E_{j}}-T^{\mathcal{B}}_{0E_{j}}\geq 2C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\geq 2/d\right)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(T^{\mathcal{B}}_{0E_{j}}\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})+\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(|T^{\mathcal{B}}_{E_{j}}-T^{\mathcal{B}}_{0E_{j}}|\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\geq 2/d\right)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(T^{\mathcal{B}}_{0E_{j}}\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\geq 1/d\right)

\displaystyle~+\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(|T^{\mathcal{B}}_{E_{j}}-T^{\mathcal{B}}_{0E_{j}}|\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\geq 1/d\right)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(T^{\mathcal{B}}_{0E_{j}}\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\geq 1/d\right)+1/d^{2},

(A.62)

where the first and second inequalities hold due to the union bound, and the last inequality holds under Assumptions
6.1
and
6.2
. Now it suffices to prove

\max_{j\in[d]}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(T^{\mathcal{B}}_{0E_{j}}\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\geq 1/d\right)\leq 1/d^{2},

(A.63)

where
T_{0E_{j}}^{\mathcal{B}}=\max_{e\in E_{j}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}(e)\xi_{i}
. By applying the union bound, we derive the following inequality

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(T^{\mathcal{B}}_{0E_{j}}\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\geq 1/d\right)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{e\in E_{j}}\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(\big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}(e)\xi_{i}\big|\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})1/d\right).
