Then we follow the same techniques as in the proof of Lemma
D.1
, i.e., applying the union bound again and reduce the problem to proving

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(\big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}(e)\xi_{i}\big|\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\geq 1/d^{2}

(A.64)

with probability less than
1/d^{2}
for any
e\in E_{j}
. Conditional on
\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, we note
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}(e)\xi_{i}
are mean zero Gaussian random variables with the variance as
\mathrm{Var}\left({\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}(e)\xi_{i}}\,|\,{\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}}\right)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}^{2}_{i}(e)
, for any
e\in E_{j}
. Under the tail condition (
\max_{e\in\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{V}}||\bm{Y}^{2}(e)||_{\psi_{1}}\leq C\sqrt{n/\log d}
) in Assumption
6.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}^{2}_{i}(e)>C\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\bm{Y}^{2}_{i}(e)}{\sqrt{n/\log d}}>C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}\right)\leq 1/d^{2}.

Then by the Bernstein’s inequality (see e.g., Section 2.1 of
[
83
]
), we prove (
A.64
). Therefore, combining (
A.62
) with (
A.63
) yields (
A.58
).
∎

Lemma A.12
.

Under Assumptions
6.1
and
6.2
, we have the generic estimator
\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}
satisfies, for any edge set
E\subseteq\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{V}
and any given
\alpha\in(0,1)
,

\lim_{(n,d)\rightarrow\infty}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{e\in E}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}_{e}|>\widehat{c}(\alpha,E)\right)-\alpha\right|=0.

(A.65)

To prove Lemma
A.12
, we use the same strategy of proving Lemma
A.1
, that is to verify the three conditions in Corollary 3.1 of
[
17
]
:

(a)

\min_{e\in\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{E}[\bm{Y}^{2}(e)]>c
and
\max_{e\in\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{V}}||\bm{Y}(e)||_{\psi_{1}}\leq C
for some positive constants
c
and
C
and
(\log(dn))^{7}/n=o(1)
.

(b)

\mathbb{P}(|T_{E}-T_{0E}|>\zeta_{1})<\zeta_{2}
holds for some
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}>0
;

(c)

And
\mathbb{P}(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(|T_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}-T_{0E}^{\mathcal{B}}|>\zeta_{1}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})>\zeta_{2})<\zeta_{2}
holds for
\zeta_{1}\sqrt{\log d}+\zeta_{2}=o(1)
.

The above three condition holds under Assumptions
6.1
and
6.2
. Therefore, by Corollary 3.1 of
[
17
]
, we establish (
A.65
).

∎

Lemma A.13
.

Recalling the definitions of
\mathrm{III}_{1},\mathrm{III}_{2}
in (
A.55
), we have

\mathrm{III}_{1}+\mathrm{III}_{2}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\epsilon^{2}h_{d}}\left(\frac{1}{\rho d_{0}}+\eta^{2}(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})\right),

where
\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})=O\big(\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}+\frac{(\log d)^{11/6}}{n^{1/6}\alpha_{L}^{1/3}}+\zeta_{1}\log d+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{\alpha_{L}}\big)
with
\zeta_{1}
and
\zeta_{2}
being the general terms defined in Assumption
6.1
.

Note that the Cramér-type deviation results in Appendix C (i.e., Propositions C.1 and C.2) are general and do not rely on any particular distributional assumptions about the data. Hence we follow the proof of Lemma
A.3
to establish the following bounds

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{1}+\mathrm{III}_{2}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\epsilon^{2}h_{d}}\left(\frac{1}{\rho d_{0}}+\eta^{2}(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})\right)

for some constant
C^{\prime}
, where
\alpha_{L}=q|\mathcal{B}|/d
as defined similarly in the proof of Theorem
5.2
and
\rho=|\mathcal{B}|/d
. The only difference between this lemma and Lemma
A.3
is that
\zeta_{1}
and
\zeta_{2}
are not derived for a specific model but defined as general terms in Assumption
6.1
.

∎

Lemma A.14
.

Recalling the definition of
\mathrm{III}_{3}
in (
A.55
), we have

\mathrm{III}_{3}\leq\frac{C^{\prime\prime\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\rho\epsilon^{2}h_{d}}\left(\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})+\frac{|S|\log d}{d^{2}_{0}}\right),

where
\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})=O\big(\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}+\frac{(\log d)^{11/6}}{n^{1/6}\alpha_{L}^{1/3}}+\zeta_{1}\log d+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{\alpha_{L}}\big)
with
\zeta_{1}
and
\zeta_{2}
being the general terms defined in Assumption
6.1
.

Note that
\mathrm{III}_{3}
in (
A.55
) equals

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{III}_{3}&=\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}}\mathrm{Cov}\left({I_{j_{1}}(\alpha_{m})},{I_{j_{2}}(\alpha_{m})}\right)}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}},\\
&\quad\text{ where $I_{j}(\alpha)=\mathds{1}{(T_{N_{0j}}=\max_{e\in N_{0j}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}_{e}|=\max_{e\in N_{0j}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{e}|\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j}))}$ }\end{split}

(A.66)

for
j\in\{j_{1},j_{2}\}
. To quantify the covariance between
I_{j_{1}}(\alpha_{m})
and
I_{j_{2}}(\alpha_{m})
for
j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}
, we define

W_{j}(\alpha)=\mathds{1}{(\max_{e\in N_{0j}}|Z_{e}|\geq c(\alpha,N_{0j}))},

(A.67)

where
(Z_{e})_{e\in E}
(with
E=N_{0j}
) is a Gaussian random vector and shares the same mean vector and covariance matrix as the term
(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}(e))_{e\in E}
in
T_{0E}
. Here
T_{0E}
(with
E=N_{0j}
) has the explicit form below

T_{0E}=\max_{e\in E}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}(e).

Following the same derivations as in the proof of Lemma, we define
\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}
as

\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}:=\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}}\mathrm{Cov}\left({W_{j_{1}}(\alpha_{m})},{W_{j_{2}}(\alpha_{m})}\right)}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}

(A.68)

then bound
\mathrm{III}_{3}-\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}
via bounding the term
\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{1}}(\alpha)I_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{1}}(\alpha)W_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]\right|
and the term
\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{1}}(\alpha)}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{1}}(\alpha)}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]\right|
. By Lemma
A.16
and applying the same strategy to the term
\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{1}}(\alpha)}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]
, we have up to some constant factor,

\displaystyle\alpha^{-2}(\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{1}}(\alpha)I_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{1}}(\alpha)W_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]\right|+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{1}}(\alpha)}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{1}}(\alpha)}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]\right|)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})}{\alpha}.

Thus we obtain

\left|\mathrm{III}_{3}-\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})}{\alpha_{m}}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\rho\epsilon^{2}h_{d}}\cdot\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L}).

where
\alpha_{L}=q|\mathcal{B}|/d
as defined similarly in the proof of Theorem
5.2
and
\rho=|\mathcal{B}|/d
.

Combining the above bound with Lemma
A.15
, immediately produces

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C^{\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\rho\epsilon^{2}h_{d}}\cdot\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})+\frac{C^{\prime\prime\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}|S|\log d}{\rho\epsilon^{2}d^{2}_{0}h_{d}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C^{\prime\prime\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\rho\epsilon^{2}h_{d}}\left(\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})+\frac{|S|\log d}{d^{2}_{0}}\right),

for some constant
C^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
∎

Lemma A.15
.

Recalling the term
\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}
from (
A.68
) in the proof of Lemma
A.14
, we have

\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}=\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}}\cov(W_{j_{1}}(\alpha_{m}),W_{j_{2}}(\alpha_{m}))}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}\leq\frac{C^{\prime\prime\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}|S|\log d}{\rho\epsilon^{2}d^{2}_{0}h_{d}}.

(A.69)

The proof of Lemma
A.15
is similar to the proof of Lemma
A.5
. The key strategy is to utilize the equivalent expression of
\cov(W_{j_{1}}(\alpha_{m}),W_{j_{2}}(\alpha_{m}))
(see (
A.25
)) and the Cramér-type Gaussian comparison bound with
\ell_{0}
norm. First, we define
(Z_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
to be jointly Gaussian such that this
(|N_{0j_{1}}|+|N_{0j_{2}}|)
-dimensional Gaussian random vector shares the same mean vector and covariance matrix as the term
(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}(e))_{e\in E}
with
E=N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}
, where
\bm{Y}_{i}(e),i\in[n]
are the general random vectors defined in Assumption
6.1
. Then we define
(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}},(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{2}}}
to be two Gaussian random vectors such that

(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}(Z_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}},~~(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{2}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}(Z_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{2}}}~~\text{ and }(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{2}}}.

Similarly as in the proof of Lemma
A.5
, we can quantify the difference between the covariance matrices of
(Z_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
and
(Z^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
as a block matrix, denoted as
\Delta
; see (
A.26
) for the expression. We have that the diagonal block entries of
\Delta
are all zero and the each of off-diagonal block entries equals to the covariance between
Z_{e_{1}}
and
Z_{e_{2}}
for
e_{1}\in N_{0j_{1}},e_{2}\in N_{0j_{2}}
. Thus we have,

\displaystyle~\frac{\left|\cov(W_{j_{1}}(\alpha_{m}),W_{j_{2}}(\alpha_{m}))\right|}{\alpha^{2}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\Big|\mathbb{P}(\max_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}|Z_{e}|\geq t)-\mathbb{P}(\max_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}|Z^{\prime}_{e}|\geq t)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{\alpha}\cdot C^{\prime\prime}\Delta_{0}\log d

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle~\frac{C^{\prime\prime}\log d}{\alpha}\sum_{e_{1}:=(j_{1},k_{1})\in N_{0j_{1}},e_{2}:=(j_{2},k_{2})\in N_{0j_{2}}}\mathds{1}{(\cov(\bm{Y}((j_{1},k_{1})),\bm{Y}((j_{2},k_{2})))\neq 0)}

(A.70)

where the first equality holds due to (
A.25
), the first inequality holds by applying the Cramér-type Gaussian comparison bound with
\ell_{0}
norm (i.e., Theorem
B.6
), and the last equality holds by the definition of
\Delta_{0}
and the construction of
(Z_{e})_{e\in N_{0j_{1}}\cup N_{0j_{2}}}
. Note that when applying the Cramér-type Gaussian comparison bounds, we do not impose any assumption about the connectivity of the associated graph of
\Delta
, hence deriving a bound of
O(\Delta_{0}\log d)
instead of
O(\frac{\Delta_{0}\log d}{\mathfrak{p}})
as in the proof of Lemma
A.5
.
Now we obtain the following bound for
\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}^{\prime}_{3}=~

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}}\cov(W_{j_{1}}(\alpha_{m}),W_{j_{2}}(\alpha_{m}))}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{C^{\prime\prime}\log d}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}\alpha_{m}}\Big)\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}}\sum_{(j_{1},k_{1})\in N_{0j_{1}},(j_{2},k_{2})\in N_{0j_{2}}}\mathds{1}{(\cov(\bm{Y}((j_{1},k_{1})),\bm{Y}((j_{2},k_{2})))\neq 0)}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{m=1}^{\lambda_{d}}\frac{1}{\alpha_{m}}\Big)\frac{C^{\prime\prime}|S|\log d}{\epsilon^{2}d_{0}^{2}}\leq\frac{C^{\prime\prime\prime}t_{\lambda_{d}}}{\rho h_{d}}\cdot\frac{|S|\log d}{\epsilon^{2}d^{2}_{0}},

where the first inequality holds due to (
A.70
), the first equality holds by the definition of
S
, and the last inequality holds due to the same derivations for
\mathrm{III}_{1}
in the proof of Lemma
A.3
. Therefore (
A.69
) is established.
∎

Lemma A.16
.

Recall the definitions of
I_{j}(\alpha)
and
W_{j}(\alpha)
in (
A.66
) and (
A.67
), for
j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},j_{1}\neq j_{2}
, when
\alpha\in[\alpha_{L},1]
, we have

{\big|\mathbb{E}\left[{I_{j_{1}}(\alpha)I_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{W_{j_{1}}(\alpha)W_{j_{2}}(\alpha)}\right]\big|}\leq{\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})}{\alpha}.

Lemma
A.16
is the same as Lemma
A.6
except that the terms
I_{j}(\alpha)
and
W_{j}(\alpha)
in (
A.66
) and (
A.67
) are defined in terms of general
T_{E},T_{0E},T_{E}^{\mathcal{B}},T_{0E}^{\mathcal{B}}
in Assumption
6.1
. The proof of Lemma
A.6
only relies on Assumption
6.1
and general results in Appendix C and
[
43
]
. Thus we can follow it to establish the above bounds for
I_{j}(\alpha)
and
W_{j}(\alpha)
(defined in (
A.66
) and (
A.67
)).
∎

B
Proofs of Cramér-type comparison bounds

In this section, we will prove two types of Cramér-type comparison bounds: Theorems
3.1
and
3.3
.
One of the challenges to derive the comparison bounds for Gaussian maxima is that the maximum function is non-smooth. In order to show the Cramér-type comparison bound, we first consider smooth approximation of the maximum. The following lemma from
[
8
]
show the existence of such smooth approximation.

Lemma B.1
(Theorem 1,
[
8
]
).

Consider the Euclidean space
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\ell_{\infty}
-norm, for any
t,\epsilon\geq 0
, there exists a smooth approximating function
\varphi_{r,\epsilon}
satisfying the following:

(a)

\varphi_{r,\epsilon}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[0,1],\varphi_{r,\epsilon}\in\mathbb{C}^{\infty}
, where
\mathbb{C}^{\infty}
is the smooth function class with functions differentiable for all degrees of differentiation.

(b)

\varphi_{r,\epsilon}(x)=1
if
||x||_{\infty}\leq r
,
\varphi_{r,\epsilon}(x)=0
if
||x||_{\infty}\geq r+\epsilon
,

(c)

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}||D^{j}\varphi_{r,\epsilon}(x)||_{1}\leq c(j)\epsilon^{-j}\log^{j-1}(d+1)
,

where
||D^{j}\varphi_{r,\epsilon}(x)||_{1}=\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{d}\cdots\sum_{i_{j}=1}^{d}\left|\frac{\partial^{j}\varphi_{r,\epsilon}(x)}{\partial x_{i_{1}}\cdots\partial x_{i_{j}}}\right|
and the constants
c(j)
only depends on
j
.

Remark B.2
.

[
43
]
gives a concrete example of
\varphi_{r,\epsilon}(x)
satisfying the three properties in Lemma
B.1
:

\varphi_{r,\epsilon}(x)=g_{0}\left(\frac{2(F_{\beta}(z_{x}-r\mathbf{1}_{2d})-\epsilon/2)}{\epsilon}\right),

(B.1)

where
\beta=2\log(2d)/\epsilon
,
g_{0}(t):=30\mathds{1}{(0\leq t\leq 1)}\int_{t}^{1}s^{2}(1-s)^{2}ds+\mathds{1}{(t\leq 0)}
,
F_{\beta}(\cdot)
is the “softmax” function

F_{\beta}(z):=\frac{1}{\beta}\log\Big(\sum_{m=1}^{2d}\exp\left(\beta z_{m}\right)\Big)\quad\text{ for }z\in\mathbb{R}^{2d},

z_{x}=(x^{\top},-x^{\top})^{\top}
, and
\mathbf{1}_{2d}
is the vector of
1
’s of dimension
2d
.

In fact, in the proof of Theorem
3.1
, we do not need a specific form of
\varphi_{r,\epsilon}(x)
and any function satisfying Lemma
B.1
will work. While in the proof of Theorem
3.3
, we need to utilize the specific form in (
B.1
).

B.1
Proof of Theorem
3.1

As mentioned in Remark
3.2
, we can prove the Cramér-type comparison bound with max norm difference as

M_{3}(\log d)^{3/2}A(\Delta_{\infty})e^{M_{3}(\log d)^{3/2}A(\Delta_{\infty})},

without the assumption on
\Delta_{\infty}
.
Therefore we state the more general form of Theorem
3.1
below and give its proof. Note that under the assumption
(\log d)^{5}\Delta_{\infty}=O(1)
and the discussions in Remark
3.2
, the bound (
3.1
) in Theorem
3.1
immediately follows from Theorem
B.3
.

Theorem B.3
(CCB with max norm difference).

Let
U
and
V
be two Gaussian random vectors and we have

\sup_{0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}}\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(||U||_{\infty}>t)}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}-1\right|\leq M_{3}(\log d)^{3/2}A(\Delta_{\infty})e^{M_{1}(\log d)^{3/2}A(\Delta_{\infty})},

(B.2)

where
C_{0}>0
is some constant,
A(\Delta_{\infty})=M_{1}\log d\Delta_{\infty}^{1/2}\exp{(M_{2}\log^{2}d\Delta_{\infty}^{1/2})}
, the constants
M_{1},M_{2}
only depend on
\min_{1\leq j\leq d}\{\sigma^{U}_{jj},\sigma^{V}_{jj}\},\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\{\sigma^{U}_{jj},\sigma^{V}_{jj}\}
, and
M_{3}
is a universal constant.

Using the smooth approximation in Lemma
B.1
, we can bound the difference between the distribution functions of Gaussian maxima as

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}(||U||_{\infty}>t)-\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)\big|

(B.3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}{(||U||_{\infty}\leq t)}-\mathds{1}{(||V||_{\infty}\leq t)}]\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(t-\epsilon\leq||V||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)+\max_{j=1,2}\left|\mathbb{E}\varphi_{j}(U)-\mathbb{E}\varphi_{j}(V)\right|,

where
\varphi_{1}(x):=\varphi_{t,\epsilon}(x),\varphi_{2}(x):=\varphi_{t-\epsilon,\epsilon}(x)
. Regarding the inequality in (
B.3
), we first notice that

\mathds{1}{(||x||_{\infty}\leq t)}=\varphi_{t,\epsilon}(x)-\mathds{1}{(t<||x||_{\infty}<t+\epsilon)}\cdot\varphi_{t,\epsilon}(x)=\varphi_{t-\epsilon,\epsilon}(x)-\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon<||x||_{\infty}<t)}\cdot\varphi_{t-\epsilon,\epsilon}(x),

where the first equality is due to property (b) in Lemma
B.1
. Hence we have

\displaystyle\mathds{1}{(||U||_{\infty}\leq t)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\varphi_{j}(U),\quad j=1,2

\displaystyle\mathds{1}{(||V||_{\infty}\leq t)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\varphi_{1}(V)-\mathds{1}{(t<\left\|V\right\|<t+\epsilon)},

\displaystyle\mathds{1}{(||V||_{\infty}\leq t)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\varphi_{2}(V)-\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon<\left\|V\right\|<t)},

then (
B.3
) immediately follows by combining the above three inequalities.

The first term in (
B.3
) is related to the anti-concentration inequalities for the Gaussian maxima. By applying Theorem 2.1 in
[
43
]
, we have

\mathbb{P}(t-\epsilon\leq||V||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)\leq K_{1}(t+1)\epsilon\exp(K_{2}(t+1)\epsilon)\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t).

(B.4)

The explicit forms of
K_{1},K_{2}
can be found in Theorem 2.1 of
[
43
]
. They only depend on
\min_{1\leq j\leq d}\{\sigma^{U}_{jj},\sigma^{V}_{jj}\},\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\{\sigma^{U}_{jj},\sigma^{V}_{jj}\}
and the median of Gaussian maxima.
Remark that the median of
||V||_{\infty}
is bounded by
O(\sqrt{\log d})
by the maximal inequalities for sub-Gaussian random variables (Lemma 5.2 in
[
82
]
). Plugging this into the explicit form of
K_{1},K_{2}
in Theorem 2.1 of
[
43
]
, we have
K_{1}=O(\log d),K_{2}=O({\log^{2}d})
. Then (
B.4
) can be written as

\mathbb{P}(t-\epsilon\leq||V||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)\leq M_{1}\log d(t+1)\epsilon\exp({M_{2}\log^{2}d}~(t+1)\epsilon)\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t),

for some constants
M_{1},M_{2}
only depending on
\min_{1\leq j\leq d}\{\sigma^{U}_{jj},\sigma^{V}_{jj}\},\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\{\sigma^{U}_{jj},\sigma^{V}_{jj}\}
.

Overall the above bound has only a logarithmic dependence on the dimension
d
, similar to the anti-concentration bounds from
[
18
]
. But it quantifies the deviation with respect to the tail probability of the Gaussian maxima, thus offers a more refined characterization, which is crucial to our proof.

Now we deal with the second term in (
B.3
). It is not hard to check that the following proof works for both
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}
. Therefore, without loss of generality, we use a unified notation
\varphi
to represent either functions. We consider the Slepian interpolation between
U
and
V
:
W(s):=\sqrt{s}U+\sqrt{1-s}V,~~s\in[0,1]
.
Let
\Psi_{t}(s)=\mathbb{E}[\varphi(W(s))]
, then we have

\left|\mathbb{E}\varphi(U)-\mathbb{E}\varphi(V)\right|=|\Psi_{t}(1)-\Psi_{t}(0)|=\left|\int_{0}^{1}\Psi_{t}^{\prime}(s)ds\right|,

(B.5)

where
\Psi_{t}^{\prime}(s)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{j}\varphi(W(s))(s^{-1/2}U_{j}-(1-s)^{-1/2}V_{j})]
. Applying Stein’s identity (Lemma 2 of
[
19
]
) to
(s^{-1/2}U_{j}-(1-s)^{-1/2}V_{j},W(s)^{\top})^{\top}
and
\partial_{j}\varphi(W(s))
, we have

\displaystyle\Psi_{t}^{\prime}(s)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j,k=1}^{d}(\sigma_{jk}^{U}-\sigma_{jk}^{V})\mathbb{E}[\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi(W(s))].

(B.6)

Hence we obtain the following bound on (
B.5
),

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{1}\Psi_{t}^{\prime}(s)ds\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j,k=1}^{d}|\sigma_{jk}^{U}-\sigma_{jk}^{V}|\cdot\left|\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi(W(s))]ds\right|

(B.7)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{\infty}}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\sum_{j,k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[\left|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi(W(s))\right|]ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{\infty}}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\sum_{j,k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi(W(s))|\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||W(s)||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}]ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{\infty}}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}||D^{2}\varphi(x)||_{1}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||W(s)||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon))}]ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c(2)\Delta_{\infty}\log(d+1)}{2\epsilon^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{P}(t-\epsilon\leq||W(s)||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)ds

where the second inequality is by the definition of
\Delta_{\infty}
and the third one comes from the property (b) in Lemma
B.1
for
\varphi_{j}(x),j=1,2
(recalling
\varphi_{1}(x)=\varphi_{t,\epsilon}(x)
and
\varphi_{2}(x)=\varphi_{t-\epsilon,\epsilon}(x)
). Note that property (c) gives a upper bound for the partial derivative terms. Thus the fourth inequality holds.

By the definition of Slepian interpolation, we have, for any
s\in[0,1]
,
W(s)
is a Gaussian random vector and the variances can be controlled between
\min_{1\leq j\leq d}\{\sigma^{U}_{jj},\sigma^{V}_{jj}\}
and
\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\{\sigma^{U}_{jj},\sigma^{V}_{jj}\}
.
The median of
||W(s)||_{\infty}
can also be similarly bounded by
O(\sqrt{\log d})
as
||V||_{\infty}
. Applying the anti-concentration inequalities again to
W(s)
in (
B.7
), we thus obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{1}\Psi_{t}^{\prime}(s)ds\right|\leq\frac{c(2)\Delta_{\infty}\log(d+1)}{2\epsilon^{2}}\cdot M_{1}\log d(t+1)\epsilon\exp(M_{2}\log^{2}d(t+1)\epsilon)\cdot\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{P}(||W(s)||_{\infty}>t)ds.

(B.8)

Let
Q_{t}(u)=\mathbb{P}(||W(u)||_{\infty}>t)
and
R_{t}(u)=Q_{t}(u)/Q_{t}(0)-1
. Combining (
B.3
), (
B.4
), (
B.5
) and (
B.8
), we have

\displaystyle|Q_{t}(1)-Q_{t}(0)|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}(||U||_{\infty}>t)-\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)\big|

(B.9)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{1}\log d(t+1)\epsilon\exp(M_{2}\log^{2}d(t+1)\epsilon)Q_{t}(0)

\displaystyle+~\frac{c(2)\Delta_{\infty}\log(d+1)}{2\epsilon^{2}}M_{1}\log d(t+1)\epsilon\exp(M_{2}\log^{2}d(t+1)\epsilon)\int_{0}^{1}Q_{t}(s)ds.

If starting with the interpolation between
W(s)
and
V
instead of that between
U
and
V
, we can similarly obtain the bound on
|Q_{t}(s)-Q_{t}(0)|
. And the integral
\int_{0}^{1}Q_{t}(s)ds
in (
B.9
) can be directly replaced by
\int_{0}^{u}Q_{t}(s)ds
. Namely, we have

\frac{|Q_{t}(u)-Q_{t}(0)|}{|Q_{t}(0)|}=|R_{t}(u)|\leq A(t,\epsilon)B(\Delta_{\infty},\epsilon)\int_{0}^{u}|R_{t}(s)|ds+A(t,\epsilon)B(\Delta_{\infty},\epsilon)\cdot u+A(t,\epsilon),

(B.10)

where we denote
A(t,\epsilon)=M_{1}\log d(t+1)\epsilon\exp(M_{2}\log^{2}d(t+1)\epsilon)
and
B(\Delta_{\infty},\epsilon)=\frac{c(2)\Delta_{\infty}\log(d+1)}{2\epsilon^{2}}
.

Notice that (
B.10
) is an integral inequality and we can thus bound
R_{t}(s)
by Grönwall’s inequality
[
29
]

|R_{t}(u)|\leq(A(t,\epsilon)B(\Delta_{\infty},\epsilon)u+A(t,\epsilon))e^{A(t,\epsilon)B(\Delta_{\infty},\epsilon)u}.

In particular, we have
|R_{t}(1)|\leq(A(t,\epsilon)B(\Delta_{\infty},\epsilon)+A(t,\epsilon))e^{A(t,\epsilon)B(\Delta_{\infty},\epsilon)}
. Remember that
\epsilon
is the smoothing parameter that controls the level of approximation. Choosing
\epsilon=\Delta_{\infty}^{1/2}/(t+1)
, we then have
A(\Delta_{\infty}):=A(t,\epsilon)=M_{1}\log d\Delta_{\infty}^{1/2}\exp{(M_{2}\log^{2}d\Delta_{\infty}^{1/2})}
for some constants
M_{1},M_{2}
only depending on
\min_{1\leq j\leq d}\{\sigma^{U}_{jj},\sigma^{V}_{jj}\},\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\{\sigma^{U}_{jj},\sigma^{V}_{jj}\}
and
B(t):=B(\Delta_{\infty},\epsilon)=\frac{c(2)\log(d+1)(t+1)^{2}}{2}
. When
0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
, we have
B(t)\leq M_{1}(\log d)^{3/2}
for some universal constant
M_{3}
. Therefore the bound in (
B.2
) is established, i.e.,

\sup_{0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}}|R_{t}(1)|\leq M_{3}(\log d)^{3/2}A(\Delta_{\infty})e^{M_{3}(\log d)^{3/2}A(\Delta_{\infty})}.

∎

B.2
Proof of Theorem
3.3

Before proving Theorem
3.3
, we note its assumption about the connectivity can be relaxed. Therefore, we first present Theorem
B.6
with a weaker connectivity assumption,
which is stated below.

Assumption B.4

(
\mathfrak{p}
-connectivity property)
.

We say two Gaussian random vectors
U
and
V
satisfy the
\mathfrak{p}
-connectivity property if for any
j
such that
\sigma^{U}_{jk}\neq\sigma^{V}_{jk}
for some
k
, there exists a subset
\mathcal{E}_{0}\subset[d]
satisfying the following three requirements:

(a)

j\in\mathcal{E}_{0},|\mathcal{E}_{0}|=\mathfrak{p}+1
;

(b)

When
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}_{0}
and
m\neq m^{\prime}
,
\sigma^{U}_{mm}=\sigma^{U}_{m^{\prime}m^{\prime}}
and
\sigma^{U}_{mm^{\prime}}=\sigma^{V}_{mm^{\prime}}=0
hold;

(c)

\forall~k\in[d]
,
|\{m\in\mathcal{E}_{0}:|\sigma^{U}_{km}|+|\sigma^{V}_{km}|\neq 0\}|\leq c_{0}
for some constant
c_{0}
.

This assumption gives a characterization of the connectivity of the associated graphs of the Gaussian random vectors
U
and
V
. Below we give a few sufficient conditions (SC) for it.

SC1

U
and
V
have unit variances. There exists a disjoint
(\mathfrak{p}+2)
-partition of nodes
\cup_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{p}+2}\mathcal{C}_{\ell}=[d]
such that
\sigma^{U}_{jk}=\sigma^{V}_{jk}=0
when
j\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell}
and
k\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell^{\prime}}
for some
\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}
.

SC2

U
and
V
have unit variances. There exist disjoint partitions of nodes
\cup_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{p}+2}\mathcal{C}^{U}_{\ell}=\cup_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{p}+2}\mathcal{C}^{V}_{\ell}=[d],
such that
\sigma^{U}_{jk}
(
\sigma^{V}_{jk}
) equals
0
when
j,k
belong to different elements
\mathcal{C}^{U}_{\ell}
(
\mathcal{C}^{V}_{\ell}
), and
\forall\ell\in[\mathfrak{p}+2]
,
\mathcal{C}^{U}_{\ell}\cap\mathcal{C}^{V}_{\ell}\neq\emptyset
.

SC3

\forall~s\in[0,1]
, the Gaussian random vector
W(s):=\sqrt{s}U+\sqrt{1-s}V
always has the same variances
\sigma_{s}^{2}
across different components. The associated graph of
W(s)
has at least
\mathfrak{p}+2
components, i.e., there exists a disjoint partition of nodes
\cup_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{p}+2}\mathcal{C}^{W}_{\ell}=[d]
, such that each
\mathcal{C}^{W}_{\ell}
comes from a different component. And the partition
\cup_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{p}+2}\mathcal{C}^{W}_{\ell}=[d]
works any
s\in[0,1]
.

Remark B.5
.

Note that the above first condition SC1 is the main assumption of Theorem
3.3
(except that
\mathfrak{p}+2
is replaced by
\mathfrak{p}
). It is immediate that the condition SC1 implies SC2. We will verify SC2 is indeed a sufficient condition of Assumption
B.4
in the following paragraph.
Regarding SC3, its sufficiency can be verified similarly, thus we omit the details.

Simply, we have
\sigma^{U}_{jj}=\sigma^{V}_{jj}=1,j\in[d]
by the unit variance assumption. For any
j
such that
\sigma^{U}_{jk}\neq\sigma^{V}_{jk}
for some
k
, we will construct a subset
\mathcal{E}_{0}
and show it satisfies the three requirements (a), (b) and (c).
Note that the condition SC1 assumes the existence of disjoint partitions of nodes
\cup_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{p}+2}\mathcal{C}^{U}_{\ell}=\cup_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{p}+2}\mathcal{C}^{V}_{\ell}=[d]
. We suppose
j\in\mathcal{C}^{U}_{\ell_{1}}\cap\mathcal{C}^{V}_{\ell_{2}}
for some
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}
, then
\mathcal{E}_{0}
is constructed by including
j
and picking one element
m_{\ell}
from
\mathcal{C}^{U}_{\ell}\cap\mathcal{C}^{V}_{\ell}
for each
\ell\in[\mathfrak{p}+2]\setminus\{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\}
. As
\mathcal{C}^{U}_{\ell}\cap\mathcal{C}^{V}_{\ell}\neq\emptyset,\forall\ell\in[\mathfrak{p}+2]
, we have
|\mathcal{E}_{0}|\geq 1+\mathfrak{p}
, hence the requirement (a) is satisfied. Regarding the requirement (b), when
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}_{0},m\neq m^{\prime}
, we immediately have
\sigma^{U}_{mm}=\sigma^{V}_{m^{\prime}m^{\prime}}=1
by the unit variance assumption. Since every element in
\mathcal{E}_{0}
comes from a different component
\mathcal{C}^{U}_{\ell}
(
\mathcal{C}^{V}_{\ell}
), we also have
\sigma^{U}_{mm^{\prime}}=\sigma^{V}_{mm^{\prime}}=0
when
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}_{0},m\neq m^{\prime}
. Lastly, due to the same reason, we have
\forall k\in[d]
,
|\{m\in\mathcal{E}_{0}:|\sigma^{U}_{km}|+|\sigma^{V}_{km}|\neq 0\}|\leq 2
. Hence the requirement (c) is also satisfied.

Now we prove Theorem
B.6
, which is stated below. Note that it requires weaker connectivity assumption compared with Theorem
3.3
but needs to assume minimal eigenvalue conditions.

Theorem B.6
(CCB with elementwise
\ell_{0}
norm difference).

Consider the two Gaussian random vectors
U
and
V
to have equal variances
\sigma^{U}_{jj}=\sigma^{V}_{jj}=O(1)
, for
j\in[d]
and we assume
\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}^{U})\geq 1/b_{0}>0,\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}^{V})\geq 1/b_{0}>0
for some constant
b_{0}>0
. Suppose
U
and
V
also satisfy Assumption
B.4
, we then have

\sup_{0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}}\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(||U||_{\infty}>t)}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}-1\right|=O\left(\frac{\Delta_{0}\log d}{\mathfrak{p}}\right).

(B.11)

for some constant
C_{0}>0
.

Following the same derivations as in Theorem
B.3
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}(||U||_{\infty}>t)-\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)\right|

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle M_{1}\log d(t+1)\epsilon\exp(M_{2}\log^{2}d(t+1)\epsilon)\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)+\max_{j=1,2}\left|\mathbb{E}[\varphi_{j}(U)]-\mathbb{E}[\varphi_{j}(V)]\right|

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle M_{1}\log d(t+1)\epsilon\exp(M_{2}\log^{2}d(t+1)\epsilon)\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)+\left|\int_{0}^{1}\Psi_{t}^{\prime}(s)ds\right|,

(B.12)

where
\epsilon={c}/{\max\{(\log d)^{3/2},\mathfrak{p}\log d\}}
for some small enough constant
c>0
, and the constants
M_{1},M_{2}
only depend on
\min_{1\leq j\leq d}\{\sigma^{U}_{jj}\}
,
\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\{\sigma^{U}_{jj}\}
.
The above two inequalities hold by (
B.3
), (
B.4
) and (
B.5
). We further bound
\left|\int_{0}^{1}\Psi_{t}^{\prime}(s)ds\right|
as below,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{1}\Psi_{t}^{\prime}(s)ds\right|

(B.13)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j,k=1}^{d}|\sigma_{jk}^{U}-\sigma_{jk}^{V}|\left|\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi(W(s))]ds\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M}{2}\sum_{j\neq k,\sigma^{U}_{jk}\neq\sigma^{V}_{jk}}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}[|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi(W(s))|]ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M}{2}\sum_{j\neq k,\sigma^{U}_{jk}\neq\sigma^{V}_{jk}}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}[|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi(W(s))|\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||W(s)||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}]ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M\Delta_{0}}{2}\max_{j\neq k,\sigma^{U}_{jk}\neq\sigma^{V}_{jk}}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}[|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi(W(s))|\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||W(s)||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}]ds,

where the first inequality holds due to (
B.6
), the second inequality is because
\sigma_{jk}^{U}=O(1),\sigma_{jk}^{V}=O(1)
for all
j,k
and the constant
M
only depends on the maximal variances of the elements of
U,V
, the third inequality holds by the property (b) in Lemma
B.1
for
\varphi_{j}(x),j=1,2
, and the last inequality holds by the definition of
\Delta_{0}
. Note that
\varphi_{1}(x):=\varphi_{t,\epsilon}(x),\varphi_{2}(x):=\varphi_{t-\epsilon,\epsilon}(x)
as defined in the proof of Theorem
B.3
. We use the same strategy to deal with
\varphi_{1}(x)
and
\varphi_{2}(x)
. Below we give the derivations when
\varphi=\varphi_{1}(x)
and it is not hard to check these derivations work for
\varphi_{2}(x)
as well. Recall the explicit construction of
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
introduced in Remark
B.2
,

\varphi(x)=\varphi_{r,\epsilon}(x)=g_{0}\left(\frac{2(F_{\beta}(z_{x}-r\mathbf{1}_{2d})-\epsilon/2)}{\epsilon}\right),

where
\beta=2\log(2d)/\epsilon
,
g_{0}(t):=30\mathds{1}{(0\leq t\leq 1)}\int_{t}^{1}s^{2}(1-s)^{2}ds+\mathds{1}{(t\leq 0)}
,
F_{\beta}
is the “softmax” function

F_{\beta}(z):=\frac{1}{\beta}\log\Big(\sum_{m=1}^{2d}\exp\left(\beta z_{m}\right)\Big)\quad\text{ for }z\in\mathbb{R}^{2d},

z_{x}=(x^{\top},-x^{\top})^{\top}
and
\mathbf{1}_{2d}
is the vector of
1
’s of dimension
2d
.

To bound (
B.13
), we consider the case where
j\neq k
and
\sigma^{U}_{jk}\neq\sigma^{V}_{jk}
. Note that

|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi(W(s))|\leq||g^{\prime\prime}||_{\infty}|\widetilde{\pi}_{j}(Z)\widetilde{\pi}_{k}(Z)|+\beta||g^{\prime}||_{\infty}|\widetilde{\pi}_{j}(Z)\widetilde{\pi}_{k}(Z)|,

(B.14)

where
g(t):=g_{0}(\frac{2(t-\epsilon/2)}{\epsilon})
,
Z:=W(s)
and

\widetilde{\pi}_{j}(z):=\frac{e^{\beta z_{j}}-e^{-\beta z_{j}}}{\sum_{m=1}^{d}e^{\beta z_{m}}+\sum_{m=1}^{d}e^{-\beta z_{m}}}.

The above result follows from a direct calculation. Due to the boundedness of
||g_{0}^{\prime}||_{\infty},||g_{0}^{\prime\prime}||_{\infty}
and
\beta={2\log(2d)}/{\epsilon}
, we obtain the following bound on (
B.14
),

\displaystyle|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi(W(s))|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(||g^{\prime\prime}||_{\infty}+\beta||g^{\prime}||_{\infty})|\widetilde{\pi}_{j}(Z)\widetilde{\pi}_{k}(Z)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{4}{\epsilon^{2}}||g^{\prime\prime}_{0}||_{\infty}+\frac{2\beta}{\epsilon}||g_{0}^{\prime}||_{\infty}\Big)|\widetilde{\pi}_{j}(Z)\widetilde{\pi}_{k}(Z)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}\log(2d)}{\epsilon^{2}}|\widetilde{\pi}_{j}(Z)\widetilde{\pi}_{k}(Z)|\leq\frac{C_{1}\log(2d)}{\epsilon^{2}}|{\pi}_{j}(Z){\pi}_{k}(Z)|,

for some constant
C_{1}
, where
\pi_{j}(z)={e^{\beta|z_{j}|}}/{\sum_{m=1}^{d}e^{\beta|z_{m}|}}
.
Recalling
Z=W(s)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\left[{|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi(W(s))|\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||W(s)||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right]ds

(B.15)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}\log(2d)}{\epsilon^{2}}{\int_{0}^{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[{\pi_{j}(Z)\pi_{k}(Z)\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||Z||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right]}ds}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}\log(2d)}{\epsilon^{2}}\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)\int_{0}^{1}\underbrace{\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\pi_{j}(Z)\pi_{k}(Z)\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||Z||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right]}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}}_{\rm{II}(s)}ds.

Below we focus on bounding the term
\rm{II(s)}
for any
s\in[0,1]
. First we rewrite
\pi_{j}(Z)\pi_{k}(Z)
and simply derive the following inequality,

\displaystyle\pi_{j}(Z)\pi_{k}(Z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\beta|Z_{j}|}}{\sum_{m=1}^{d}e^{\beta|Z_{m}|}}\cdot\frac{e^{\beta|Z_{k}|}}{\sum_{m=1}^{d}e^{\beta|Z_{m}|}}

(B.16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-\beta(||Z||_{\infty}-|Z_{j}|)}\cdot e^{-\beta(||Z||_{\infty}-|Z_{k}|)}}{(1+\sum_{|Z_{m}|\neq||Z||_{\infty}}e^{-\beta(||Z||_{\infty}-|Z_{m}|)})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{-\beta(||Z||_{\infty}-|Z_{j}|)}\cdot e^{-\beta(||Z||_{\infty}-|Z_{k}|)},

where the second equality comes from dividing both the numerator and denominator by
e^{2\beta||Z||_{\infty}}
in the first line. Note that
\mathbb{P}(|Z_{j}|=|Z_{k}|)=0
since the random vector
Z
follows a non-degenerate
d
-dimensional multivariate Gaussian distribution. Hence we have

1=\mathds{1}{(|Z_{j}|=||Z||_{\infty},|Z_{k}|<||Z||_{\infty})}+\mathds{1}{(|Z_{j}|<||Z||_{\infty})},\text{ almost surely}.

(B.17)

Plugging the equality (
B.17
) into (
B.16
), we can further bound
\pi_{j}(Z)\pi_{k}(Z)
as

\pi_{j}(Z)\pi_{k}(Z)\leq e^{-\beta(||Z||_{\infty}-|Z_{k}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(|Z_{k}|<||Z||_{\infty})}+e^{-\beta(||Z||_{\infty}-|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(|Z_{j}|<||Z||_{\infty})},\text{ almost surely}.

Then we can bound
\mathrm{II}(s)
by

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}(s)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\pi_{j}(Z)\pi_{k}(Z)\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||Z||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right]}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{~\mathbb{E}[e^{-\beta(||Z||_{\infty}-|Z_{k}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(|Z_{k}|<||Z||_{\infty})}\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||Z||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}]}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}

(B.18)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{~\mathbb{E}[e^{-\beta(||Z||_{\infty}-|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(|Z_{j}|<||Z||_{\infty})}\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||Z||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}]}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}.

(B.19)

We use the same strategy to bound (
B.18
) and (
B.19
). Below we give the derivations for bounding (
B.19
) and note these also work for (
B.18
).

For any
j\neq k
and
\sigma^{U}_{jk}\neq\sigma^{V}_{jk}
, Assumption
B.4
says that there exists a subset
\mathcal{E}_{0}\subset[d]
satisfying
j\in\mathcal{E}_{0},|\mathcal{E}_{0}|=\mathfrak{p}+1
, and
\sigma^{U}_{mm}=\sigma^{U}_{m^{\prime}m^{\prime}}
,
\sigma^{U}_{mm^{\prime}}=\sigma^{V}_{mm^{\prime}}=0
when
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}_{0},m\neq m^{\prime}
. This implies the following: when
s=0
or
1
(i.e.,
Z=U
or
V
), we can find a
\mathfrak{p}-
dimensional random vector
G
such that
(Z_{j},G)
are all independent and
\var(G_{\ell})=\var(Z_{j})=\sigma_{j}^{2}
for
\ell\in[\mathfrak{p}]
. Note that
G
is constructed as
(Z_{m})_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{0},m\neq j}
with
\mathcal{E}_{0}
being the same for
Z=U
and
V
. Therefore, for any
s\in(0,1),Z=W(s)=\sqrt{s}U+\sqrt{1-s}V
, we can construct
G=(Z_{m})_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{0},m\neq j}
such that
(Z_{j},G)
are all independent and
\var(G_{\ell})=\var(Z_{j})=\sigma_{j}^{2}
for
\ell\in[\mathfrak{p}]
. Throughout the following proof and the lemmas in Appendix
B.3
, we will use the notation
Z,G
without making the dependence on
s
explicitly. And we
denote the indices of the random variables in
G
(among
Z
) by
\mathcal{E}_{G}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{E}_{G}=\mathcal{E}_{0}\setminus\{j\}=\{m\in[d]:Z_{m}=G_{\ell}\text{ for some }\ell\in[\mathfrak{p}]\}
.

We will consider two separate cases based on whether
||G||_{\infty}=||Z||_{\infty}
holds. Formally, we write
\mathds{1}{(|Z_{j}|<||Z||_{\infty})}\leq\mathds{1}{(E_{1})}+\mathds{1}{(E_{2})}

with
{E_{1}}
and
{E_{2}}
defined as

\displaystyle E_{1}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{||Z||_{\infty}>||G||_{\infty},||Z||_{\infty}>|Z_{j}|\},

(B.20)

\displaystyle{E_{2}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{||G||_{\infty}=||Z||_{\infty}>|Z_{j}|\}.

(B.21)

Then the numerator of the fraction in (
B.19
) can be bounded by the summation of the following two terms:

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{-\beta(||Z||_{\infty}-|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(E_{1})}\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||Z||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right],

(B.22)

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{-\beta(||Z||_{\infty}-|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(E_{2})}\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||Z||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right].

Combining (
B.19
) with (
B.22
) and applying Lemmas
B.7
and
B.8
, we have

\mathrm{II}(s)\leq\frac{2(\mathrm{II}_{1}+\mathrm{II}_{2})}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}\epsilon\log d}{\beta\mathfrak{p}},\quad\forall s\in[0,1],

(B.23)

for some constant
C^{\prime}
.
By (
B.12
), (
B.13
), (
B.15
) and (
B.23
), we thus obtain the following inequality

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}(||U||_{\infty}>t)-\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)\right|

(B.24)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle A(t,\epsilon)\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)+\frac{C_{1}M\Delta_{0}\log(2d)}{2\epsilon^{2}}\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)\cdot\frac{C^{\prime}\epsilon\log d}{\beta\mathfrak{p}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)\left(A(t,\epsilon)+B(\Delta_{0},\mathfrak{p})\right),

where
A(t,\epsilon):=M_{1}\log d(t+1)\epsilon\exp(M_{2}\log^{2}d(t+1)\epsilon)
,
B(\Delta_{0},\mathfrak{p}):=C^{\prime\prime}{(\log d/\mathfrak{p})\Delta_{0}}
for some constants
M_{1},M_{2},C^{\prime\prime}
. In the last line, we also subsitute
\beta=\frac{2\log(2d)}{\epsilon}
.
By re-arranging (
B.24
), we finally have

\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(||U||_{\infty}>t)}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}-1\right|\leq A(t,\epsilon)+B(\Delta_{0},\mathfrak{p}).

Since
0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
and
\epsilon={c}/{\max\{(\log d)^{3/2},\mathfrak{p}\log d\}}
for some small enough constant
c>0
, we have
A(t,\epsilon)=O(B(\Delta_{0},\mathfrak{p}))
. Then (
B.11
) can be established, i.e.,

\sup_{0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}}\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(||U||_{\infty}>t)}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}-1\right|\leq C^{\prime\prime\prime}B(\Delta_{0},\mathfrak{p})=O\left(\frac{\Delta_{0}\log d}{\mathfrak{p}}\right).

∎Now we prove Theorem
3.3
using similar strategies as in Theorem
B.6
. Recall that the connectivity assumption in Theorem
3.3
assumes that
there exists a disjoint
\mathfrak{p}
-partition of nodes
\cup_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathcal{C}_{\ell}=[d]
such that
\sigma^{U}_{jk}=\sigma^{V}_{jk}=0
when
j\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell}
and
k\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell^{\prime}}
for some
\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}
. Since this connectivity assumption is stronger than that in Theorem
B.6
, we are able to do slightly more careful analysis in Lemma
B.7
. As a result, the minimal eigenvalue condition is no longer needed. Also note that Theorem
3.3
assumes the unit variance condition and there exists some
\sigma_{0}<1
such that
|\sigma^{V}_{jk}|\leq\sigma_{0},|\sigma^{U}_{jk}|\leq\sigma_{0}
for any
j\neq k
. Both the variance condition and the covariance condition can be relaxed.
In the following proof, we establish the Cramér-type comparison bound under a general variance condition. This general version is actually used in the proof of Theorem
5.2
. Specifically, the general variance condition says that
a_{0}\leq\sigma^{U}_{jj}=\sigma^{V}_{jj}\leq a_{1},~\forall j\in[d]
. After relaxing the unit variance assumption, some balanced variance assumption on the above components
\mathcal{C}_{\ell}
is required. It says that given any
j\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell}
with some
\ell
, there exists at least one
m\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell^{\prime}}
such that
\sigma^{U}_{jj}=\sigma^{V}_{jj}=\sigma^{U}_{mm}=\sigma^{V}_{mm}
for any
\ell^{\prime}\neq\ell
. Remark this condition is mainly needed for Lemma
B.15
. We will call all these assumptions about variances as general variance condition.
Denote
\widetilde{\sigma}^{U}_{jk}={\sigma}^{U}_{jk}/\sqrt{{\sigma}^{U}_{jj}{\sigma}^{U}_{kk}}
. Accordingly, the covariance condition on
\sigma_{jk}
in Theorem
3.3
can also be relaxed into the following: there exists some
\sigma_{0}<1
such that
|\widetilde{\sigma}^{V}_{jk}|=|{\sigma}^{V}_{jk}|/\sqrt{{\sigma}^{V}_{jj}{\sigma}^{V}_{kk}}\leq\sigma_{0}
for any
j\neq k
and
|\{(j,k):j\neq k,|\widetilde{\sigma}^{U}_{jk}|=|{\sigma}^{U}_{jk}|/\sqrt{\sigma^{U}_{jj}\sigma^{U}_{kk}}>\sigma_{0}\}|\leq b_{0}
for some constant
b_{0}
. We will call this condition as general covariance condition.

Following exactly the same derivations in Theorem
B.6
(up to (
B.22
)), we arrive at the following

\mathrm{II}(s)\leq\frac{2(\mathrm{II}_{1}+\mathrm{II}_{2})}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)},

where
\mathrm{II}(s),\mathrm{II}_{1},\mathrm{II}_{2}
are defined in (
B.15
) and (
B.22
), except that the random vector
G
can be constructed to satisfy more properties. Assuming the connectivity assumption of Theorem
3.3
and the general variance condition, we construct
G
by choosing one random variable
Z_{m}
from each component (except the one to which
Z_{j}
belongs) satisfying
\mathrm{Var}\left({Z_{m}}\right)=\sigma^{U}_{mm}=\sigma^{V}_{mm}=\sigma^{U}_{jj}=\sigma^{V}_{jj}=\mathrm{Var}\left({Z_{j}}\right)
. Such construction still satisfies the mentioned properties in Theorem
B.6
. Specifically,
(G,Z_{j})
consists of
(\mathfrak{p}+1)
i.i.d. Gaussian random variables. Moreover, for any
k\neq j,k\notin\mathcal{E}_{G}=\{m\in[d]:Z_{m}=G_{\ell}\text{ for some }\ell\in[\mathfrak{p}]\}
, there exists at most one
m\in\{j\}\cup\mathcal{E}_{G}
, such that
Z_{k}
and
Z_{m}
belong to the same component. Based on this property, we prove Lemma
B.15
and Lemma
B.17
, which do not require minimal eigenvalue conditions compared with Lemma
B.7
and Lemma
B.9
. We still apply Lemma
B.17
to bound the term
\mathrm{II}_{2}
. Regarding the term
\mathrm{II}_{1}
, we control it by using Lemma
B.8
. Therefore, we obtain the following

\mathrm{II}(s)\leq\frac{C^{\prime}\epsilon\log d}{\beta\mathfrak{p}}\left(1+\frac{b_{0}}{\sqrt{1-(s+(1-s)\sigma_{0})^{2}}}\right).

(B.25)

Note a simple calculus result:

\int_{0}^{1}\frac{b_{0}}{\sqrt{1-(s+(1-s)\sigma_{0})^{2}}}\leq\frac{0.5\pi b_{0}}{1-\sigma_{0}}<C^{\prime\prime}

for some constant
C^{\prime\prime}
when
\sigma_{0}<1
. Combining the above bound with (
B.25
), (
B.12
), (
B.13
), (
B.15
) and (
B.23
), we establish the bound (
3.2
) thus prove Theorem
3.3
.
∎

B.3
Ancillary lemmas for Theorem
B.6

Throughout the lemmas in this section, we will use
Z
and
G
without making the dependence on
s
explicitly, as mentioned in the proof of Theorem
B.6
.

Lemma B.7
.

Suppose
\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}^{U})\geq 1/b_{0}>0,\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}^{V})\geq 1/b_{0}>0
for some constant
b_{0}>0
. For the term
\mathrm{II}_{1}=\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{-\beta(||Z||_{\infty}-|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(E_{1})}\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||Z||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right]
with
{E_{1}}
defined in (
B.20
) and
\epsilon={c}/{\max\{(\log d)^{3/2},\mathfrak{p}\log d\}}
for some small enough constant
c>0
,
whenever
t
satisfies
0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
for some constant
C_{0}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{II}_{1}}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}\epsilon\log d}{\beta\mathfrak{p}}.

(B.26)

We will bound
\mathrm{II}_{1}
by the law of total expectation. Specifically, we first calculate the conditional expectation given
(G,Z_{j})
then take expectation with respect to
(G,Z_{j})
. Denoting the conditional density function of
||Z||_{\infty}\mid Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g
by
f_{g,z_{j}}(u)
, we write out the integral form of
\mathrm{II}_{1}
as

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}_{1}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{-\beta(||Z||_{\infty}-|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(||Z||_{\infty}>||G||_{\infty},||Z||_{\infty}>Z_{j})}\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||Z||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{\beta|Z_{j}|}\cdot\mathds{1}{(||G||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon,|Z_{j}|\leq t+\epsilon)}\left(\int_{t-\epsilon}^{t+\epsilon}f_{G,Z_{j}}(u)e^{-\beta u}\mathds{1}{(u>||G||_{\infty},u>|Z_{j}|)}du\right)}\right]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{\beta|Z_{j}|}\cdot\mathds{1}{(||G||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon,|Z_{j}|\leq t+\epsilon)}\left(\int_{t-\epsilon}^{t+\epsilon}C\sqrt{\log d}\cdot e^{-\beta u}\mathds{1}{(u>|Z_{j}|)}du\right)}\right]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle C\sqrt{\log d}~\mathbb{P}(||G||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[{\int_{|z_{j}|\leq t+\epsilon}\phi\left(\frac{z_{j}}{\sigma_{j}}\right)e^{\beta|z_{j}|}\left(\int_{t-\epsilon}^{t+\epsilon}e^{-\beta u}\mathds{1}{(u>|z_{j}|)}du\right)dz_{j}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle C\sqrt{\log d}~\mathbb{P}(||G||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)\underbrace{\int_{|z_{j}|\leq t+\epsilon}\phi\left(\frac{z_{j}}{\sigma_{j}}\right)e^{\beta|z_{j}|}\left(\int_{t-\epsilon}^{t+\epsilon}e^{-\beta u}\mathds{1}{(u>|z_{j}|)}du\right)dz_{j}}_{\mathrm{III}},

(B.27)

where the first inequality holds since
\mathds{1}{(u>||G||_{\infty},|Z_{j}|)}\leq\mathds{1}{(u>|Z_{j}|)}
and the conditional density function
f_{g,z_{j}}(u)
is bounded by
C\sqrt{\log d}
when
||g||_{\infty},|z_{j}|<u\leq t+\epsilon
and
0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
, as a result of Lemma
B.9
. Recall that
\phi(\cdot)
denotes the standard Gaussian PDF. We use the fact that
Z_{j}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}G
,
Z_{j}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{j}^{2})
and write out the integral form of the expectation with respect to
Z_{j}
, thus the second inequality follows. Then the integral
\mathrm{III}
can be further rewritten as

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\int_{t-\epsilon}^{t+\epsilon}e^{-\beta u}\left(\int_{0}^{u}\phi\left(\frac{x}{\sigma_{j}}\right)e^{\beta x}dx\right)du

(B.28)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\int_{t-\epsilon}^{t+\epsilon}e^{-\beta u}\left(e^{\frac{\beta^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}}{2}}\int_{0}^{u}\phi\left(\frac{x}{\sigma_{j}}-\beta\sigma_{j}\right)dx\right)du

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\int_{t-\epsilon}^{t+\epsilon}e^{-\beta u}\left(e^{\frac{\beta^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}}{2}}\int_{-\beta\sigma_{j}}^{u/\sigma_{j}-\beta\sigma_{j}}\phi\left(x\right)dx\right)du

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\int_{t-\epsilon}^{t+\epsilon}e^{-\beta u}\left(e^{\frac{\beta^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}}{2}}\bar{\Phi}\big(\beta\sigma_{j}-{u}/{\sigma_{j}}\big)\right)du

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\int_{t-\epsilon}^{t+\epsilon}e^{-\beta u}\left(e^{\frac{\beta^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}}{2}}\frac{e^{-\frac{(\beta\sigma_{j}-{u}/{\sigma_{j}})^{2}}{2}}}{\beta\sigma_{j}-{u}/{\sigma_{j}}}\right)du

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\beta\sigma_{j}}\int_{t-\epsilon}^{t+\epsilon}e^{-\beta u}\left(e^{\beta u}{e^{-\frac{u}{2\sigma_{j}}}}\right)du\leq\frac{8\epsilon}{\beta\sigma_{j}}\exp\left(-\frac{(t-\epsilon)^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}_{j}}\right),

where the first equality holds by Fubini’s theorem, and the second equality holds by the definition of
\phi(\cdot)
. Regarding the first inequality, we use the fact that
u/\sigma_{j}-\beta\sigma_{j}<2u/\sigma_{j}-\beta\sigma_{j}<0
for
u\leq t+\epsilon
and
t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
. This is because

\beta=\frac{2\log(2d)}{\epsilon}
and
\epsilon={c}/{\max\{(\log d)^{3/2},\mathfrak{p}\log d\}}
for some small enough constant
c>0
.
Then
\int_{-\beta\sigma_{j}}^{u/\sigma_{j}-\beta\sigma_{j}}\phi\left(x\right)dx\leq\bar{\Phi}(\beta\sigma_{j}-{u}/{\sigma_{j}})
, recalling
\bar{\Phi}=1-{\Phi}
, where
{\Phi}
is the standard Gaussian CDF. The second inequality holds as a result of Lemma
B.10
. The third inequality holds due to
\beta\sigma_{j}>2u/\sigma_{j}
for
u\leq t+\epsilon
.

By (
B.27
) and (
B.28
), we arrive at the following bound

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{II}_{1}}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sqrt{\log d}\cdot\frac{\mathbb{P}(||G||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}\cdot\frac{8\epsilon}{\beta\sigma_{j}}\exp\left(-\frac{(t-\epsilon)^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sqrt{\log d}\cdot\frac{C_{1}\epsilon}{\beta}\cdot\frac{\mathbb{P}(||G||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}{\mathbb{P}(||G||_{\infty}>t)}\cdot\phi\big(\frac{t-\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}\big)/\sigma_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\sqrt{\log d}\cdot\frac{C_{1}\epsilon}{\beta}\cdot\underbrace{\frac{(1-2\bar{\Phi}(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}))^{\mathfrak{p}}}{1-(1-2\bar{\Phi}\big(\frac{t}{\sigma_{j}})\big)^{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\phi\big(\frac{t-\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}\big)/\sigma_{j}}_{\Lambda(t,\epsilon,\mathfrak{p})},

for some constants
C,C_{1}
, where the second inequality holds due to the definition of
\phi(z)
and
\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)\geq\mathbb{P}(||G||_{\infty}>t)
. This is because

\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)\geq\mathbb{P}(\max_{k\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}|V_{k}|>t)=\mathbb{P}(||G_{V}||_{\infty}>t)=\mathbb{P}(||G||_{\infty}>t),

(B.29)

where
G_{V}=(Z_{m})_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{0},m\neq j}
with
Z=V
has the same distribution as
G
.
Regarding the last line, by the construction of
G=(G_{\ell})_{\ell\in[\mathfrak{p}]}=(Z_{m})_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{0},m\neq j}
in the proof of Theorem
B.6
, we have
\{G_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in[\mathfrak{p}]}
are
\mathfrak{p}
i.i.d. Gaussian random variables with
\mathrm{Var}\left({G_{\ell}}\right)=\mathrm{Var}\left({Z_{j}}\right)=\sigma_{j}^{2}
.
By applying Lemma
B.11
to the term
{\Lambda(t,\epsilon,\mathfrak{p})}
in the last line, we further obtain,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{II}_{1}}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\sqrt{\log d}\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\beta}\frac{\sqrt{\log d}}{\mathfrak{p}}=\frac{C^{\prime}\epsilon\log d}{\beta\mathfrak{p}},

for some constant
C^{\prime}
, therefore (
B.26
) is established.
∎

Lemma B.8
.

For the term
\mathrm{II}_{2}=\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{-\beta(||Z||_{\infty}-|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(E_{2})}\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||Z||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right]
with
{E_{2}}
defined in (
B.21
) and
\epsilon={c}/{\max\{(\log d)^{3/2},\mathfrak{p}\log d\}}
for some small enough constant
c>0
,
whenever
t
satisfies
0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
for some constant
C_{0}>0
,
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{II}_{2}}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}\leq\frac{C^{\prime\prime}\epsilon\sqrt{\log d}}{\beta\mathfrak{p}}.

(B.30)

By the definition of
E_{2}
in (
B.21
) and the tower property, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}_{2}

\displaystyle~=\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{-\beta(||Z||_{\infty}-|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(||G||_{\infty}=||Z||_{\infty}>|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||Z||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right]

\displaystyle~=\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{-\beta(||G||_{\infty}-|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(||G||_{\infty}=||Z||_{\infty},||G||_{\infty}>|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||G||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right]

\displaystyle~\leq\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{-\beta(||G||_{\infty}-|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(||G||_{\infty}>|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||G||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right]

\displaystyle~=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{\beta|Z_{j}|}\mathds{1}{(|Z_{j}|<||G||_{\infty})}}\,|\,{G}\right]e^{-\beta||G||_{\infty}}\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||G||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right].

(B.31)

First we bound
\mathrm{III}(g):=\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{\beta|Z_{j}|}\mathds{1}{(|Z_{j}|<||G||_{\infty})}}\,|\,{G=g}\right]
when
||g||_{\infty}\in[t-\epsilon,t+\epsilon]
. Specifically,

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}(g)=\frac{2}{\sigma_{j}}\int_{0}^{||g||_{\infty}}e^{\beta x}\phi\Big(\frac{x}{\sigma_{j}}\Big)dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2e^{{\beta^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}}/{2}}}{\sigma_{j}}\int_{0}^{||g||_{\infty}}\phi\Big(\frac{x-\beta\sigma_{j}^{2}}{\sigma_{j}}\Big)dx

(B.32)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{2e^{\beta^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}/2}}\int_{-\infty}^{||g||_{\infty}/\sigma_{j}-\beta\sigma_{j}}\phi(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{2e^{\beta^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}/2}}~\bar{\Phi}(\beta\sigma_{j}-{||g||_{\infty}}/{\sigma_{j}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{2e^{\beta^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}/2}}\frac{\phi\left(\beta\sigma_{j}-{||g||_{\infty}}/{\sigma_{j}}\right)}{\beta\sigma_{j}-{||g||_{\infty}}/{\sigma_{j}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\beta\sigma_{j}}\phi\left(\frac{||g||_{\infty}}{\sigma_{j}}\right)e^{\beta||g||_{\infty}},

where the first equality holds due to
Z_{j}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}G
, and the second equality comes from rearranging. The first inequality holds by the change of variable
y=(x-\beta\sigma_{j}^{2})/\sigma_{j}
and setting the lower limit of the integral as
-\infty
. Because
\beta=\frac{2\log(2d)}{\epsilon}
and
\epsilon={c}/{\max\{(\log d)^{3/2},\mathfrak{p}\log d\}}
for some small enough constant
c>0
, we have
||g||_{\infty}/\sigma_{j}<\beta\sigma_{j}
for
||g||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon
and
t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
.
Then the second inequality holds as a result of Lemma
B.10
and the fact that
\beta\sigma_{j}-||g||_{\infty}/\sigma_{j}>0
. The last inequality comes from rearranging and the fact that
\beta\sigma_{j}>2||g||_{\infty}/\sigma_{j}
for
||g||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon
and
t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
.
Combining (
B.32
) with (
B.31
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{III}(G)\cdot e^{-\beta||G||_{\infty}}\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||G||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right]

(B.33)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\beta\sigma_{j}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\phi\Big(\frac{||G||_{\infty}}{\sigma_{j}}\Big)e^{\beta(||G||_{\infty})}\cdot e^{-\beta||G||_{\infty}}\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||G||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\beta\sigma_{j}}\int_{t-\epsilon}^{t+\epsilon}\phi\big(\frac{y}{\sigma_{j}}\big)f(y)dy,

where
f(y)
denotes the PDF of
||G||_{\infty}
. As
\{G_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in[\mathfrak{p}]}
are i.i.d. Gaussian random variables satisfying
\forall~\ell\in[\mathfrak{p}]
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{G_{\ell}}\right]=0
and
\mathrm{Var}\left({G_{\ell}}\right)=\sigma_{j}^{2}
, we have for
y>0
,

\mathbb{P}(||G||_{\infty}\leq y)=\mathbb{P}(\bigcup_{\ell\in[\mathfrak{p}]}|G_{\ell}|\leq y)=(1-2\mathbb{P}(G_{\ell}/\sigma_{j}>y/\sigma_{j}))^{\mathfrak{p}}=(1-2\bar{\Phi}({y}/{\sigma_{j}}))^{\mathfrak{p}}.

(B.34)

Thus we have the PDF of
||G||_{\infty}
equals
f(y)=\frac{2\mathfrak{p}}{\sigma_{j}}\left(1-2\bar{\Phi}(\frac{y}{\sigma_{j}}))^{\mathfrak{p}}\right)^{\frac{\mathfrak{p}-1}{\mathfrak{p}}}\phi(\frac{y}{\sigma_{j}})
. Plugging the expression of
f(y)
into (
B.33
), we further derive the following bound

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{II}_{2}}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{8\mathfrak{p}}{\beta\sigma_{j}^{2}}\int_{t-\epsilon}^{t+\epsilon}\frac{\left(1-2\bar{\Phi}(\frac{y}{\sigma_{j}}))^{\mathfrak{p}}\right)^{\frac{\mathfrak{p}-1}{\mathfrak{p}}}\phi^{2}(\frac{y}{\sigma_{j}})}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}dy

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{8\mathfrak{p}}{\beta\sigma_{j}^{2}}\int_{t-\epsilon}^{t+\epsilon}\frac{\left(1-2\bar{\Phi}(\frac{y}{\sigma_{j}}))^{\mathfrak{p}}\right)^{\frac{\mathfrak{p}-1}{\mathfrak{p}}}\phi^{2}(\frac{y}{\sigma_{j}})}{1-\mathbb{P}(||G||_{\infty}\leq t)}dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{8\mathfrak{p}}{\beta\sigma_{j}^{2}}\int_{t-\epsilon}^{t+\epsilon}\frac{\left((1-2\bar{\Phi}(\frac{y}{\sigma_{j}}))^{\mathfrak{p}}\right)^{\frac{\mathfrak{p}-1}{\mathfrak{p}}}\phi^{2}(\frac{y}{\sigma_{j}})}{1-(1-2\bar{\Phi}(\frac{t}{\sigma_{j}}))^{\mathfrak{p}}}dy

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{16\epsilon}{\beta\sigma_{j}^{2}\mathfrak{p}}\frac{\left((1-2\bar{\Phi}(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}))^{\mathfrak{p}}\right)^{\frac{\mathfrak{p}-1}{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathfrak{p}\phi(\frac{t-\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}))^{2}}{1-(1-2\bar{\Phi}(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}))^{\mathfrak{p}}}\leq\frac{C^{\prime\prime}\epsilon\sqrt{\log d}}{\beta\mathfrak{p}},

for some constant
C^{\prime}
, where the second inequality holds due to (
B.29
), as mentioned in the proof of Lemma
B.7
.
The equality holds as a result of substituting the expression of
\mathbb{P}(||G||_{\infty}\leq t)
by (
B.34
). The third inequality holds since
1-2\bar{\Phi}(z)
is monotonically increasing and
{\phi}(z)
is monotonically decreasing when
z\geq 0
. As for the last line, we apply Lemma
B.12
.
Finally, (
B.30
) is established.
∎

Lemma B.9
.

Suppose
\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}^{U})\geq 1/b_{0}>0,\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}^{V})\geq 1/b_{0}>0
for some constant
b_{0}>0
. Recall that the density function of the conditional distribution of
||Z||_{\infty}\mid\{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g\}

is denoted by
f_{g,z_{j}}(z)
. Suppose
\epsilon>0
, when
0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
for some constant
C_{0}>0
and
|z_{j}|,||g||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon
, we have

f_{g,z_{j}}(z)\leq C\sqrt{\log d},\quad\forall~z\in(\max\{|z_{j}|,||g||_{\infty}\},t+\epsilon].

First we introduce some new notations. Let
(\sigma_{jk})_{1\leq j,k\leq d}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
be the covariance matrix of
Z
. For given
j
, we denote

\sigma_{kk\cdot j}:=\sigma_{kk}-\sigma_{kj}^{2}\sigma_{jj}^{-1}-\sum_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}\sigma_{km}^{2}\sigma_{mm}^{-1}.

(B.35)

As
z\in(\max\{|z_{j}|,||g||_{\infty}\},t+\epsilon]
, we can choose
\delta
such that
0<\delta<z-\max\{|z_{j}|,||g||_{\infty}\}
. Throughout the following proof, we will work with such
\delta
. Since
\max\{|z_{j}|,||g||_{\infty}\}-z<-\delta
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left({\big|||Z||_{\infty}-z\big|\leq\delta}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left({\big|||X||_{\infty}-z\big|\leq\delta}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right),

(B.36)

where
X
denotes the
(d-\mathfrak{p}-1)
-dimensional random vector by excluding
Z_{j},G
from
Z
and therefore
||Z||_{\infty}=\max\{||X||_{\infty},|Z_{j}|,||G||_{\infty}\}
.

Recalling
G=(G_{\ell})_{\ell\in[\mathfrak{p}]}=(Z_{m})_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}
, where
\mathcal{E}_{G}
denotes the indices of the random variables in
G
(among
Z
), i.e.,
\mathcal{E}_{G}=\{m\in[d]:Z_{m}=G_{\ell}\text{ for some }\ell\in[\mathfrak{p}]\}
, we have

||X||_{\infty}=\max_{k\in[d],~k\notin\{j,\mathcal{E}_{G}\}}\{\max\{Z_{k},-Z_{k}\}\}.

Given
j
and the choice of
G
, we also denote

\quad\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}:=\min_{k\in[d],k\notin\{j,\mathcal{E}_{G}\}}\sqrt{\sigma_{kk\cdot j}},\quad\bar{\rho}_{j}:=\max_{k\in[d],k\notin\{j,\mathcal{E}_{G}\}}\frac{|\sigma_{jk}|}{\sigma_{jj}}.

(B.37)

For each
k\in[d],k\notin\{j,\mathcal{E}_{G}\}
, the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}\left[{Z_{k}}\,|\,{Z_{j},G}\right]
has the following expression,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{Z_{k}}\,|\,{Z_{j},G}\right]=\sigma_{kj}\sigma_{jj}^{-1}Z_{j}+\sum_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}(\sigma_{km}\sigma_{mm}^{-1}Z_{m}),

(B.38)

since
(Z_{j},G)
are all independent. Note that the requirement
(c)
in Assumption
B.4
says
\forall~k\in[d]
,
|\{m\in\mathcal{E}_{0}:|\sigma^{U}_{km}|+|\sigma^{V}_{km}|\neq 0\}|\leq c_{0}
, we thus have

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}\mathds{1}{(\sigma_{km}\neq 0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}\mathds{1}{((s\sigma^{U}_{km}+{(1-s)}\sigma^{V}_{km})\neq 0)}

(B.39)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}\mathds{1}{(\sigma^{U}_{km}\neq 0\text{ or }\sigma^{V}_{km}\neq 0)}\leq c_{0},

where the first equality holds by the definition of
\sigma_{km}
and
Z=W(s)=\sqrt{s}U+\sqrt{1-s}V
.
Combining (
B.39
) with (
B.38
), it yields the following bound on
|\mathbb{E}\left[{Z_{k}}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right]|
,

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}\left[{Z_{k}}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right]|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big|\sigma_{kj}\sigma_{jj}^{-1}z_{j}+\sum_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}(\sigma_{km}\sigma_{mm}^{-1}z_{m})\big|

(B.40)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bar{\rho}_{j}(|z_{j}|+c_{0}||g||_{\infty}),

where
\bar{\rho}_{j}=\max_{k\in\mathcal{E}_{X}}\frac{|\sigma_{jk}|}{\sigma_{jj}}
as defined.
Denoting
\mathcal{E}_{X}:=\{k:k\in[d],~k\notin\{j,\mathcal{E}_{G}\}\}
, we define the following random variables,

\widetilde{W}_{2k-1}=\frac{{Z_{k}}-{z}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{kk\cdot j}}}+\frac{\widetilde{z}}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}},\quad\widetilde{W}_{2k}=\frac{-{Z_{k}}-{z}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{kk\cdot j}}}+\frac{\widetilde{z}}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}},\quad k\in\mathcal{E}_{X},

(B.41)

where
\widetilde{z}=z+\bar{\rho}_{j}(|z_{j}|+c_{0}||g||_{\infty})
.
Then by the definitions of
\sigma_{kk\cdot j},\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}
and
\bar{\rho}_{j}
in (
B.35
) and (
B.37
), we have the above random variables satisfy the following properties,

\mathbb{E}\left[{\widetilde{W}_{m}}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right]\geq 0,\quad\mathrm{Var}\left({\widetilde{W}_{m}}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right)=1,

where
m=2k-1
or
2k
and
k\in\mathcal{E}_{X}
. Denote those random variables defined in (
B.41
) by
\{\widetilde{W}_{m}\}
for notation simplicity. We let
q_{z_{j},g}(w)
be the PDF of the conditional distribution of
\max_{m}\{\widetilde{W}_{m}\}\mid Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g
. Then we will apply the derivation of Step
2
in Theorem
3
of
[
19
]
to bound
q_{z_{j},g}(w)
. Note that for the following derivations, we always conditional on the event
Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g
. First, we verify the condition on
\{\widetilde{W}_{m}\}
. Since
|\mathrm{Corr}{(U_{j},U_{k})}|\neq 1
,
|\mathrm{Corr}{(V_{j},V_{k})}|\neq 1
for distinct
j,k\in[d]
, we then have the correlation between
\widetilde{W}_{m_{1}}
and
\widetilde{W}_{m_{2}}
for
m_{1}\neq m_{2}
is less than
1
.
Therefore, by applying the derivation of Step
2
in Theorem
3
of
[
19
]
to
\{\widetilde{W}_{m}\}
, we have

q_{z_{j},g}(w)\leq h(w):=2(w\vee 1)\exp\left\{-\frac{(w-\bar{w}-a_{d})^{2}_{+}}{2}\right\},

where
\bar{w}=\max_{m}\mathbb{E}\left[{\widetilde{W}_{m}}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right]
and

a_{d}=\max_{m}\mathbb{E}\left[{\Big(\widetilde{W}_{m}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\widetilde{W}_{m}}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right]\Big)}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right].

When
w\leq\bar{w}+a_{d}
, we have
h(w)\leq 2(\bar{w}+a_{d})
. To deal with the case where
w>\bar{w}+a_{d}
, we consider

\displaystyle\log(h(w))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log(2w)-\frac{(w-\bar{w}-a_{d})^{2}}{2},

\displaystyle\frac{d\log(h(w))}{dw}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{w}-(w-\bar{w}-a_{d}),

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}\log(h(w))}{dw^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{w^{2}}-1<0.

Solving
\frac{d}{dw}\log(h(w))=0
yields
w^{\star}=\frac{\bar{w}+a_{d}+\sqrt{(\bar{w}+a_{d})^{2}+4}}{2}
. Therefore, the PDF of the conditional distribution of
\max_{m}\{\widetilde{W}_{m}\}\mid Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g
can be bounded by

h(w)\leq h(w^{\star})\leq 3(\bar{w}+a_{d}).

(B.42)

Now we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\big|||Z||_{\infty}-z\big|\leq\delta}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\left|||{X}||_{\infty}-{z}\right|\leq\delta}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\left|\max_{k\in\mathcal{E}_{X}}\left\{{Z}_{k},-{Z}_{k}\right\}-{z}\right|\leq\delta}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\left|\max_{k\in\mathcal{E}_{X}}\left\{\frac{{Z}_{k}-{z}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{kk\cdot j}}},\frac{-{Z}_{k}-{z}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{kk\cdot j}}}\right\}\right|\leq\frac{\delta}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}\left({\left|\max_{k\in\mathcal{E}_{X}}\left\{\frac{{Z}_{k}-{z}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{kk\cdot j}}}+\frac{\widetilde{z}}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}},\frac{-{Z}_{k}-{z}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{kk\cdot j}}}+\frac{\widetilde{z}}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}\right\}-y\right|\leq\frac{\delta}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}\left({\left|\max_{m}\{\widetilde{W}_{m}\}-y\right|\leq\frac{\delta}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right)\leq\frac{6\delta}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}(\bar{w}+a_{d}),

(B.43)

where the first equality holds by (
B.36
), the second equality holds by the definition of
X
and
\mathcal{E}_{X}
, the first inequality holds since
\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}=\min_{k\neq j}\sqrt{\sigma_{kk\cdot j}}
, the third equality holds by the definition of
\{\widetilde{W}_{m}\}
in (
B.41
), and the last inequality holds by the bound on
h(w)
in (
B.42
).
Regarding the quantity
\bar{w}=\max_{m}\mathbb{E}\left[{\widetilde{W}_{m}}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right]
, we have

\displaystyle\bar{w}=~

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\mathcal{E}_{X}}\left\{\frac{\pm\mathbb{E}\left[{{Z_{k}}}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right]-{z}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{kk\cdot j}}}+\frac{\widetilde{z}}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\mathcal{E}_{X}}\left\{\frac{\pm\mathbb{E}\left[{{Z_{k}}}\,|\,{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g}\right]}{\sqrt{\sigma_{kk\cdot j}}}\right\}+\max_{k\in\mathcal{E}_{X}}\left\{\frac{1}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\sigma_{kk\cdot j}}}\right\}z+\frac{\bar{\rho}_{j}(|z_{j}|+c_{0}||g||_{\infty})}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\mathcal{E}_{X}}\left\{\frac{\pm\big(\sigma_{kj}\sigma_{jj}^{-1}z_{j}+\sum_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}(\sigma_{km}\sigma_{mm}^{-1}z_{m})\big)}{\sqrt{\sigma_{kk\cdot j}}}\right\}+\frac{z}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}+\frac{\bar{\rho}_{j}(|z_{j}|+c_{0}||g||_{\infty})}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{2\bar{\rho}_{j}(|z_{j}|+c_{0}||g||_{\infty})}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}+\frac{z}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}\leq\frac{2\bar{\rho}_{j}(1+c_{0})+1}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}(t+\epsilon),

(B.44)

where
\max\{\pm A\}:=\max\{A,-A\}
, the first inequality holds by the definition of
\widetilde{z}
, the second inequality holds by (
B.38
), and the last inequality holds by the definitions of
\bar{\rho}_{j}
and
\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}
and the fact
\sum_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}\mathds{1}{(\sigma_{km}\neq 0)}\leq c_{0}
.

Let
\delta
in (
B.43
) go to
0
, we get the following bound on the density function of the conditional distribution of
||Z||_{\infty}\mid\{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g\}
, i.e., when
0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
and
|z_{j}|,||g||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon
,

f_{g,z_{j}}(z)\leq\frac{6}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}(\bar{w}+a_{d})\leq\frac{6}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}\left(\frac{2\bar{\rho}_{j}(1+c_{0})+1}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}C_{1}\sqrt{\log d}+C_{2}\sqrt{\log d}\right),

(B.45)

for any
z\in(\max\{|z_{j}|,||g||_{\infty}\},t+\epsilon]
. The first inequality holds by (
B.43
). Regarding the second inequality, we apply the result in
(
B.44
)
and bound
(t+\epsilon)
and
a_{d}
by
C_{1}\sqrt{\log d}
for some constant
C_{1}
. Note
a_{d}\leq C_{1}\sqrt{\log d}
is because of the maximal inequalities for sub-Gaussian random variables (Lemma 5.2 in
[
82
]
).
As for
\bar{\rho}_{j}=\max_{k\in\mathcal{E}_{X}}\frac{|\sigma_{jk}|}{\sigma_{jj}}
, we have

\bar{\rho}_{j}^{2}\leq\max_{k\neq j}{\frac{\sigma_{kk}}{\sigma_{jj}}}\leq\frac{\max_{j}\sigma_{jj}^{U}}{\min_{j}\sigma_{jj}^{U}}\leq\frac{\max_{j}\sigma_{jj}^{U}}{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}^{U})}=O(1),

where the first inequality holds by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the second inequality holds by the definition of
Z
and
\sigma_{jj}^{U}=\sigma_{jj}^{V}
, the third inequality holds by the fact that
\min_{j}\sigma_{jj}^{U}\geq\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}^{U})
, and the last step holds under the stated assumption of Theorem
B.6
. As for
\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}=\min_{k\in\mathcal{E}_{X}}\sqrt{\sigma_{kk\cdot j}}
where
\sigma_{kk\cdot j}=\sigma_{kk}-\sigma_{kj}^{2}\sigma_{jj}^{-1}-\sum_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}\sigma_{km}^{2}\sigma_{mm}^{-1}=\mathrm{Var}\left({Z_{k}}\,|\,{Z_{j},G}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\underline{\sigma^{2}_{\cdot j}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\min_{k\in\mathcal{E}_{X}}\mathrm{Var}\left({Z_{k}}\,|\,{Z_{j},G}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\min_{k}\mathrm{Var}\left({Z_{k}}\,|\,{Z_{\text{-}k}}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{k}((\bm{\Sigma}^{Z})^{-1})_{kk}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}((\bm{\Sigma}^{Z})^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1/\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}^{Z})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\min\{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}^{U}),\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}^{V}\})^{-1}\leq b_{0},

under the stated assumption that
\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}^{U})\geq 1/b_{0},\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}^{V})\geq 1/b_{0}
, where the first inequality holds since
(Z_{j},G)
is a sub-vector of
Z_{\text{-}k}:=Z_{(1:d)\setminus k}
, the second equality holds by the relationship between the partial variances and the inverse covariance matrix, and the last three hold by the definitions of
\lambda_{\min}(\cdot),\lambda_{\max}(\cdot)
. Thus we have
f_{g,z_{j}}(z)\leq C\sqrt{\log d}
for some constant
C
, i.e., Lemma
B.9
is proved.

∎

Lemma B.10
.

For
z>0
, we have

\frac{\phi(z)}{2(z\vee 1)}\leq\bar{\Phi}(z)=1-{\Phi}(z)\leq\frac{\phi(z)}{z},

where
\phi(z),\Phi(z)
is the PDF and CDF of the standard Gaussian distribution respectively.

This is a simple fact derived from Mill’s inequality; see the derivations in the proof of Theorem 3 in
[
19
]
.
∎

Lemma B.11
.

Whenever
0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
for some constant
C_{0}>0
, and
\epsilon={c}/{\max\{(\log d)^{3/2},\mathfrak{p}\log d\}}
for some small enough constant
c>0
, we have

\Lambda(t,\epsilon,\mathfrak{p}):=\frac{(1-2\bar{\Phi}(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}))^{\mathfrak{p}}}{1-(1-2\bar{\Phi}(\frac{t}{\sigma_{j}}))^{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\phi\big(\frac{t-\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}\big)=O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\log d}}{\mathfrak{p}}\right).

(B.46)

By Lemma
B.10
, we can simplify
\Lambda(t,\epsilon,\mathfrak{p})
into the following

\Lambda(t,\epsilon,\mathfrak{p})\leq\frac{\left(1-\frac{{\phi}(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}})}{\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}\vee 1}\right)^{\mathfrak{p}}}{1-\left(1-\frac{\phi(\frac{t}{\sigma_{j}})}{\frac{t}{\sigma}\vee 1}\right)^{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\phi\big(\frac{t-\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}\big).

When
\frac{t}{\sigma_{j}}\leq 1
, we have
\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}\leq 2
due to the choice of
\epsilon
. Because
\frac{t}{\sigma_{j}}>0,\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}>0
and
\phi(z)
is monotonically decreasing when
z>0
, we then have the the bound below,

\Lambda(t,\epsilon,p)\leq\frac{(1-\phi(2)/2)^{\mathfrak{p}}}{1-(1-\phi(1))^{\mathfrak{p}}}=O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\log d}}{\mathfrak{p}}\right),

where the second inequality holds due to
0<\phi(2)<\phi(1)<0.5
and
\mathfrak{p}>1
. Now it suffices to consider the case where
\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}>\frac{t}{\sigma_{j}}>1
and deal with the following

\Lambda(t,\epsilon,\mathfrak{p})\leq\frac{\left(1-\frac{{\phi}(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}})}{\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}}\right)^{\mathfrak{p}}}{1-\left(1-\frac{\phi(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}})}{\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}}\right)^{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\phi\left(\frac{t-\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}\right).

We further bound
\Lambda(t,\epsilon,\mathfrak{p})
as

\displaystyle\Lambda(t,\epsilon,\mathfrak{p})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\left(1-\frac{{\phi}(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}})}{\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}}\right)^{\mathfrak{p}}}{1-\left(1-\frac{\phi(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}})}{\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}}\right)^{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\phi\Big(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}\Big)\cdot e^{\frac{t\epsilon}{2\sigma^{2}_{j}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2~\frac{\left(1-\frac{{\phi}(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}})}{\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}}\right)^{\mathfrak{p}}}{1-\Bigg(1-\frac{\phi(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}})}{\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}}\Bigg)^{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\phi\Big(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}\Big)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle 2e^{H(\lambda)}\cdot\frac{t+\epsilon}{\mathfrak{p}\sigma_{j}}

where the first inequality comes from rearranging, the second inequality holds since
\exp{(\frac{t\epsilon}{2\sigma^{2}_{j}})}<2
for
t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
. This is because
\epsilon={c}/{\max\{(\log d)^{3/2},\mathfrak{p}\log d\}}
for some small enough constant
c>0
. The last line holds by rewriting using some new notations:
\lambda:=\mathfrak{p}~\frac{{\phi}(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}})}{\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}}
and

\displaystyle H(\lambda):=\log\left(\frac{(1-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{p}})^{\mathfrak{p}}}{1-(1-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{p}})^{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\lambda\right)=\mathfrak{p}\log\Big(1-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{p}}\Big)-\log\Big({1-(1-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{p}})^{\mathfrak{p}}}\Big)+\log\lambda.

(B.47)

Since
\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}>1
, we have
0<\lambda<\mathfrak{p}
. Below we will first deal with
H(\lambda)
then obtain the bound on
\Lambda(t,\epsilon,\mathfrak{p})
. To bound
H(\lambda)
, consider taking the derivative of
H(\lambda)
with respect to
\lambda
, then we have

\displaystyle H^{\prime}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{p}}{\lambda-\mathfrak{p}}-\frac{(1-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{p}})^{(\mathfrak{p}-1)}}{1-(1-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{p}})^{\mathfrak{p}}}+\frac{1}{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{p}}{\lambda-\mathfrak{p}}-\frac{1}{1-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\frac{(1-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{p}})^{\mathfrak{p}}}{1-(1-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{p}})^{\mathfrak{p}}}+\frac{1}{\lambda}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{p}}{\lambda-\mathfrak{p}}-\frac{1}{1-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\frac{1-\lambda}{1-(1-\lambda)}+\frac{1}{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{p}}{\lambda-\mathfrak{p}}+\frac{1}{1-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{p}}}-\frac{1}{\lambda}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{p}}}+\frac{1}{\lambda}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda}\Big(1-\frac{\mathfrak{p}}{\mathfrak{p}-\lambda}\Big)<0,

where the first inequality holds by the Bernoulli’s inequality:
{(1+x)^{r}\geq 1+rx}
when
r\in\mathbb{N},1+x\geq 0
, and the last inequality holds since
0<\lambda<\mathfrak{p}
. Now we have
H(\lambda)
is monotone decreasing. When
0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
, we will first find the lower bound on
\lambda=\mathfrak{p}~\frac{{\phi}(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}})}{\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}}
, denoted by
\underline{\lambda}
. Then we have
H(\lambda)
is bounded by
H(\underline{\lambda})
due to its monotonicity.
Regarding
\underline{\lambda}
, we denote
\bar{x}:=2C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}/\sigma_{j}
and note
\frac{\phi(x)}{x}
is monotone decreasing when
x\geq 0
. Then we have, when
0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}~\frac{\phi(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}})}{\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}}\geq\mathfrak{p}~\frac{\phi(\bar{x})}{\bar{x}}\geq\frac{\mathfrak{p}}{d^{a_{1}}}:=\underline{\lambda},

where
a_{1}>2
.
Therefore we obtain

\displaystyle H(\lambda)\leq H({\underline{\lambda}})=\left.\log\left(\frac{(1-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{p}})^{\mathfrak{p}}}{1-(1-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{p}})^{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\lambda\right)\right|_{\lambda=\underline{\lambda}}\leq\left.\log\left(\frac{{\lambda}}{1-(1-\mathfrak{p}\frac{{\lambda}}{\mathfrak{p}}+\frac{(\mathfrak{p}-1)\mathfrak{p}}{2}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\mathfrak{p}^{2}})}\right)\right|_{\lambda=\underline{\lambda}}\leq C^{\prime},

(B.48)

where the second inequality holds due to the fact that
(1-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{p}})^{\mathfrak{p}}\leq 1
,
\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{p}}\in[0,1]
and Lemma
B.13
. The third inequality holds since
\underline{\lambda}=\frac{\mathfrak{p}}{d^{a_{1}}}\leq\frac{1}{d^{a_{1}-1}}<\frac{1}{d}
, then we have

\left.\left(\frac{{\lambda}}{1-(1-\mathfrak{p}\frac{{\lambda}}{\mathfrak{p}}+\frac{(\mathfrak{p}-1)\mathfrak{p}}{2}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\mathfrak{p}^{2}})}\right)\right|_{\lambda=\underline{\lambda}}=\frac{\underline{\lambda}}{\underline{\lambda}-\frac{2(\mathfrak{p}-1)}{\mathfrak{p}}\underline{\lambda}^{2}}\leq\frac{\underline{\lambda}}{\underline{\lambda}-2\underline{\lambda}^{2}}=\frac{1}{1-2\underline{\lambda}}\leq C^{\prime}_{1},

for some constant
C^{\prime}_{1}
. Now we figure out the bound on
\Lambda(t,\epsilon,\mathfrak{p})
,

\displaystyle\Lambda(t,\epsilon,\mathfrak{p})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\left(1-\frac{{\phi}(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}})}{\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}}\right)^{\mathfrak{p}}}{1-\left(1-\frac{\phi(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}})}{\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}}\right)^{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\phi\Big(\frac{t-\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\left(1-\frac{{\phi}(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}})}{\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}}\right)^{\mathfrak{p}}}{1-\left(1-\frac{\phi(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}})}{\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}}\right)^{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\phi\Big(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}\Big)\cdot e^{\frac{t\epsilon}{2\sigma^{2}_{j}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2~\frac{\left(1-\frac{{\phi}(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}})}{\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}}\right)^{\mathfrak{p}}}{1-\Bigg(1-\frac{\phi(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}})}{\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}}\Bigg)^{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\phi\Big(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2e^{H(\lambda)}\cdot\frac{t+\epsilon}{\mathfrak{p}\sigma_{j}}\leq\frac{C\sqrt{\log d}}{\mathfrak{p}},
