where the second inequality comes from rearranging, the third inequality holds since
\exp{(\frac{t\epsilon}{2\sigma^{2}_{j}})}<2
for
t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
. This is because
\epsilon={c}/{\max\{(\log d)^{3/2},\mathfrak{p}\log d\}}
for some small enough constant
c>0
. And the last line holds by (
B.47
) and (
B.48
). Therefore Lemma
B.11
is established.
∎

Lemma B.12
.

Under the same conditions as Lemma
B.11
, we have

\frac{(1-2\bar{\Phi}(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}))^{\mathfrak{p}-1}}{1-(1-2\bar{\Phi}(\frac{t}{\sigma_{j}}))^{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\Big(\mathfrak{p}\phi\big(\frac{t-\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}\big)\Big)^{2}=O\left(\sqrt{\log d}\right).

(B.49)

Note that the result (
B.46
) in Lemma
B.11
can be rewritten as

\mathfrak{p}~\Lambda(t,\epsilon,\mathfrak{p})=\frac{(1-2\bar{\Phi}(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}))^{\mathfrak{p}}}{1-(1-2\bar{\Phi}(\frac{t}{\sigma_{j}}))^{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\Big(\mathfrak{p}\phi\big(\frac{t-\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}\big)\Big)=O\left(\sqrt{\log d}\right).

By similar derivations as in the proof of Lemma
B.11
, we can establish

\frac{(1-2\bar{\Phi}(\frac{t+\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}))^{\mathfrak{p}-1}}{1-(1-2\bar{\Phi}(\frac{t}{\sigma_{j}}))^{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\Big(\mathfrak{p}\phi\big(\frac{t-\epsilon}{\sigma_{j}}\big)\Big)^{2}=O\left(\sqrt{\log d}\right).

∎

Lemma B.13
.

For
x\in[0,1]
, we have

(1-x)^{\mathfrak{p}}\leq 1-\mathfrak{p}x+0.5\mathfrak{p}(\mathfrak{p}-1)x^{2}
.

When
\mathfrak{p}=1
, the above simply holds. Now we consider the case where
\mathfrak{p}>1
. Let
Q(x)=(1-x)^{\mathfrak{p}}-(1-\mathfrak{p}x+0.5\mathfrak{p}(\mathfrak{p}-1)x^{2})
, we have
Q(0)=0
and

\displaystyle Q^{\prime}(x)=-\mathfrak{p}(1-x)^{(\mathfrak{p}-1)}+\mathfrak{p}-\mathfrak{p}(\mathfrak{p}-1)x\leq-\mathfrak{p}(1-(\mathfrak{p}-1)x)+\mathfrak{p}-\mathfrak{p}(\mathfrak{p}-1)x=0,

(B.50)

where the inequality holds by applying Bernoulli’s inequality to
(1-x)^{(\mathfrak{p}-1)}
for
\mathfrak{p}>1,x\in[0,1]
. Therefore,
Q(x)
is monotonically decreasing, and the statement is proved.
∎

B.4
Ancillary lemmas for Theorem
3.3

Remark B.14
.

Recall that the connectivity assumption of Theorem
3.2
assumes
that there exists a disjoint
\mathfrak{p}
-partition of nodes
\cup_{\ell=1}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathcal{C}_{\ell}=[d]
such that
\sigma^{U}_{jk}=\sigma^{V}_{jk}=0
when
j\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell}
and
k\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell^{\prime}}
for some
\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}
. The more general version of the variance condition assumes:
a_{0}\leq\sigma^{U}_{jj}=\sigma^{V}_{jj}\leq a_{1},~\forall j\in[d]
; given any
j\in\mathcal{C}^{U}_{\ell}
with some
\ell
, there exists at least one
m\in\mathcal{C}^{U}_{\ell^{\prime}}
such that
\sigma^{U}_{jj}=\sigma^{U}_{mm}
for any
\ell^{\prime}\neq\ell
.
Denote
\widetilde{\sigma}^{U}_{jk}={\sigma}^{U}_{jk}/\sqrt{{\sigma}^{U}_{jj}{\sigma}^{U}_{kk}}
. And the general covariance condition says that there exists some
\sigma_{0}<1
such that
|\widetilde{\sigma}^{V}_{jk}|=|{\sigma}^{V}_{jk}|/\sqrt{{\sigma}^{V}_{jj}{\sigma}^{V}_{kk}}\leq\sigma_{0}
for any
j\neq k
and
|\{(j,k):j\neq k,|\widetilde{\sigma}^{U}_{jk}|=|{\sigma}^{U}_{jk}|/\sqrt{\sigma^{U}_{jj}\sigma^{U}_{kk}}>\sigma_{0}\}|\leq b_{0}
for some constant
b_{0}
.

Lemma B.15
.

For the term
\mathrm{II}_{1}=\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{-\beta(||Z||_{\infty}-|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(E_{1})}\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||Z||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right]
with
{E_{1}}
defined in (
B.20
) and
\epsilon={c}/{\max\{(\log d)^{3/2},\mathfrak{p}\log d\}}
for some small enough constant
c>0
,
whenever
t
satisfies
0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
for some constant
C_{0}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{II}_{1}}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}\epsilon\log d}{\beta\mathfrak{p}}\left(1+\frac{b_{0}}{\sqrt{1-(s+(1-s)\sigma_{0})^{2}}}\right).

(B.51)

for any
s\in(0,1)
, where
\sigma_{0}<1
and
b_{0}
are the constants in the assumption of Theorem
3.2
.

Remark B.16
.

Recall the definition of
Z=W(s)
. Hence the term
\mathrm{II}_{1}
depends on
s
. In Lemma
B.7
, we are able to derive a uniform upper bound when assuming the minimal eigenvalue condition as in Theorem
B.6
. Since Theorem
3.3
does not make assumptions about the minimal eigenvalue condition, we will bound the term
\mathrm{II}_{1}
differently and the upper bound depend on
s
, as showed in the following proof.

We basically use the same proof strategy as Lemma but will separately deal with two cases. First recall that

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}_{1}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{-\beta(||Z||_{\infty}-|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(||Z||_{\infty}>||G||_{\infty},||Z||_{\infty}>Z_{j})}\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||Z||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right].

We define
Z^{\dagger}=(Z_{k})_{k\in\mathcal{E}^{\dagger}}
where

\mathcal{E}^{\dagger}=\{j\}\cup\mathcal{E}_{G}\cup\{k\in[d]:|\widetilde{\sigma}^{U}_{jk}|\leq\sigma_{0},\max_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}\{|\widetilde{\sigma}^{U}_{mk}|\}\leq\sigma_{0}\}.

(B.52)

Under the condition of Theorem
3.3
, we have

|[d]\setminus\mathcal{E}^{\dagger}|\leq|\{(j,k):j\neq k,|\widetilde{\sigma}^{U}_{jk}|\}>\sigma_{0}\}|\leq b_{0}
for some constant
b_{0}
. Note we can write
1=\mathds{1}{(||Z^{\dagger}||_{\infty}=||Z||_{\infty})}+\sum_{k\in[d]\setminus\mathcal{E}^{\dagger}}\mathds{1}{(|Z_{k}|=||Z||_{\infty})}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{II}_{1}}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}\leq\frac{\mathrm{II}^{\dagger}_{1}}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}+b_{0}\cdot\max_{k\in[d]\setminus\mathcal{E}^{\dagger}}\frac{\mathrm{II}^{(k)}_{1}}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)},

(B.53)

where
\mathrm{II}^{\dagger}_{1}
and
\mathrm{II}^{(k)}_{1}
are defined as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{II}^{\dagger}_{1}:=&\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{-\beta(||Z^{\dagger}||_{\infty}-|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(||Z^{\dagger}||_{\infty}>||G||_{\infty},||Z^{\dagger}||_{\infty}>Z_{j})}\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq||Z^{\dagger}||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right],\\
\mathrm{II}^{(k)}_{1}:=&\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{-\beta(|Z_{k}|-|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(|Z_{k}|>||G||_{\infty},|Z_{k}|>Z_{j})}\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq|Z_{k}|\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right].\end{split}

(B.54)

Denote the conditional density function of
||Z^{\dagger}||_{\infty}\mid Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g
by
f^{\dagger}_{g,z_{j}}(u)
. Then we apply exactly the same derivations as in Lemma
B.7
(except that
f^{\dagger}_{g,z_{j}}(u)
is bounded using Lemma
B.17
instead of Lemma
B.9
) and obtain the following bound

\frac{\mathrm{II}^{\dagger}_{1}}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}\epsilon\log d}{\beta\mathfrak{p}}.

(B.55)

Regarding the term
\mathrm{II}^{(k)}_{1}
, we follow the same derivations as in the beginning of the proof of Lemma
B.7
. Specifically, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}^{(k)}_{1}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{-\beta(|Z_{k}|-|Z_{j}|)}\cdot\mathds{1}{(|Z_{k}|>||G||_{\infty},|Z_{k}|>Z_{j})}\cdot\mathds{1}{(t-\epsilon\leq|Z_{k}|\leq t+\epsilon)}}\right]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{\beta|Z_{j}|}\cdot\mathds{1}{(||G||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon,|Z_{j}|\leq t+\epsilon)}\left(\int_{t-\epsilon}^{t+\epsilon}f_{Z_{j}}(u)e^{-\beta u}\mathds{1}{(u>||G||_{\infty},u>|Z_{j}|)}du\right)}\right],

(B.56)

where
f_{Z_{j},G}(u)
denotes the conditional density of
Z_{k}
given
Z_{j},G
. Recall the construction of
G
described in the proof of Theorem
3.3
, we have for any
k\neq j,k\notin\mathcal{E}_{G}=\{m\in[d]:Z_{m}=G_{\ell}\text{ for some }\ell\in[\mathfrak{p}]\}
, there exists at most one
m\in\{j\}\cup\mathcal{E}_{G}
, such that
Z_{k}
and
Z_{m}
belong to the same component. Denote that random variable by
Z_{m_{0}}
, then
f_{Z_{j},G}(u)
is just the conditional density function of
Z_{k}
given
Z_{m_{0}}
. Since
Z
follows a multivariate Gaussian distribution, we can immediately figure out the expression of the conditional density
f_{Z_{m_{0}}}(u)
and simply derive a bound

\displaystyle f_{Z_{j},G}(u)=f_{Z_{m_{0}}}(u)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\mathrm{Var}\left({Z_{k}}\,|\,{Z_{m_{0}}}\right)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi(\sigma_{kk}-\sigma^{2}_{km_{0}}/\sigma_{m_{0}m_{0}}))}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\sigma_{kk}}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\sigma^{2}_{km_{0}}/(\sigma_{kk}\sigma_{m_{0}m_{0}})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi a_{0}}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\sigma^{2}_{km_{0}}/(\sigma_{kk}\sigma_{m_{0}m_{0}})},

(B.57)

where
\sigma_{kk}=\mathrm{Var}\left({Z_{k}}\right),\sigma_{m_{0}m_{0}}=\mathrm{Var}\left({Z_{m_{0}}}\right),\sigma_{km_{0}}=\mathrm{Cov}\left({Z_{k}},{Z_{m_{0}}}\right)
and we use the fact that

\sigma_{kk}=\mathrm{Var}\left({Z_{k}}\right)=\sigma^{U}_{kk}\geq a_{0}
(under the general variance assumption). Note
Z=\sqrt{s}U+\sqrt{1-s}V
, then we have
\sigma^{2}_{km_{0}}=(\mathrm{Cov}\left({Z_{k}},{Z_{m_{0}}}\right))^{2}=(s{\sigma}_{km_{0}}^{U}+(1-s){\sigma}_{km_{0}}^{V})^{2}
where
m_{0}\in\{j\}\cup\mathcal{E}_{G}
.
Since
|\widetilde{\sigma}_{km_{0}}^{U}|\leq 1
by definition and
|\widetilde{\sigma}_{km_{0}}^{V}|\leq\sigma_{0}
under the assumption of Theorem
3.3
, we have

(s{\sigma}_{km_{0}}^{U}+(1-s)\sigma_{km_{0}}^{V})^{2}/(\sigma_{kk}\sigma_{m_{0}m_{0}})=(s\widetilde{\sigma}_{km_{0}}^{U}+(1-s)\widetilde{\sigma}_{km_{0}}^{V})^{2}\leq(s+(1-s)\sigma_{0})^{2}.

(B.58)

Now we obtain a upper bound on the conditional density function
f_{Z_{j},G}(u)
based on (
B.57
) and (
B.58
). Combining this bound and following the same derivations as in Lemma
B.7
to deal with the term in (
B.56
), we establish the upper bound on the term
\mathrm{II}^{(k)}_{1}/\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)
for any
k\in[d]\setminus\mathcal{E}^{\dagger}
,

\frac{\mathrm{II}^{(k)}_{1}}{\mathbb{P}(||V||_{\infty}>t)}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}\epsilon\log d}{\beta\mathfrak{p}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-(s+(1-s)\sigma_{0})^{2}}}.

(B.59)

Combining (
B.53
), (
B.54
),(
B.55
) with (
B.59
), we derive the bound in (
B.51
).
∎

Lemma B.17
.

Recall that the density function of the conditional distribution of
||Z^{\dagger}||_{\infty}\mid\{Z_{j}=z_{j},G=g\}
is denoted by
f^{{\dagger}}_{g,z_{j}}(z)
where
Z^{\dagger}
is defined in …. Suppose
\epsilon>0
, when
0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
for some constant
C_{0}>0
and
|z_{j}|,||g||_{\infty}\leq t+\epsilon
, we have

f^{{\dagger}}_{g,z_{j}}(z)\leq C\sqrt{\log d},\quad\forall~z\in(\max\{|z_{j}|,||g||_{\infty}\},t+\epsilon].

(B.60)

where the finite constant
C
depends on
a_{0}
and
\sigma_{0}<1
.

Following exactly the same derivations as in Lemma
B.9
(up to (
B.45
)), we have

f^{{\dagger}}_{g,z_{j}}(z)\leq\frac{6}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}\left(\frac{2\bar{\rho}_{j}(1+c_{0})+1}{\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}}C_{1}\sqrt{\log d}+C_{2}\sqrt{\log d}\right),

(B.61)

for any
z\in(\max\{|z_{j}|,||g||_{\infty}\},t+\epsilon]
, where
C_{1},C_{2}
are some constants. First,
\bar{\rho}_{j}
is defined in (
B.37
). Simply, we have

\bar{\rho}_{j}\leq\max_{k\neq j}\frac{|\sigma_{jk}|}{\sigma_{jj}}\leq\frac{\max_{j}\sigma_{jj}^{U}}{\min_{j}\sigma_{jj}^{U}}\leq\frac{a_{1}}{a_{0}}

under the general variance assumption. Recall the construction of
G
described in the proof of Theorem
3.3
, we have for any
k\neq j,k\notin\mathcal{E}_{G}=\{m\in[d]:Z_{m}=G_{\ell}\text{ for some }\ell\in[\mathfrak{p}]\}
, there is at most one
m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}
, such that
Z_{k}
and
Z_{m}
belong to the same component. Then we have

\sum_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}\mathds{1}{(\sigma_{km}\neq 0)}\leq 1,

hence
c_{0}=1
by definition. Also note by the definition of
Z^{\dagger}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\dagger}
in (
B.52
), for any
k\in\mathcal{E}^{\dagger},k\neq j,k\neq\mathcal{E}_{G}
, we have

\max\{|\widetilde{\sigma}^{U}_{jk}|,|\widetilde{\sigma}^{V}_{jk}|\}\}\leq\sigma_{0},\quad\max_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}\{|\widetilde{\sigma}^{U}_{mk}|,|\widetilde{\sigma}^{V}_{mk}|\}\leq\sigma_{0}

(B.62)

under the assumption of Theorem
3.3
.
We will take advantage of this together with the above property of
G
to derive a bound on
\underline{\sigma_{\cdot j}}
. Similarly as in (
B.37
), we have
\underline{\sigma^{2}_{\cdot j}}:=\min_{k\in\mathcal{E}_{X}}\mathrm{Var}\left({Z_{k}}\,|\,{Z_{j},G}\right)
with
\mathcal{E}_{X}:=\{k\in\mathcal{E}^{\dagger}:k\neq j,k\notin\mathcal{E}_{G}\}
. For each
k\in\mathcal{E}_{X}
, we have it can at most belong to the same component as one of
\{j\}\cup\mathcal{E}_{G}
,
due to the property of
G
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left({Z_{k}}\,|\,{Z_{j},G}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\min\{\mathrm{Var}\left({Z_{k}}\,|\,{Z_{j}}\right),\min_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}\{\mathrm{Var}\left({Z_{k}}\,|\,{Z_{m}}\right)\}\}

(B.63)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\sigma_{kk}}\cdot\min\{1-\sigma_{jk}^{2}/(\sigma_{jj}\sigma_{kk}),\min_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}\{1-\sigma^{2}_{mk}/(\sigma_{mm}\sigma_{kk})\}\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle a_{0}\cdot\min\{1-\sigma_{jk}^{2}/(\sigma_{jj}\sigma_{kk}),\min_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}\{1-\sigma^{2}_{mk}/(\sigma_{mm}\sigma_{kk})\}\}.

since
(Z_{j},G)
are all independent and
\sigma_{kk}=\mathrm{Var}\left({Z_{k}}\right)=\sigma^{U}_{kk}\geq a_{0}
(under the general variance assumption). Recall the definition of
Z=\sqrt{s}U+\sqrt{1-s}V
, we have

|\sigma_{mk}|/\sqrt{\sigma_{mm}\sigma_{kk}}=|\mathrm{Cov}\left({Z_{k}},{Z_{m}}\right)|/\sqrt{\sigma_{mm}\sigma_{kk}}=|s\widetilde{\sigma}^{U}_{mk}+(1-s)\widetilde{\sigma}^{V}_{mk}|\leq\sigma_{0},\quad\forall~s\in[0,1],

(B.64)

when
m\in\{j\}\cup\mathcal{E}_{G}
. This is due to (
B.62
). Then we can derive a bound on
{1}/{\underline{\sigma^{2}_{\cdot j}}}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\underline{\sigma^{2}_{\cdot j}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\min_{k\in\mathcal{E}_{X}}\mathrm{Var}\left({Z_{k}}\,|\,{Z_{j},G}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a_{0}\min_{k\in\mathcal{E}_{X}}\min\{1-\sigma_{jk}^{2}/(\sigma_{jj}\sigma_{kk}),\min_{m\in\mathcal{E}_{G}}\{1-\sigma_{mk}^{2}/(\sigma_{mm}\sigma_{kk})\}\}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a_{0}(1-\sigma^{2}_{0})},

where the first inequality holds by (
B.63
) and the second equality holds by (
B.64
).
Combining the above bound with (
B.61
), we finally establish (
B.60
) for some finite constant
C
.
∎

C
Ancillary propositions for FDR control

Throughout this section, we introduce some new notations. For a given mean zero random vector
\bm{Y}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with positive semi-definite covariance matrix
\bm{\Sigma}^{Y}:=\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{Y}\bm{Y}^{\top}}\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, we denote its Gaussian counterpart by
\bm{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
(i.e.,
\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{Z}}\right]=\mathbf{0}
and its covariance matrix
\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{\top}}\right]:=\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}
equals
\bm{\Sigma}^{Y}=(\sigma_{jk}^{Y})_{1\leq j,k\leq d}
). Consider
n
i.i.d. copies of
\bm{Y}
, denoted by
\bm{Y}_{1},\cdots,\bm{Y}_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We define the maximum
T_{\bm{Y}}
and
T_{\bm{Z}}
as below,

T_{\bm{Y}}:=\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}\right\|_{\infty},\quad T_{\bm{Z}}:=||\bm{Z}||_{\infty},

(C.1)

where
q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Y}})
and
q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})
(
\alpha\in[0,1]
) are the corresponding upper quantile functions. Define the Gaussian multiplier bootstrap counterpart as

T_{\bm{W}}:=\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}\xi_{i}\right\|_{\infty},

(C.2)

where
\xi_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
and are independent from
\bm{Y}_{1},\cdots,\bm{Y}_{n}
. Let
q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}})
be the conditional quantile of
T_{\bm{W}}
, then we have
\mathbb{P}_{\xi}\left({T_{\bm{W}}\geq q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}})}\right)=\alpha
. Note that we use the
\xi
subscript to remind ourselves that the probability measure is induced by the multiplier random variables
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
conditional on
\{\bm{Y}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. And we have the covariance matrix of
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}\xi_{i}
(conditional on
\{\bm{Y}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
) equals
\bm{\Sigma}^{W}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i}\bm{Y}_{i}^{\top}
. Denote
\Delta_{\infty}=||\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}-\bm{\Sigma}^{W}||_{\infty}
, which measures the maximal differences between the true covariance matrix
\bm{\Sigma}^{Z}
and the sample version
\bm{\Sigma}^{W}
.

C.1
Cramér-type deviation bounds for the Gaussian multiplier bootstrap

Based on the Cramér-type Gaussian comparison bound in Theorem
3.1
, the Cramér-type approximation bound
[
43
]
, the maximal inequalities and a careful treatment to the comparison of quantiles, we will establish the Cramér-type deviation bounds for the Gaussian multiplier bootstrap (CGMB) in this section.

Proposition C.1

(CGMB)
.

Assuming the covariance matrix
\bm{\Sigma}^{Y}
satisfies
0<c_{1}\leq\sigma^{Y}_{jj}\leq c_{2}<\infty
, for any
j\in[d]
and
\bm{Y}
satisfies the tail condition that
\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\max_{1\leq j\leq p}||\bm{Y}_{ij}||_{\psi_{1}}\leq K_{3}
for some constants
c_{1},c_{2},K_{3}
, under the scaling condition
(\log ed)^{3}(\log(ed+n))^{56/3}/n=o(1)
, we have the following bound,

\sup_{\alpha\in[\alpha_{L},1]}\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}}))}{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}-1\right|=O\left(\frac{(\log d)^{11/6}}{n^{1/6}\alpha_{L}^{1/3}}+\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}\right),

(C.3)

where

\alpha_{L}
satisfies
q(\alpha_{L};T_{\bm{Z}})=O\left(\sqrt{\log d}\right)
and
\frac{\log^{11}d}{n\alpha_{L}}=O(1)
.

The proof can be found in Appendix
C.2
. In practice, there are many situations where the relevant statistics come from the maxima of approximated averages. In particular, the test statistics in our node selection problem can not be directly expressed as maxima of scaled averages, but can be approximated by a
T_{\bm{Y}}
-like term with the approximation error suitably controlled. Therefore, we also prove an extended version of Proposition
C.1
. Suppose the statistics of interest and its Gaussian multiplier bootstrap counterpart, denoted by
T
and
T^{\mathcal{B}}
respectively, can be approximated by
T_{\bm{Y}}
(defined in (
C.1
)) and
T_{\bm{W}}
(defined in (
C.2
)). The quantile functions
q(\alpha;T)
and
q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}})
are defined correspondingly.

Proposition C.2

(CGMB with approximation)
.

Under the same conditions as in Proposition
C.1
and the additional assumption about the differences between the maximum statistics:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|T-T_{\bm{Y}}|>\zeta_{1})<\zeta_{2},

(C.4)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(|T^{\mathcal{B}}-T_{\bm{W}}|>\zeta_{1})>\zeta_{2})<\zeta_{2},

(C.5)

where
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\geq 0
characterize the approximation error and satisfy
\zeta_{1}\log d=O(1),\zeta_{2}=O(\alpha_{L})
, we have the following Cramér-type deviation bound

\sup_{\alpha\in[\alpha_{L},1]}\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(T>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}}))}{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}-1\right|=\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L}),

(C.6)

where
\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})=O\left(\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}+\frac{(\log d)^{11/6}}{n^{1/6}\alpha_{L}^{1/3}}+\zeta_{1}\log d+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{\alpha_{L}}\right)
.

C.2
Proof of Proposition
C.1

Before proving Proposition
C.1
, we present Lemma
C.3
. It bounds the conditional quantile
q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}})
in terms of the quantile
q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})
of the Gaussian maxima
T_{\bm{Z}}
when the maximal covariance matrix differences are controlled. In the proof of Lemma
C.3
, we apply the
Cramér-type comparison bound (
3.1
), which is establised in Theorem
3.1
. To simplify the notation, we denote the bound
C_{1}(\log d)^{5/2}\Delta_{\infty}^{1/2}
in (
3.1
) by
\pi(\Delta_{\infty})
, where the constant
C_{1}
only depends on
\min_{1\leq j\leq d}\{\sigma^{U}_{jj},\sigma^{V}_{jj}\}
,
\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\{\sigma^{U}_{jj},\sigma^{V}_{jj}\}
.

Lemma C.3
.

Suppose
\delta
satisfies
(\log d)^{5}\delta=O(1)
. On the event
\{\Delta_{\infty}\leq\delta\}
, we have

\displaystyle{q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}})\geq q\Big(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)},

(C.7)

\displaystyle{q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}})\leq q\Big(\frac{\alpha}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)}.

(C.8)

On the event
\{\Delta_{\infty}\leq\delta\}
, we have
(\log d)^{5}\Delta_{\infty}\leq(\log d)^{5}\delta=O(1)
, then by applying Theorem
3.1
to
\bm{Z}
and
\bm{W}
, we obtain the following,

\sup_{0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}}\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(T_{\bm{W}}>t)}{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>t)}-1\right|\leq\pi(\delta).

Therefore we have

\mathbb{P}_{\xi}\left({T_{\bm{W}}\geq q\Big(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)}\right)\geq\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}}\geq q\Big(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)}\right)\cdot(1-\pi(\delta))=\alpha,

when
t
satisfies
0\leq t\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}
. Then
q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}})\geq q\Big(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)
immediately follows, i.e., (
C.7
) holds. Similarly, on the event
\{\Delta_{\infty}\leq\delta\}
, we have

\mathbb{P}_{\xi}\left({T_{\bm{W}}\geq q\Big(\frac{\alpha}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}}\geq q\Big(\frac{\alpha}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)}\right)\cdot(1+\pi(\delta))=\alpha.

Thus
q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}})\leq q\Big(\frac{\alpha}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)
, i.e., (
C.8
) holds.
∎

By the triangle inequality, we have

\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}}))}{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}-1\right|\leq\underbrace{\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}-1\right|}_{\mathrm{I}}+\underbrace{\frac{\left|{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}}))}-\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))\right|}{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}}_{\mathrm{II}}.

(C.9)

Regarding the first term
\mathrm{I}
, we will directly apply Corollary 5.1 in
[
43
]
. Specifically, we verify the tail assumption on
\bm{Y}
and the condition on the quantile that
{q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})}\leq q(\alpha_{L};T_{\bm{Z}})=O\left(\sqrt{\log d}\right)
when
\alpha\in[\alpha_{L},1]
. Then we obtain the following bound

\mathrm{I}=\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}{\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})}\right)}-1\right|=O\left(\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}\right).

(C.10)

Regarding the second term
\mathrm{II}
, we write it as

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\left|{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}}))}-\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{P}(\{T_{\bm{Y}}>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}})\}\ominus\{T_{\bm{Y}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})\})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\Big(\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}}),T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))+\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}}),T_{\bm{Y}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}}),T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}),\Delta_{\infty}\leq\delta)

\displaystyle+~\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}}),T_{\bm{Y}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}),\Delta_{\infty}\leq\delta)+\frac{2\mathbb{P}\left({\Delta_{\infty}>\delta}\right)}{\alpha},

where the first inequality holds by the definition of the symmetric difference; recall the symmetric difference between
A
and
B
is defined as
A\ominus B=(A\setminus B)\cup(B\setminus A)
). Remark that we will give the explicit choice of
\delta
later in the proof. Now we apply Lemma
C.3
(whose condition will be verified in (
C.16
)) and further bound
\mathrm{II}
as,

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\Big(\mathbb{P}\big(T_{\bm{Y}}\geq q\big(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big),T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})\big)

(C.11)

\displaystyle\quad+~\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q\big(\frac{\alpha}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big),T_{\bm{Y}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))\Big)+\frac{2\mathbb{P}\left({\Delta_{\infty}>\delta}\right)}{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}~{\mathbb{P}\left({q\big(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)\leq T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q\big(\frac{\alpha}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)}\right)}+\frac{2\mathbb{P}\left({\Delta_{\infty}>\delta}\right)}{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}~{\mathbb{P}\left({q\big(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)\leq T_{\bm{Z}}\leq q\big(\frac{\alpha}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)}\right)}+\frac{2\mathbb{P}\left({\Delta_{\infty}>\delta}\right)}{\alpha}+\mathrm{III}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi(\delta)}{1-\pi^{2}(\delta)}+\frac{2\mathbb{P}\left({\Delta_{\infty}>\delta}\right)}{\alpha}+\mathrm{III},

(C.12)

where the term
\mathrm{III}
in the second inequality is defined as,

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}:=\frac{1}{\alpha}\left|\mathbb{P}\left({q\big(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)\leq T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q\big(\frac{\alpha}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({q\big(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)\leq T_{\bm{Z}}\leq q\big(\frac{\alpha}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)}\right)\right|.

Below we further rewrite
\mathrm{III}
as

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}=\frac{1}{\alpha}\left|\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)}\cdot\mathrm{III}_{1}-\frac{\alpha}{1+\pi(\delta)}\cdot\mathrm{III}_{2}\right|,

with
\mathrm{III}_{1},\mathrm{III}_{2}
defined as

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}}>q\big(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}}>q\big(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)}\right)}{\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}}>q\big(\frac{\alpha}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)}\right)},

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}}>q\big(\frac{\alpha}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}}>q\big(\frac{\alpha}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)}\right)}{\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}}>q\big(\frac{\alpha}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)}\right)}.

Thus by applying Corollary 5.1 of
[
43
]
to
\mathrm{III}_{1},\mathrm{III}_{2}
similarly as in (
C.10
), we have the following bound on
\mathrm{III}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}=O\left(\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}\right).

(C.13)

Combining (
C.12
) and (
C.13
) yields the following bound,

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{P}(\{T_{\bm{Y}}>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}})\}\ominus\{T_{\bm{Y}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})\})

(C.14)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\frac{C(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}}+C_{0}^{\prime}\pi(\delta)+{\frac{C^{\prime\prime}~\mathbb{P}\left({\Delta_{\infty}>\delta}\right)}{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\frac{C(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}}+C^{\prime}(\log d)^{5/2}\delta^{1/2}+{\frac{C^{\prime\prime}~\mathbb{E}\left[{\Delta_{\infty}}\right]}{\delta\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O\left(\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\Delta_{\infty}}\right]\log^{5}d}{\alpha}\right)^{1/3}+\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}\right),

where the second inequality holds due to the definition of
\pi(\delta)
and Markov’s inequality, the last line holds by choosing
\delta
to be
(\mathbb{E}\left[{\Delta_{\infty}}\right])^{2/3}/(\alpha^{1/3}(\log d)^{5/3})
. We will bound the term
\mathbb{E}\left[{\Delta_{\infty}}\right]
using Lemma C.1 in
[
17
]
. Specifically, under the stated tail assumption on
\bm{Y}
, the condition (E.1) of Lemma C.1 in
[
17
]
is satisfied; see Comment 2.2 in
[
17
]
. Thus we have

\mathbb{E}\left[{\Delta_{\infty}}\right]\leq\sqrt{\frac{B_{n}^{2}\log d}{n}}\vee\frac{B_{n}^{2}(\log(dn))^{2}(\log d)}{n},

(C.15)

where
B_{n}
equals some constant
C
which does not depend on
n
. As promised previously, we
verify the assumption of Lemma
C.3
for our choice of
\delta
. Specifically, for
\delta=(\mathbb{E}\left[{\Delta_{\infty}}\right])^{2/3}/(\alpha^{1/3}(\log d)^{5/3})
, we have
(\log d)^{5}\delta
satisfies the following

(\log d)^{5}\delta\leq\frac{(\log d)^{5}(\mathbb{E}\left[{\Delta_{\infty}}\right])^{2/3}}{\alpha_{L}^{1/3}(\log d)^{5/3}}=\left(\frac{\log^{11}d}{n\alpha_{L}}\right)^{1/3}=O(1),

(C.16)

under the stated condition on
\alpha_{L}
.
Finally, when
\alpha\in[\alpha_{L},1]
, we combine (
C.9
), (
C.10
), (
C.14
) with (
C.15
), then establish (
C.3
), i.e.,

\sup_{\alpha\in[\alpha_{L},1]}\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T_{\bm{W}}))}{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}-1\right|=O\left(\frac{(\log d)^{11/6}}{n^{1/6}\alpha_{L}^{1/3}}+\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}\right).

∎

C.3
Proof of Proposition
C.2

Before proving Proposition
C.2
, we need to present a simple lemma. It translates the approximation error
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}
into the bounds on the quantiles. And its proof is quite straightforward thus omitted.

Lemma C.4
.

Under the assumption in (
C.5
), we have, for
\alpha\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}})\leq q_{\xi}(\alpha+\zeta_{2};T_{\bm{W}})+\zeta_{1})\geq 1-\zeta_{2},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}})\geq q_{\xi}(\alpha-\zeta_{2};T_{\bm{W}})-\zeta_{1})\geq 1-\zeta_{2}.

By the triangle inequality, we have

\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(T>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}}))}{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}-1\right|\leq\underbrace{\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}-1\right|}_{\mathrm{I}}+\underbrace{\frac{|\mathbb{P}(T>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}}))-\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))|}{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}}_{\mathrm{II}}.

(C.17)

Note that (
C.10
) in the proof of Proposition
C.1
immediately gives the bound on
\mathrm{I}
, i.e.,

\mathrm{I}=O\Big(\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}\Big).

(C.18)

Regarding the term
\mathrm{II}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\left|\mathbb{P}(T>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}}))-\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))\right|

(C.19)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\left|\mathbb{P}(\{T>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}})\}\ominus\{T_{\bm{Y}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})\})\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{P}(T>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}}),T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))+\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{P}(T\leq q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}}),T_{\bm{Y}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})).

To bound the two terms in (
C.19
), first notice that on the event
|T-T_{\bm{Y}}|>\zeta_{1}
, we have

\{T>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}}),T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})\}\subset\{T_{\bm{Y}}>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}})-\zeta_{1},T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})\}.

Then under the assumption in (
C.4
), i.e.,
\mathbb{P}(|T-T_{\bm{Y}}|>\zeta_{1})<\zeta_{2}
, we obtain

\mathbb{P}(T>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}}),T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))\leq\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}})-\zeta_{1},T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))+\zeta_{2}.

Applying such strategies to the second term in

(
C.19
)
similarly, we get the following,

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}_{1}+\mathrm{II}_{2}+\frac{2\zeta_{2}}{\alpha},\quad\text{where}

(C.20)

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}_{1}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}})-\zeta_{1},T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})),

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}_{2}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}})+\zeta_{2},T_{\bm{Y}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})).

Under the assumption (
C.5
), by Lemma
C.4
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}})\leq q_{\xi}(\alpha+\zeta_{2};T_{\bm{W}})+\zeta_{1})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-\zeta_{2},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}})\geq q_{\xi}(\alpha-\zeta_{2};T_{\bm{W}})-\zeta_{1})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-\zeta_{2}.

Hence we can bound
\mathrm{II}_{1},\mathrm{II}_{2}
as below,

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}}>q_{\xi}(\alpha-\zeta_{2};T_{\bm{W}})-2\zeta_{1},T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})}\right)+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{\alpha},

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q_{\xi}(\alpha+\zeta_{2};T_{\bm{W}})+2\zeta_{1},T_{\bm{Y}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})}\right)+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{\alpha}.

Now we will use the strategy of deriving (
C.11
) in the proof of Proposition
C.1
, i.e., apply Lemma
C.3
, then we have,

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha-\zeta_{2}}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)-2\zeta_{1},T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})}\right)+\frac{\mathbb{P}\left({\Delta_{\infty}>\delta}\right)}{\alpha}+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{\alpha},

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}}\leq q\Big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)+2\zeta_{1},T_{\bm{Y}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}})}\right)+\frac{\mathbb{P}\left({\Delta_{\infty}>\delta}\right)}{\alpha}+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{\alpha}.

Combining the above two inequalities with (
C.20
), we have

\mathrm{II}\leq\mathrm{III}+\frac{2\mathbb{P}\left({\Delta_{\infty}>\delta}\right)}{\alpha}+\frac{4\zeta_{2}}{\alpha},

(C.21)

where
\mathrm{III}
is defined as below,

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}:=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\left|\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha-\zeta_{2}}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)-2\zeta_{1}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)+2\zeta_{1}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\Big|\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha-\zeta_{2}}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)-2\zeta_{1}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha-\zeta_{2}}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)-2\zeta_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Y}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)+2\zeta_{1}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)+2\zeta_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha-\zeta_{2}}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)-2\zeta_{1}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)+2\zeta_{1}}\right)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{1}+\mathrm{III}_{2}+\mathrm{III}_{3}.

The last line comes from the triangle inequality, with
\mathrm{III}_{1},\mathrm{III}_{2},\mathrm{III}_{3}
defined as,

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{1}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Y}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)+2\zeta_{1}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Z}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)+2\zeta_{1}\Big)\Big|,

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{2}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Y}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha-\zeta_{2}}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)-2\zeta_{1}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Z}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha-\zeta_{2}}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)-2\zeta_{1}\Big)\Big|,

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{3}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Z}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha-\zeta_{2}}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)-2\zeta_{1}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Z}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)+2\zeta_{1}\Big)\Big|.

We first bound
\mathrm{III}_{3}
by the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Z}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha-\zeta_{2}}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)-2\zeta_{1}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Z}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)+2\zeta_{1}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{1}{\alpha}\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Z}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)+2\zeta_{1}\Big)-\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)}\Big|}_{\mathrm{III}_{31}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{1}{\alpha}\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Z}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha-\zeta_{2}}{1-\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)-2\zeta_{1}\Big)-\frac{\alpha-\zeta_{2}}{1-\pi(\delta)}\Big|}_{\mathrm{III}_{32}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{\alpha}\Big|\frac{\alpha-\zeta_{2}}{1-\pi(\delta)}-\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)}\Big|}_{\mathrm{III}_{33}}.

Note that
\mathrm{III}_{31}
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{31}=~

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{\alpha(1+\pi(\delta))}\cdot\frac{\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Z}}>q\big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)+2\zeta_{1}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}}>q\big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)}\right)\Big|}{\mathbb{P}\left({T_{\bm{Z}}>q\big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)}\right)}

(C.22)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{\alpha(1+\pi(\delta))}\cdot K_{4}\zeta_{1}\Big(q\big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)+\zeta_{1}\Big)\leq C\zeta_{1}\log d,

(C.23)

where the first inequality holds by applying a non-uniform anti-concentration bound. Specifically, we apply the part
3
of Theorem 2.1 in
[
43
]
(with
r-\epsilon=q\big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big),r+\epsilon=q\big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)+2\zeta_{1}
) to the Gaussian random vector
\bm{Z}
. Remark that the term
K_{3}
is a constant only depending on
\min_{1\leq j\leq d}\{\sigma^{Y}_{jj}\},\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\{\sigma^{Y}_{jj}\}
and the median of Gaussian maxima (up to 2-nd power, hence at most of rate
O({\log d})
). As for the second inequality, under the assumption
\zeta_{2}=O(\alpha_{L})
, we have
\frac{\zeta_{2}}{\alpha}\leq\frac{\zeta_{2}}{\alpha_{L}}=O(1)
when
\alpha\in[\alpha_{L},1]
; we also use the fact that
\zeta_{1}=O(\sqrt{\log d})
(which holds under the stated assumption), and
q\big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)=O(\sqrt{\log d})
(which will be verified later in (
C.27
)). Thus we show
\mathrm{III}_{31}=O(\zeta_{1}\log d)
. Similarly,
\mathrm{III}_{32}
can be bounded as
O(\zeta_{1}\log d)
. As for
\mathrm{III}_{33}
, we have

\mathrm{III}_{33}=\frac{1}{\alpha}\Big|\frac{\alpha-\zeta_{2}}{1-\pi(\delta)}-\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)}\Big|\leq\frac{2\pi(\delta)}{1-\pi^{2}(\delta)}+\frac{2\zeta_{2}}{\alpha(1-\pi^{2}(\delta))}.

Thus by combining the bounds on
\mathrm{III}_{31},\mathrm{III}_{32},\mathrm{III}_{33}
, we obtain

\mathrm{III}_{3}\leq\mathrm{III}_{31}+\mathrm{III}_{32}+\mathrm{III}_{33}\leq C^{\prime}\zeta_{1}\log d+\frac{2\pi(\delta)}{1-\pi^{2}(\delta)}+\frac{2\zeta_{2}}{\alpha(1-\pi^{2}(\delta))}.

(C.24)

Regarding the term
\mathrm{III}_{1}
, we first consider the following,

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{11}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Z}}>q\big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)+2\zeta_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Z}}>q\big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)\Big)\cdot\left(1+K_{4}\zeta_{1}\Big(q\big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)+\zeta_{1}\Big)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{\alpha(1+\pi(\delta))}\cdot\left(1+K_{4}\zeta_{1}\Big(q\big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)+\zeta_{1}\Big)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{\prime\prime}+C\zeta_{1}\log d=O(1),

where the first inequality holds due to the derivations from (
C.22
) to (
C.23
), the second inequality holds due to the last inequality in (
C.23
) and the stated assumption
\zeta_{2}=O(\alpha_{L})
.
Then we bound
\mathrm{III}_{1}
in terms of
\mathrm{III}_{11}
and write

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Y}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)+2\zeta_{1}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Z}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)+2\zeta_{1}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{11}\cdot\Bigg|\frac{\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Y}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)+2\zeta_{1}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Z}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)+2\zeta_{1}\Big)}{\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{\bm{Z}}>q\Big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\Big)+2\zeta_{1}\Big)}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{11}\cdot\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}=O\Big(\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}\Big),

where the inequality holds by applying Corollary 5.1 in
[
43
]
again to
T_{\bm{Y}}
as the derivations of (
C.10
) in the proof of Proposition
C.1
. The term
\mathrm{III}_{2}
can be similarly bounded as
\mathrm{III}_{1}
. Combining the above bounds on
\mathrm{III}_{1},\mathrm{III}_{2}
and (
C.24
) yields the following bound on
\mathrm{III}
,

\mathrm{III}\leq\frac{C(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}+C^{\prime}\zeta_{1}\log d+\frac{2\pi(\delta)}{1-\pi^{2}(\delta)}+\frac{2\zeta_{2}}{\alpha(1-\pi^{2}(\delta))}.

(C.25)

By (
C.17
), (
C.18
), (
C.21
) and (
C.25
), we have, when
\alpha\in[\alpha_{L},1]
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(T>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}}))}{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}-1\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}+\mathrm{II}\leq\mathrm{I}+\mathrm{III}+\frac{2\mathbb{P}\left({\Delta_{\infty}>\delta}\right)}{\alpha}+\frac{4\zeta_{2}}{\alpha}

(C.26)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}+C^{\prime}\zeta_{1}\log d+\frac{C^{\prime\prime}\zeta_{2}}{\alpha}+\frac{2\pi(\delta)}{1-\pi^{2}(\delta)}+\frac{2\mathbb{P}\left({\Delta_{\infty}>\delta}\right)}{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}+C^{\prime}\zeta_{1}\log d+\frac{C^{\prime\prime}\zeta_{2}}{\alpha}+\frac{C(\log d)^{11/6}}{n^{1/6}\alpha_{L}^{1/3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O\left(\frac{(\log d)^{19/6}}{n^{1/6}}+\frac{(\log d)^{11/6}}{n^{1/6}\alpha_{L}^{1/3}}+\zeta_{1}\log d+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{\alpha_{L}}\right).

where the third line holds due to the derivations between (
C.13
) and (
C.16
) in the proof of Proposition
C.1
. Remark by the choice of
\delta
and (
C.16
), we have
\pi(\delta)=O(1)
. Also note that
\zeta_{2}=O(\alpha_{L})
, hence we can show

q\big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)=O(\sqrt{\log d}).

(C.27)

when
\alpha\in[\alpha_{L},1]
. Hence we are able to verify
q\big(\frac{\alpha+\zeta_{2}}{1+\pi(\delta)};T_{\bm{Z}}\big)=O(\sqrt{\log d})
, as promised when deriving (
C.23
). Denoting the bound in (
C.26
) by
\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L})
, we finally establish (
C.6
), i.e.,

\sup_{\alpha\in[\alpha_{L},1]}\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(T>q_{\xi}(\alpha;T^{\mathcal{B}}))}{\mathbb{P}(T_{\bm{Z}}>q(\alpha;T_{\bm{Z}}))}-1\right|=\eta(d,n,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\alpha_{L}).

∎

D
Validity and power analysis of single node testing

In this section, we focus on Lemma
A.2
and Lemma
C.4
. Note that these results are established using the same strategies as Theorem 4.1, Lemma S.1 and Theorem S.7 in
[
57
]
. We still present their proofs for completeness.

D.1
Proof of Lemma
A.2

For given node
j
, we denote
N_{0j}=\{(j,k):\bm{\Theta}_{jk}=0\}
, then

N_{0j}^{c}=\{(j,k):|\bm{\Theta}_{jk}|>0\}
. First we
consider the following event,

\mathcal{E}=\Big\{\min_{e\in N_{0j}^{c}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{e}|>\widehat{c}(\alpha,E_{0})\Big\},\quad\text{ where }E_{0}=\{(j,k):k\neq j,k\in[d]\}.

By the definition of Algorithm
1
, we immediately have the rejected edge set in the first iteration can be written as

E_{1}=\{(j,k)\in E_{0}:\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jk}|>\widehat{c}(\alpha,E_{0})\}.

Regarding (i) i.e., under the alternative hypothesis
H_{1j}:\|\bm{\Theta}_{j,-j}\|_{0}\geq k_{\tau}
, we first note
\psi_{j,\alpha}=1
on the event
\mathcal{E}
. Also notice that
N_{0j}^{c}\subseteq E_{1}
given
\mathcal{E}
. Then the following bound immediately follows:

\mathbb{P}\left({\psi_{j,\alpha}=1}\right)\geq\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{E}}\right).

(D.1)

We further derive a lower bound for
\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{E}}\right)
by the triangle inequality:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{E}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left({\min_{e\in N_{0j}^{c}}|\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{e}|>\frac{\widehat{c}(\alpha,E_{0})}{\sqrt{n}}+C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}\text{ and }||\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}-\bm{\Theta}^{\star}||_{\max}\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}}\right).

(D.2)

For any fixed
\alpha\in(0,1)
, we consider sufficiently large
d
such that
1/d\leq\alpha
. By applying Lemma
D.1
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(T^{\mathcal{B}}_{E_{0}}\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\leq 1/d\right)\geq 1-1/d^{2},

where
E_{0}=\{(j,k):k\neq j,k\in[d]\}
. Recall the definition of
\widehat{c}(\alpha,E)

\widehat{c}(\alpha,E)=\inf\left\{t\in\mathbb{R}:\mathbb{P}_{\xi}\left(T^{\mathcal{B}}_{E}\leq t\right)\geq 1-\alpha\right\}.

We then have

\widehat{c}(\alpha,E_{0})\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}

for some constant
C_{0}>0
, with probability greater than
1-1/d^{2}
.
Choosing the constant in the signal strength condition of Lemma
A.2
to be
2C_{0}

(i.e., for any
(j,k)\in N_{0j}^{c}
,
|\bm{\Theta}_{jk}|\geq 2C_{0}\sqrt{\log d/n}
)
and applying (
D.36
),
we have with probability greater than
1-1/d^{2}

\displaystyle\min_{e\in N_{0j}^{c}}|\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{e}|\geq 2C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}\geq\frac{\widehat{c}(\alpha,E_{0})}{\sqrt{n}}+C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}\text{~~and~}

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\left({||\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}-\bm{\Theta}^{\star}||_{\max}\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}}\right)\geq 1-2/d^{2}.

Combining the above two inequalities with (
D.1
) and (
D.2
), we have
{\mathbb{P}\left({\psi_{j,\alpha}=1}\right)\geq\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{E}}\right)>1-3/d^{2}}
.
Therefore, we establish

\lim_{(n,d)\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\left({\psi_{j,\alpha}=1}\right)=1.

Now we consider (ii), i.e., the case when
\|\bm{\Theta}_{j,-j}\|_{0}<k_{\tau}
. Since
\|\bm{\Theta}_{j,-j}\|_{0}\leq k_{\tau}-1
,
\psi_{j,\alpha}=1
implies at least one edge in
N_{0j}
is rejected in Algorithm
1
. Suppose the first rejected edge in
N_{0j}
is
(j,k_{\ast})
and it is rejected at the
t_{\ast}
-th iteration. Then we have
N_{0j}\subseteq E_{t_{\ast}-1}
and

\max_{e\in N_{0j}}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{e}|\geq\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jk_{\ast}}-\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{jk_{\ast}}|\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,E_{t_{\ast}-1})\geq\widehat{c}(\alpha,N_{0j}),

(D.3)

where the first inequality holds since
(j,k_{\ast})\subset N_{0j}
, the
second inequality holds since
\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{jk_{\ast}}=0
and the edge
(j,k_{\ast})
is rejected at the
t_{\ast}
-th iteration. The last inequality holds simply because
N_{0j}\subseteq E_{t_{\ast}-1}
.
Therefore by applying Lemma
A.1
with
E
chosen to be
N_{0j}
, we have

\lim_{(n,d)\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\left({\psi_{j,\alpha}=1}\right)\leq\alpha.

∎

Lemma D.1
.

Under the same conditions as Lemma
A.1
, we have for any
j\in[d]
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(T^{\mathcal{B}}_{E_{j}}\geq 2C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\leq 2/d\right)\geq 1-1/d^{2}

(D.4)

holds for some constant
C_{0}>0
, where
E_{j}:=\{(j,k):k\neq j,k\in[d]\}
.

Recall the definitions of
T_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}
and
\breve{T}_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}
in (
A.5
) and (
A.7
) respectively.
First, we have

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(T^{\mathcal{B}}_{E_{j}}\geq 2C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\geq 2/d\right)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(\breve{T}_{E_{j}}^{\mathcal{B}}+T^{\mathcal{B}}_{E_{j}}-\breve{T}_{E_{j}}^{\mathcal{B}}\geq 2C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\geq 2/d\right)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(\breve{T}_{E_{j}}^{\mathcal{B}}\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})+\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(|T^{\mathcal{B}}_{E_{j}}-\breve{T}_{E_{j}}^{\mathcal{B}}|\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\geq 2/d\right)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(\breve{T}_{E_{j}}^{\mathcal{B}}\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\geq 1/d\right)

\displaystyle~+\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(|T^{\mathcal{B}}_{E_{j}}-\breve{T}_{E_{j}}^{\mathcal{B}}|\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\geq 1/d\right)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(\breve{T}_{E_{j}}^{\mathcal{B}}\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\geq 1/d\right)+1/d^{2},

(D.5)

where the first and second inequalities hold due to the union bound, and the last inequality holds due to (
D.33
) in the proof of Lemma
A.1
.
Now it suffices to prove

\max_{j\in[d]}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(\breve{T}_{E_{j}}^{\mathcal{B}}\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\geq 1/d\right)\leq 1/d^{2}.

(D.6)

Then we notice the following,

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}_{\xi}\big(\breve{T}_{E_{j}}^{\mathcal{B}}\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\big)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}_{\xi}\Big(\underset{(j,k)\in E_{j}}{\max}\;\frac{1}{\sqrt{n~{{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}{{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\left(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\right)}\xi_{i}\bigg|\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle~\sum_{(j,k)\in E_{j}}\mathbb{P}_{\xi}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n~{{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}{{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\left(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\right)}\xi_{i}\bigg|\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\Big).

(D.7)

where the equality holds by the definition of
\breve{T}^{\mathcal{B}}_{E_{j}}
in (
A.5
) and the inequality holds by the union bound. In the following, we will bound (
D.7
) for each
(j,k)\in E_{j}
. Note that conditioning on
\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, the following random variable is a mean zero Gaussian random variable

G_{jk}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n~{{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\left(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\right)}\xi_{i}.

Hence we will bound its conditional variance then apply the sub-Gaussian tail probability bound (in Section 2.1.2 of
[
83
]
).
Specifically, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\mathrm{Var}(G_{jk}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})>2C_{0}\big)=

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\big((\bm{\Theta}_{jj}\bm{\Theta}_{kk})^{-1}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[{{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k})}\big]^{2}>2C_{0}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\big((\bm{\Theta}_{jj}\bm{\Theta}_{kk})^{-1}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[{{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k})}\big]^{2}>C_{0}+C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~1/d^{2},

for some constant
C_{0}>0
, where the equality holds by the definition of
G_{jk}
, the first inequality holds under the scaling condition of Lemma
A.1
, and the last inequality holds due to the Bernstein’s inequality (in Section 2.2.2 of
[
81
]
) and the assumption that
\bm{\Theta}\in\mathcal{U}(M,s,r_{0})
and
\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}N_{d}(0,\bm{\Sigma})
.
Therefore, we have with probability greater than
1-1/d^{2}
,

\mathbb{P}_{\xi}\left(G_{jk}\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\right)\leq\frac{1}{d^{2}}

for some constant
C_{0}>0
by the sub-Gaussian tail probability bound (in Section 2.1.2 of
[
83
]
). Combining the above bound with (
D.7
), we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}\big(\breve{T}^{\mathcal{B}}_{E_{j}}\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\big)\leq d\cdot\frac{1}{d^{2}}\right)\geq 1-1/d^{2}

since
|E_{j}|\leq d
. The above derivations hold for any
j\in[d]
, thus (
D.6
) is established. Finally, combining (
D.6
) with (
D.5
) yields (
D.4
).
∎

D.2
Proof of Lemma
A.1

We first recall the definition of
\mathcal{U}(M,s,r_{0})
and write down the statement of Lemma
A.1
below.

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}(M,s,r_{0})

\displaystyle=\Big\{\bm{\Theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\,\big|\,\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Theta})\geq 1/r_{0},\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Theta})\leq r_{0},\max_{j\in[d]}\|\bm{\Theta}_{j}\|_{0}\leq s,\|\bm{\Theta}\|_{1}\leq M\Big\}.

(D.8)

Lemma D.2
.

Suppose that
\bm{\Theta}\in\mathcal{U}(M,s,r_{0})
. If
(\log(dn))^{7}/n+s^{2}(\log dn)^{4}/{n}=o(1)
, for any edge set
E\subseteq\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{V}
, we have for any
\alpha\in[0,1]
,

\lim_{(n,d)\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{\bm{\Theta}\in\mathcal{U}(M,s,r_{0})}\sup_{\alpha\in(0,1)}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{e\in E}\sqrt{n}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{e}-\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{e}|>\widehat{c}(\alpha,E)\right)-\alpha\right|=0.

(D.9)

Throughout the following parts, we will write the standardized one-step estimator explicitly:

\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jk}/\sqrt{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jj}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{kk}},\quad\text{ where }\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jk}:=\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{jk}-\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\right)}{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{j}}.

In order to prove (
D.9
), we need preliminary results on the estimation rates of CLIME estimator.
[
10
]
gives the following theorem. We can also prove the same result for the GLasso estimator
[
36
]
. Therefore, Lemma
D.2
applies for both the CLIME estimator and the GLasso estimator. This also implies that the results in our paper apply to both the CLIME estimator and the GLasso estimator.

Lemma D.3
.

Suppose
\bm{\Theta}\in\mathcal{U}(M,s,r_{0})
and we choose the tuning parameter
\lambda\geq CM\sqrt{\log d/n}
in the CLIME estimator. With probability greater than
1-c/d^{2}
, we have the following bounds:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&~||\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}||_{\max}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}},||\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}-\mathbf{I}||_{\max}\leq CM\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}},\text{ and }\\
&~||\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}-\bm{\Theta}||_{\max}\leq CM\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}},||\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}-\bm{\Theta}||_{1}\leq CM\sqrt{\frac{s^{2}\log d}{n}},\end{split}

(D.10)

where
C
is a universal constant only depending on
r_{0}
in (
D.8
).

Remark D.4
.

Note the first inequality in (
D.10
) directly follows from Equation (26) in
[
10
]
, the second inequality follows from the constraint in the CLIME estimator and the third inequality holds due to Theorem 6 in
[
10
]
.

Given a random variable
Z
, we define its
\psi_{\ell}
-norm for
\ell\geq 1
as
\|Z\|_{\psi_{\ell}}=\sup_{p\geq 1}p^{-1/\ell}(\mathbb{E}|Z|^{p})^{1/p}
. The following lemma controls the
\psi_{\ell}
-norm of
\bm{X}
and gives the lower bound of the variance of the debiased estimator.

Lemma D.5
.

There exist universal constants
c
and
C
only depending on
r_{0}
in (
D.8
) such that

\sup_{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}\|\mathbf{v}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{X}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C\text{ and }\min_{j,k\in[d]}\mathbb{E}[(\bm{\Theta}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}-\bm{\Sigma})\bm{\Theta}_{k})^{2}]\geq c.

(D.11)

The first inequality in (
D.11
) immediately follows since

\mathbf{v}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{X}\sim N(0,1)
for any
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1
.
Regarding the second inequality, note that
\mathbb{E}[(\bm{\Theta}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}-\bm{\Sigma})\bm{\Theta}_{k})^{2}]=\var(\bm{\Theta}_{j}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\Theta}_{k})
. Below we calculate the expression of the general form
\mathrm{Var}\left({\mathbf{u}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}\mathbf{v}}\right)
. Specifically, we apply Isserlis’ theorem
[
33
]
to deal with the moments of Gaussian random variables. For any deterministic vectors
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, Isserlis’ theorem says

\displaystyle\var(\mathbf{u}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}\mathbf{v})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{u}^{\top}\bm{X})^{2}(\mathbf{v}^{\top}\bm{X})^{2}]-(\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{u}^{\top}\bm{X}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\bm{X}])^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{u}^{\top}\bm{X})^{2}]\mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{v}^{\top}\bm{X})^{2}]+(\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{u}^{\top}\bm{X}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\bm{X}])^{2}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{u}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{u}^{\top})(\mathbf{v}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{v}^{\top})+(\mathbf{u}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{v}^{\top})^{2}.

Therefore, we obtain the following,

\mathbb{E}[(\bm{\Theta}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}-\bm{\Sigma})\bm{\Theta}_{k})^{2}]=(\bm{\Theta}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Theta}_{j}^{\top})(\bm{\Theta}_{k}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Theta}_{k}^{\top})+(\bm{\Theta}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Theta}_{k}^{\top})^{2}=\bm{\Theta}_{jj}\bm{\Theta}_{kk}+\bm{\Theta}_{jk}^{2}\geq 1/r_{0}^{2},

where the last inequality holds since
\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Theta})\geq 1/r_{0}
when
\bm{\Theta}\in\mathcal{U}(M,s,r_{0})
.
∎

Now we are ready to prove Lemma
A.1
. Note the proof of this lemma follows a similar idea as the one used in Proposition 3.1 of
[
64
]
. Since Lemma
A.1
involves
the standardized version of the one-step estimator in
[
64
]
, we still present the detailed proof for completeness.

To approximate

T_{E}:=\max_{(j,k)\in E}\sqrt{n}\left|(\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jk}/\sqrt{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jj}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{kk}}-{\bm{\Theta}_{jk}}/{\sqrt{\bm{\Theta}_{jj}\bm{\Theta}_{jk}}})\right|,

(D.12)

by the multiplier bootstrap process

T_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}:=\max_{(j,k)\in E}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n~\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k})\xi_{i}\bigg|,

(D.13)

we define two intermediate processes

\displaystyle\breve{T}_{E}

\displaystyle:=\max_{(j,k)\in E}\bigg|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n~{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k})\bigg|,

(D.14)

\displaystyle\breve{T}_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle:=\max_{(j,k)\in E}\bigg|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n~\bm{\Theta}_{jj}\bm{\Theta}_{kk}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k})\xi_{i}\bigg|.

(D.15)

The strategy of proving this lemma is to verify the three conditions in Corollary 3.1 of
[
17
]
:

(a)

\min_{j,k}\mathbb{E}[({\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}))^{2}]>c
and
\max_{j,k\in[d]}||\bm{\Theta}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k})||_{\psi_{1}}\leq C
for some positive constants
c
and
C
;

(b)

\mathbb{P}(|T_{E}-\breve{T}_{E}|>\zeta_{1})<\zeta_{2}
holds for some
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}>0
;

(c)

And
\mathbb{P}(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(|T_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}-\breve{T}_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}|>\zeta_{1}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})>\zeta_{2})<\zeta_{2}
holds for
\zeta_{1}\sqrt{\log d}+\zeta_{2}=o(1)
.

Notice that in
[
17
]
, the original conditions require the last scaling to be
\zeta_{1}\sqrt{\log d}+\zeta_{2}=o(n^{-c_{1}})
for some
c_{1}
. This is because
they pursue a stronger result that
|\mathbb{P}(T_{E}>\widehat{c}(\alpha,E))-\alpha|=O(n^{-c_{1}})
.
Since we do not emphasize on the polynomial decaying in our result, we only require
\zeta_{1}\sqrt{\log d}+\zeta_{2}=o(1)
.

We start by checking the first condition (a). Lemma
D.5
immediately implies the first part. By the second condition in (
D.11
), we have
\|\bm{X}_{j}\bm{X}_{k}-\mathbb{E}[\bm{X}_{j}\bm{X}_{k}]\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C
. By the definition of the
\psi
-norms, we have

\displaystyle\max_{j,k\in[d]}\|{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k})\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq r_{0}^{2}\|(\bm{X}_{j}\bm{X}_{k}-\mathbb{E}[\bm{X}_{j}\bm{X}_{k}])\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq r_{0}^{2}\sup_{\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}\|\mathbf{v}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}\mathbf{v}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{v}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}\mathbf{v}]\|_{\psi_{1}}=O(1).

Regarding the condition (b), we check by bounding the difference
|T_{E}-\breve{T}_{E}|
. Recall the one-step estimator

\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jk}=\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{jk}-\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\right)}{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{j}},

and plug it into
T_{E}
. Then we have the following bound,

\displaystyle|T_{E}-\breve{T}_{E}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\underset{(j,k)\in E}{\max}\;\sqrt{n}\cdot{\bigg|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jk}}{\sqrt{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jj}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{kk}}}-\frac{\bm{\Theta}_{jk}}{\sqrt{\bm{\Theta}_{jj}\bm{\Theta}_{kk}}}\bigg|}-\max_{(j,k)\in E}\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}{\bigg|{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k})\bigg|}\right|

(D.16)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{I}_{1}\mathrm{I}_{2}}{\underset{(j,k)\in E}{\min}\sqrt{{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}+\frac{\mathrm{I}_{3}}{\underset{(j,k)\in E}{\min}\sqrt{{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jj}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{kk}}}},

where
\mathrm{I}_{1}=\underset{(j,k)\in E}{\max}|\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jj}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{kk}-\bm{\Theta}_{jj}\bm{\Theta}_{kk}|
,
\mathrm{I}_{2}=\underset{(j,k)\in E}{\max}|\sqrt{n}\cdot{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}{\big)}|
and

\mathrm{I}_{3}=\max_{(j,k)\in E}\Big|\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jk}-\bm{\Theta}_{jk})-\sqrt{n}\cdot{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)\Big|.

Note
\mathrm{I}_{1}
can be bounded using Lemma
D.6
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underset{(j,k)\in E}{\max}|\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jj}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{kk}-\bm{\Theta}_{jj}\bm{\Theta}_{kk}|\leq 2M\big\|\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}-{\bm{\Theta}}\big\|_{\max}\leq CM^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}},

(D.17)

with probability
1-1/d^{2}
. As for the term
\mathrm{I}_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{2}=\underset{(j,k)\in E}{\max}\left|\sqrt{n}{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underset{(j,k)\in E}{\max}\sqrt{n}\left|{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\big){\bm{\Theta}}_{k}\right|

(D.18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}M^{2}\big\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}-{\bm{\Sigma}}\big\|_{\max}\leq CM^{2}\sqrt{{\log d}}.

Denote
{\breve{\bm{\Theta}}}_{k}=(\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{k1},\ldots,\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{k(j-1)},\bm{\Theta}_{kj},\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{k(j+1)},\ldots,\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{kd})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
To deal with the term
\mathrm{I}_{3}
,
we first rewrite the following

\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jk}-{\bm{\Theta}}_{jk})=-\sqrt{n}\cdot\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}{\breve{\bm{\Theta}}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)}{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{j}},

(D.19)

then quantify
\sqrt{n}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}{\breve{\bm{\Theta}}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\big)
. Notice that

\sqrt{n}\cdot\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}{\breve{\bm{\Theta}}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\big)=\underbrace{\sqrt{n}\cdot\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\big)}_{\mathrm{II}_{1}}+\underbrace{\sqrt{n}\cdot\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}\big({\breve{\bm{\Theta}}}_{k}-{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}\big)}_{\mathrm{II}_{2}}.

(D.20)

Further we expand
\mathrm{II}_{1}
as

\mathrm{II}_{1}=\underbrace{\sqrt{n}\cdot{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)}_{\mathrm{II}_{11}}+\underbrace{\sqrt{n}\cdot\big(\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}-{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big)\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)}_{\mathrm{II}_{12}},

(D.21)

where
\mathrm{II}_{11}
can be rewritten as
\mathrm{II}_{11}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k})
. We bound
|\mathrm{II}_{12}|
as

\displaystyle|\mathrm{II}_{12}|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\cdot\big(\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}-{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}\big)^{\top}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}-{\bm{\Sigma}}\big){\bm{\Theta}}_{k}\leq\sqrt{n}\cdot\big\|\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}-{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}\big\|_{1}\big\|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}-{\bm{\Sigma}}\big\|_{\max}\|\bm{\Theta}_{k}\|_{1}.

(D.22)

According to Lemma
D.3
, (
D.22
) yields that

\max_{j,k\in[d]}|\mathrm{II}_{12}|\lesssim M^{2}\frac{s\log d}{\sqrt{n}},

(D.23)

with probability
1-1/d^{2}
. By Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
D.3
, we finally obtain the bound on
\mathrm{II}_{2}
:

\max_{j,k\in[d]}|\mathrm{II}_{2}|\leq\sqrt{n}\cdot\max_{j,k\in[d]}\|\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{-j}\|_{\infty}\big\|\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}\big\|_{1}\lesssim M^{2}\frac{s\log d}{\sqrt{n}},

(D.24)

with probability
1-1/d^{2}
.
Therefore, we conclude that by (
D.23
) and (
D.24
), with probability
1-1/d^{2}
, the following holds:

\displaystyle\max_{j,k\in[d]}\sqrt{n}\cdot\Big|\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}{\breve{\bm{\Theta}}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\big)-\bm{\Theta}_{j}^{\top}\Big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}\bm{\Theta}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\Big)\Big|\lesssim M^{2}\frac{s\log d}{\sqrt{n}}.

(D.25)

Lemma
D.3
also implies

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[d]}|\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{j}-1|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j\in[d]}||\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}-\mathbf{e}_{j}||_{\infty}\lesssim M\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}.

(D.26)

Combining (
D.20
), (
D.21
) with (
D.25
) and (
D.26
), for sufficiently large
d,n
, we have, with probability
1-1/d^{2}
, the following holds:

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{(j,k)\in E}\sqrt{n}\Big|\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}{\breve{\bm{\Theta}}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)}{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{j}}-{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{(j,k)\in E}\big(2\sqrt{n}|\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{j}-1|\cdot|{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\big){\bm{\Theta}}_{k}|\big)+2\max_{(j,k)\in E}|\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}{\breve{\bm{\Theta}}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)-{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)|

\displaystyle\leq 2M\sqrt{n}\max_{j\in[d]}|\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{j}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{j}-1|\cdot||\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}||_{\max}+2\max_{j,k\in[d]}(|I_{12}|+|I_{2}|)\lesssim M^{2}\frac{s\log d}{\sqrt{n}},

(D.27)

where the second inequality uses
|x/(1+\delta)-y|\leq 2|y\delta|+2|x-y|
for any
|\delta|<1/2
.
Therefore, combining (
D.16
), (
D.17
),(
D.18
) with (
D.27
) and the fact
\underset{(j,k)\in E}{\min}\sqrt{{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}\geq\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Theta})\geq 1/r_{0}
(as
\bm{\Theta}\in\mathcal{U}(M,s,r_{0})
), we obtain the following:

\mathbb{P}(|T_{E}-\breve{T}_{E}|>\zeta_{1})<\zeta_{2},

(D.28)

where
\zeta_{1}={s\log d}/\sqrt{n}
and
\zeta_{2}=1/d^{2}
; thus the condition (b) is verified. Also note that
\zeta_{1}\sqrt{\log d}+\zeta_{2}={s(\log d)^{3/2}}/\sqrt{n}+1/d^{2}=o(1)
holds under the stated scaling condition of Lemma
A.1
.

Regarding the third condition (c), we bound the difference between
T_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}
and
\breve{T}_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}
as

|T_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}-\breve{T}_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}|\leq\max_{(j,k)\in E}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}}{\sqrt{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}\big(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)-\frac{\bm{\Theta}^{\top}_{j}}{\sqrt{{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}\big(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)\Big)\xi_{i}\Big|

Conditioning on the data
\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, the right hand side of the above inequality is a suprema of a Gaussian process. Therefore, we need to bound the following conditional variance

\displaystyle{\max_{(j,k)\in E}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}}{\sqrt{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}\big(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)-\frac{\bm{\Theta}^{\top}_{j}}{\sqrt{{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}\big(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)\Big]^{2}}

Note the summand (for each
i
) can be bounded by

2\frac{\mathrm{III}_{1}\mathrm{III}_{2}}{\underset{(j,k)\in E}{\min}{{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}+2\frac{\mathrm{III}_{3}}{\underset{(j,k)\in E}{\min}{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}

where
\mathrm{III}_{1},\mathrm{III}_{2}
and
\mathrm{III}_{3}
are defined and bounded as below:

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{1}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\underset{(j,k)\in E}{\max}|\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}-\bm{\Theta}_{jj}\bm{\Theta}_{kk}|^{2}\leq\left(CM^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}\right)^{2}

(D.29)

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{2}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\underset{(j,k)\in E}{\max}[{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)]^{2}=\underset{(j,k)\in E}{\max}[{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}-\bm{\Sigma}\big){\bm{\Theta}}_{k}]^{2}

(D.30)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big[M^{2}\max_{i}\|\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}-\bm{\Sigma}\|_{\max}\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{(j,k)\in E}\Big|\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)-{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)\Big|^{2}

(D.31)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\Big[2M\|\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}-\bm{\Theta}\|_{1}\max_{i}\|\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}-\bm{\Sigma}\|_{\max}\Big]^{2}.

According to Lemma
D.5
, we have with probability
1-1/d^{2}
,

\max_{i}\|\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}-\bm{\Sigma}\|_{\max}\leq C\sqrt{\log(dn)}.

(D.32)

Therefore, the event

\mathcal{E}=\Big\{\max_{(j,k)\in E}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}}{\sqrt{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}\big(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)-\frac{\bm{\Theta}^{\top}_{j}}{\sqrt{{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}\big(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)\Big]^{2}\leq CM^{2}\frac{(s\log(dn))^{2}}{n}\Big\}

satisfies
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c})<1/d^{2}
.
Therefore, by the maximal inequality, under the event
\mathcal{E}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\max_{(j,k)\in E}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}}{\sqrt{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}\big(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)-\frac{\bm{\Theta}^{\top}_{j}}{\sqrt{{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}\big(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)\Big)\xi_{i}}\,|\,{\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle M^{2}\frac{(s\log dn)\sqrt{\log d}}{\sqrt{n}}.

Applying Borell’s inequality, we have with probability
1-1/d^{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\max_{(j,k)\in E}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)}{\sqrt{\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}-\frac{\bm{\Theta}^{\top}_{j}\big(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)}{\sqrt{{\bm{\Theta}}_{jj}{\bm{\Theta}}_{kk}}}\right)\xi_{i}>C\sqrt{\frac{s^{2}\log^{4}dn}{n}}}\,|\,{\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}}\right)\leq 1/d^{2}.

This implies that

\mathbb{P}\left({\mathbb{P}_{\xi}\big(|T^{\mathcal{B}}_{E}-\breve{T}_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}|>\sqrt{(s^{2}\log^{4}dn)/n}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\big)>1/d^{2}}\right)<1/d^{2}.

Now we can verify the condition (c) by showing

\mathbb{P}(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(|T_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}-\breve{T}_{E}^{\mathcal{B}}|>\zeta_{1}\mid\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})>\zeta_{2})<\zeta_{2},

(D.33)

where
\zeta_{1}={s(\log d)^{2}}/\sqrt{n}
,
\zeta_{2}=1/d^{2}
and the condition
\zeta_{1}\sqrt{\log d}+\zeta_{2}={s(\log d)^{3/2}}/\sqrt{n}+1/d^{2}=o(1)
holds under the stated scaling condition of Lemma
A.1
. Therefore, by Corollary 3.1 of
[
17
]
, we have

\lim_{(n,d)\rightarrow\infty}|\mathbb{P}(T_{E}>\widehat{c}(\alpha,E))-\alpha|=0.

(D.34)

And it holds for any edge set
E
, thus the proof is complete.
∎

Lemma D.6
.

Under the same conditions as Lemma
A.1
, we have

\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{j,k\in[d]}|\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jk}-\bm{\Theta}_{jk}|>C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}\Big)<\frac{2}{d^{2}},

(D.35)

for some constant
C_{0}>0
.

By (
D.19
) and (
D.27
), we have with probability
1-1/d^{2}
,

\max_{j,k\in[d]}|\widehat{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jk}-\bm{\Theta}_{jk}+{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)|\leq C_{1}\frac{s\log d}{n}.

By Lemma
D.5
and
\|\bm{\Theta}\|_{2}\leq r_{0}
, we have
\|{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C_{2}r_{0}^{2}
. Applying the maximal inequality (Lemma 2.2.2 in
[
81
]
), we have for some constant
C_{3}>0

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{j,k\in[d]}|{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\big(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbf{e}_{k}\big)|>C_{3}r_{0}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}\Big)

\displaystyle~\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{j,k\in[d]}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}({\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k}-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{\Theta}}^{\top}_{j}\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\Theta}}_{k})]\Big|>C_{3}r_{0}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}\Big)\leq 1/d^{2}.

With
C_{0}=C_{1}+C_{3}
, (
D.35
) is proved. And it is not hard to show a similar result for the standardized one-step estimator also holds, i.e.,

\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{j,k\in[d]}|\widetilde{\bm{\Theta}}^{\text{d}}_{jk}-\bm{\Theta}^{\star}_{jk}|>C_{0}^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}\Big)<\frac{2}{d^{2}}

(D.36)

for some constant
C_{0}^{\prime}>0
.
∎
